# Literature search ledger

All searches below were performed on 2026-11-23. A missing hit is not evidence of priority. No theorem is assigned priority from these searches.

## Root searches

- Searched general terms for closed-stratum tube descent, exit-path comparison, Lurie Higher Algebra Appendix A, Ayala–Francis–Tanaka, Haine and Douteau–Waas. Opened the current primary PDFs for Haine, AFT and Douteau–Waas; downloaded copies are temporary only. Higher Algebra retrieval initially returned HTTP 403; exact primary verification remains open.
- Searched canonical hyperbolic caps, grafting, Mondello boundary-length parametrization, Fırat three-string vertices and Hinich plumbing. Mondello’s exact title and Theorem 5.4 locator corrected; coefficient and range comparisons continue in Paper 4’s audit.
- Searched Roby polynomial laws and divided powers, Grothendieck polynomial categories/mixed coherence, Horel binomial rings, cellwise holomorphic dessin automorphisms. Roby primary PDF retrieved and exact representation statement read; Horel author PDF introduction read. Complete prior-art conclusion for the dessin classification remains a separate gate.
- Searched Bühler Exact Categories and verified the 14 November 2008 ESI version: embedding Theorem A.1, printed p.36; cancellation Proposition 7.5, p.17. The latter is not Proposition 7.6 in this particular version.
- Searched Hexagon for divided powers and Grothendieck categories. Retrieved Hua’s Montpellier compilation version2 and read printed pp.165–168: walking-complex full faithfulness and all-kernel reconstruction are known there; this attribution is now explicit. Additional topology and poset sections were checked by the separate reviewer, with exact locators in topic notes. The supplied stylistic example initially returned a retrieval failure.
- Retrieved the current mathematics-workbenches FORMAT.md directly from the public repository. Its contents are used to prepare the workbench and are not copied into this deliverable.

### PAPER1_LITERATURE.md

# Paper 1 — primary literature and division of credit

Date: 2026-11-23. Reviewer: GPT-6 Astra, register instance; effort setting not exposed. This is a record of current primary-source checks and their limits. It is separate from inherited source check events. The bibliography and `papers/01_tubes/deposit/SOURCES.tsv` give the full citations and URLs. Other authors' PDFs and extracted text are not included in the deliverables.

## Established inputs and exact locators

| Source | Primary locator checked | Use and qualification |
|---|---|---|
| Grothendieck, *Espaces stratifiés*, cote 151 | PDF scans 57–61 and 69; the quoted phrase is on PDF page 59, printed archive footer 58 | Historical problem. The archive-page verification was supplied by the root instance in this run. The register instance independently tested the mathematical statement packet, not the handwriting. |
| Lurie, *Higher Algebra* | Appendix A, Theorem A.9.3, printed p.1457; Corollary A.9.4; Remark A.9.7; Proposition A.9.16; recollement and induction in A.8.14, A.9.11–A.9.18 | Established exodromy, groupoid completion and stalk identification. The paper states paracompactness, local singular shape, conical stratification and the ascending-chain condition. Its finite induction does not pretend to replace the cited transfinite argument. |
| Haine, *On the homotopy theory of stratified spaces* | Theorem 3.2.4, pp.25–26 in the 27 April 2022 version | Established stratified homotopy framework. Primary checking for the opening context was done by the root instance; it is not claimed as a new theorem of this paper. |
| Douteau–Waas, *From homotopy links to stratified homotopy theories* | arXiv:2112.02394v2 | Established comparison of global topological and simplicial stratified homotopy theories. Opening-context reference carried with the root's current primary check. |
| Ayala–Francis–Tanaka, *Local structures on stratified spaces* | Theorem 7.3.8 | Established unzip/resolution theorem for its conically smooth hypotheses. Used with the full geometry, not to infer control from incidence identities alone. |
| Schmitt, *The moduli space of curves* | Proposition 4.14; Sections 6.1 and 7.3, author PDF dated 30 September 2020 | Stable graphs occur and their degeneration order comes from node contraction. This supports the geometric interpretation of the separately proved finite-poset classification. |
| Lurie, *Higher Topos Theory* | Remark 7.1.6.15; Proposition 6.4.5.7; Theorem 4.1.3.1; Definition 6.5.3.2, Lemma 6.5.3.11 and Theorem 6.5.3.12; Example 7.1.6.9 | Shape adjunction, 1-localic extension, homotopy finality and hypercover convergence. Full shape, hypercomplete shape, protruncation and the ordinary pro-homotopy category are kept distinct. |
| Hoyois, *Higher Galois theory* | Proposition 5.1, printed p.13 | Coherent hypercovers model the hypercomplete shape. The qualifier is retained in the paper. |
| Treumann, *Exit paths and constructible stacks* | Theorems 7.13–7.14, arXiv PDF pp.28–29 (v1, August 2007) | Established 2-categorical exit description. The primary statement explicitly treats topologically stratified spaces and constructible stacks. |
| Deopurkar, *Torsors and gerbes* | Theorem 2.7, p.3; Remark 3.1, p.4, 2023 primary PDF | Abelian gerbes correspond to degree-two cohomology; neutral-gerbe equivalences are controlled by torsors. The sphere calculation and the quotient by changes of local neutralization are supplied in the paper. |
| Barwick–Glasman–Haine, *Exodromy* | Lemma 2.7.3 and Theorem 2.7.4 | Established subdivision/décollage comparison for coherent stratified categories. The paper retains every restriction and comparison isomorphism. |
| Quinn, *Homotopically stratified sets* | Definition 3.1 and Proposition 3.2, printed p.469; Corollary 2.4(2)–(3); shrinking-arm example p.452 | Fixed nearly strict deformation for pure subsets. Used to construct actual compatible pro-maps and path homotopies. The general-strata hedgehog is not misrepresented as a manifold-strata example. |
| Grandis, *Finite sets and symmetric simplicial sets* | Section 3, pp.247–248; Theorem 4.2, p.249; Section 5, p.250; equations (3), (10), (12), (13) | Established complete presentation. The paper supplies typed substitutions, the enlarged relation list, and the factorization proof that no extra relation is silently assumed. No minimality claim is made for that list. |
| Dugger–Hollander–Isaksen, *Hypercovers and simplicial presheaves* | Propositions 3.1–3.2, preprint p.7; equation (3.4), p.8; Definition 4.2 | Relative local homotopy fillings and strict fillings for locally fibrant objects. The Ex-infinity argument states the local-fibrancy hypothesis before applying the strict criterion. |
| Barnea–Schlank, *A Projective Model Structure on Pro-Simplicial Sheaves, and the Relative Étale Homotopy Type* | arXiv:1109.5477v9, Definition 8.2, Proposition 8.4 and Remark 8.5(2)–(3), pp.62–65 | Primary PDF retrieved directly after a browser retrieval failed. Proposition 8.4 supplies the hypercover comparison; Remark 8.5(2) extends it to locally connected topoi and (3) distinguishes the further localization used by Artin–Mazur. |
| Barnea–Harpaz–Horel, *Pro-categories in homotopy theory* | Theorem 6.0.4, printed pp.625–626, PDF pages 59–60 | The derived topological realization agrees with the shape of the hypercompleted infinity-topos. The paper explicitly applies the functor to ordinary pro-homotopy types only after forming the comparison in pro-spaces. |
| Curtis, *Simplicial Homotopy Theory* | Definition 3.6 and Theorem 3.7, printed p.131, PDF page 25 | Normalized Moore complex computes simplicial-group homotopy. Used only after the cocycle object's normalized complex has been explicitly calculated. |
| Stacks Project | Theorem 14.24.3, tag 019G, accessed in this run | Dold–Kan equivalence. The concrete characteristic-two cocycle calculation is included independently of this citation. |
| Kerodon | Propositions 3.3.6.7 and 3.3.6.9, tags 00ZH and 00ZK, accessed in this run | Ex-infinity preserves weak homotopy type and is Kan. These are established inputs to the explicit subdivision filling formula. |
| Volpe, *Verdier duality on conically smooth stratified spaces* | arXiv:2206.02728v2; Lemma 2.13, printed p.11; Corollary 2.17 | Proper conically smooth fiberwise-cone formula and exit excision. Proper source bundles and compatible outer collar pushes are checked before application. |
| Albin, *On the Hodge theory of stratified spaces* | Theorem 6.3, pp.48–49 | Established compact smooth resolution/blowdown theorem. The paper translates its collective-face, horizontal/vertical, compact-fiber and radial-collar hypotheses explicitly. |
| Albin–Banagl–Piazza, *Smooth atlas stratified spaces, K-homology orientations, and Gysin maps, Part 1* | arXiv:2505.14952v2, version posted 5 November 2026; Theorems 2.10 and 2.13, pp.24 and 27 | Recent noncompact finite-depth object comparison. The PDF's earlier front-page date is distinguished from the v2 posting date. No unrestricted equivalence of map categories is asserted: liftability conditions remain. |
| Cannon, *The recognition problem: What is a topological manifold?* | Theorem 1.5, printed p.833, primary PDF page 2 | Double suspension of a homology sphere. The paper derives its TOP product/face examples and distinguishes them from PL claims. |
| Looijenga, *Moduli spaces of Riemann surfaces at Tsinghua* | Theorem 8.11, p.47; Theorem 9.13, p.61; Remark 9.14, p.62; Proposition 9.16 and Corollary 9.17, p.65 | Deformation coordinates, isotropy, suitable finite covers and the real oriented boundary blowup. The primary text itself treats the result as an orbifold with corners. |
| Bing, *Concerning hereditarily indecomposable continua* | Example 1, printed pp.43–44, primary PDF pages 2–3 | Existence of a nondegenerate plane pseudoarc. The connected-order counterexample and full Hurewicz lifting argument are given in the paper. |
| Zeeman, *On the dunce hat* | Printed p.347, passage before Theorem (5), primary PDF page 4 (two-page scan) | Mazur handle construction, contractibility, nontrivial boundary group, and original Mazur 1961 p.227 locator. Visually checked against the scanned page. The paper then constructs its five-manifold by boundary spinning, not by an invalid interval stabilization. |
| Milnor, *Lectures on the h-Cobordism Theorem* | Theorem 9.1, p.107; Proposition A, pp.108–109; p.108 is PDF page 113 | Contractible smooth manifold with simply connected boundary is a disk in the stated dimension at least six. The precise dimensional restriction was visually checked; the paper applies it to the six-manifold W times an interval. |
| Ferry, *Strongly regular mappings with compact ANR fibers are Hurewicz fiberings* | Definition and Theorem 1, printed pp.373–374, primary PDF pages 2–3; standing metric conventions on p.374 | Small controlled homotopy equivalences give the actual Hurewicz conclusion. The paper establishes strong regularity and compact ANR fibers before invoking the theorem. The definition was visually checked on p.373. |

## Credit and search limits

Ordinary sheaf descent, recollement, exodromy, the shape adjunction, hypercover convergence, the complete symmetric presentation, stratified resolution, the double-suspension theorem and strong-regularity theorem are established inputs and are credited as such. This paper's contribution is the precise analysis of the supplied tube and incidence proposals, the classification/counterexamples, and their fully written deductions under explicit hypotheses. A literature check supporting a theorem input is not an independent re-proof of that input, and failure to locate a prior occurrence is not a novelty certificate. No priority claim is made for every subsidiary observation.

The statement-only reconstruction by this instance covers only A1.2, A1.3 and A1.5. The rest of the mathematical expansion was checked after reading the supplied proofs. The named current events preserve this distinction. The source's historical readings and old execution logs retain their own provenance; they have not been relabelled as new scans or runs.

## 2026-11-23 — full coherent all-band comparison

The separate all-band worker independently derived the full statement before target-proof access, then checked the alternative finite-cover route. The exact primary inputs are Quinn, *Homotopically Stratified Sets*, Definition3.1 and Proposition3.2, printed469/PDF30, https://webhomes.maths.ed.ac.uk/~v1ranick/papers/quinn5.pdf , SHA256abcd210d31d006a5f6da1d46f3ae10c760f0047259739e5356092043176e211e; Barnea–Harpaz–Horel, *Pro-categories in homotopy theory*, author PDF Section3.2, Definition3.2.7 and Lemmas3.2.12–17, pp26–27, https://geoffroy.horel.org/Pro-categories.pdf , SHA25671d5973b56589175f00dab3860b7081cb0eb6c335d4ce60fdad17b758fa65d7b; Hatcher, *Algebraic Topology*, Proposition4G.2, printed459/PDF39 of98, https://pi.math.cornell.edu/~Hatcher/AT/ATch4.4.pdf , SHA256f83eb722fd8032f8e7484a7fb25187d148e74d71e4f890a67e35d9bf881dfde6; and Kerodon Theorem9.1.5.1, https://kerodon.net/tag/05XR , retrieved HTML SHA256a157e2c68729e0cef841b0f7512e7167dba01c3569ffbd39f6b55581bb3529c2. Root reopened the latter three exact primary statements at08:41UTC. Hatcher supplies numerable open-cover homotopy descent; Kerodon supplies filtered colimits commuting with finite limits in spaces; BHH supplies the pro-Yoneda formalism. The finite synchronized interchange, band order/refinement arguments and overlap endpoint coherence are given in full in the paper. No priority claim follows from this check. The source texts/scans remain outside deliverables.

Yu, [On some open problems in commutative algebra resolved by Rethlas](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2605.25259), v2, 28 May 2026 | Theorem 3.4 and its full proof, §3.4 pp. 7–8 | Newly found primary source. A semilocal two-branch `F_2` domain gives a failure of the multiplication tensor. The finite-difference proof was read in full. This is prior related work, not our new counterexample. |

## Search record and novelty limits

The following query families were issued on 2026-11-23. They are recorded as searches, not as exhaustive bibliographies or proof of newness.

| Query or site | Outcome |
|---|---|
| `o-minimal PL definable homeomorphism groups homotopy equivalence parametric Hauptvermutung concordance` | Located Shiota, Mnëv and related definable topology material. No source with the exact full relative higher comparison was identified in these searches. |
| Kocel-Cynk Pawłucki Valette `C^p parametrization` | Located the primary 2019 text and confirmed the real-closed-field scope. |
| Mnëv fragmentation; Igusa generalized Grassmann; Courte Porcelli torus | Primary arXiv originals found and relevant sections opened. |
| King intrinsic stratification | Located publisher metadata and the author's bibliography. Full original text still missing. |
| `"integer-valued polynomials" Tor biring` and Elliott polynomial compositeness | Located Elliott and the 2026 Rethlas paper, which changes the prior-art discussion. |
| `"definable concordance" PL` | No exact competing statement found in returned results; this does not establish novelty. |
| arXiv and ECCC searches for o-minimal concordance and polynomial tensor criteria | Returned mostly broader or unrelated material. ECCC is not a natural primary venue for these topics, but was included in the sweep. |
| [Hexagon](https://hexagonmath.org/) homepage and advanced-search page | Opened. Direct concordance and integer-valued-polynomial query URLs errored in the retrieval service. No comprehensive Hexagon search is claimed. |

At the first draft checkpoint the collar/cone obstruction is presented as a consequence and application of established concordance theory; the torus group calculation is explicitly credited. No result is labelled bibliographically new. The relationship of the compact metric resolution, the finite-join construction, and the universal Tor formulation to all nearby literature remains a task for the continuing audit.

## Original King source retry — 2026-11-23, 07:34–07:37 UTC

Root repeated exact-title and DOI/PII searches with both search engines, checked the complete current author bibliography at https://www.math.umd.edu/~hking/commentary.html and the historical reference list at https://dubouloz.perso.math.cnrs.fr/GdTs/GdTDN/index.html . Neither contains an article-file link for the 1985 paper; the latter points only to the DOI. The ScienceDirect article and PDF endpoint both returned403. Crossref's public DOI metadata was retrieved successfully (3,947 bytes, SHA2565620b0de3415c515b03f89cb53da719782bfe243c61d7b03c9cbde00e3579545), identifying the publisher's text-mining XML/plain endpoints. The publisher PDF API returned403; the two exact metadata-supplied XML/plain links were inaccessible through the web retrieval tool. Metadata and failures are recorded in paper02_king_0735_fetch_retry.json. This was a bounded additional retrieval attempt, not a reading of the original article.

# Additional primary-source comparison for Paper 2

Date: 2026-11-23. Checker: GPT-6 Astra, ChatGPT Pro mode; numeric effort
not exposed. These are actual source readings and comparisons, not a
claim of exhaustive novelty review or independent reproof of the cited
literature. The source PDF and extracted text remain temporary research
inputs and are not included in the deliverable package.

## Michel Hua, Hexagon 2611.00125v2

Landing page: <https://hexagonmath.org/2611.00125v2>. Version 2 is dated
5 November 2026. The landing title is *Grothendieck Montpellier. Énoncés
des lectures modernisées démontrés*. The PDF title is *Énoncés des notes
de travail de Grothendieck démontrés en Lean4, et questions ouvertes*.
The primary PDF has 409 pages. The copy downloaded by root has SHA256
11d22eeee51d4bb73bd2bd899e4f0676d9ad286141b7fcfd8af148941f62208b.
The landing page describes AI-generated text with partial human review;
its formal-proof claims have not been checked by this worker.

The following sections were read in full as text, including their stated
scope limitations:

| Printed pages | Result and relation to our paper |
|---|---|
| 154–160, §34 | The free-module coordinate lemma is proved on p.155. Localization coefficients and a free polynomial module give unique binary tensor cooperations on p.156. Page157 proves a sufficient injectivity condition for β:K0⊗Ω→K0[T], including flat ring epimorphisms. Theorem34.6 on p.160 states the broader ring-epimorphism-plus-free-module claim but explicitly labels it unverified. Our paper now cites the proved overlap and does not treat the unverified statement as a theorem. |
| 341–342, §71, Theorems71.1–71.2 | Coarsest frontier partitions exist under a closure-stable Boolean family, relative density and a descending rare-closed-set condition, with a connected version. This is relevant to the background of our intrinsic partition but lacks our additional singular-locus condition for every closed saturated union. |
| 342–344, §72 | The displayed finite-coordinate proofs of collapsed subsets and pushouts are supplied. No homeomorphism-family or concordance comparison is proved here. |
| 345–348, §73 | The interval example with the mark {0} versus the empty mark shows loss of apex data without strict conic compatibility. Its elementary repair is proved only in that example. The source expressly leaves the general conic reconstruction, deployment equivalence and foliation quotient claims unverified. |
| 369–370, §79 | A seven-element compatibility poset has a six-element hexagon double-polar lattice. This already supplies a non-Boolean example under the stated weak compatibility axioms; Paper7 now cites it. It is a different orthoset from the interval magasin and does not supply the finite conditional-lattice classification. |

Searches within the full primary text for concordance, Hauptvermutung,
Lipschitz and o-minimal did not identify the all-field comparison theorem.
This text search and the sections above do not exclude every potentially
related statement elsewhere in this long source. Root independently owns
the comparison of the walking-complex section with Paper6.

## Homotopy and fragmentation comparisons

* Baro–Otero, *On o-minimal homotopy groups*, arXiv:0810.0365v1:
  Theorem3.1 p.5, Proposition3.2 and proof pp.5–7, Corollary3.3 p.7,
  Corollary3.4 p.8, Theorem4.1 p.9 and Corollaries4.3–4.4 p.10 were
  freshly read. These compare homotopies of maps between finite definable
  targets. Retaining a family of homeomorphisms with its parameter
  projection is an additional condition; this source is not directly a
  theorem about the concordance simplicial groups.
* Shiota, *PL and differential topology in o-minimal structure*,
  arXiv:1002.1508v3: Lemma3.3, printed p.37, and the relative isotopy
  argument on pp.44–46 were reread from the downloaded original. These
  compare finite-polyhedron homotopy and isotopy sets. No all-higher-degree
  fiberwise-homeomorphism comparison was identified in those statements.
* Nariman, *Thurston's fragmentation and c-principles*, Forum of
  Mathematics, Sigma, published25April2023, primary publisher HTML:
  Theorems1.7 and1.10, the §4 definition of the PL simplicial group and
  Lemma4.1 were freshly read. The fragmentation and homology statements
  concern topological/PL groups and section spaces; they do not themselves
  compare definable families with PL families over arbitrary real closed
  fields. URL: <https://www.cambridge.org/core/journals/forum-of-mathematics-sigma/article/thurstons-fragmentation-and-cprinciples/534288ACBDAA4781B2F8AA07D368E556>.

Search families included o-minimal homeomorphism homotopy equivalence,
parametric Hauptvermutung, finite Lipschitz metric resolution, and
definable metric-space parametrization. Walsberg's thesis and metric-space
papers appeared as candidates; the thesis repository PDF was blocked in
this retrieval attempt, so no theorem from it is recorded as inspected.
Topological definable realization alone would not give the quantitative
Lipschitz interface required here.

none is being represented as the original proof. Additional exact-title

conical CS hypotheses. Our introduction has been adjusted accordingly.
This is a verified later description, not a resolution of the missing
King original-source gate.

## Search actions and their limits

The following query families were actually submitted to public web search, using a stronger engine where primary-source coverage or recent versions required it. Source openings, theorem finds, and local page readings are recorded above. Search results were not used as substitutes for an unseen proof.

1. Grothendieck strong transcendence / Hodge splitting / conjugate periods; Bost–Charles Conjecture 2.12; André Theorem 0.4.
2. Recent arXiv and Hexagon records containing strong transcendence, Hodge periods, or Montpellier; current results included Bakker–Tsimerman, Ayoub, and Hua.
3. Howe ordinary Hodge–Tate Remark 1.3; Ogus equation 1.2.4.5; Yoshinaga periods Theorem 18; Totaro Hodge structures with gaps; Milne CM Theorem 3.13 and errata.
4. Period torsor, complex conjugation and dimension; Brown single-valued periods / Hodge splitting / real Frobenius; Brown–Dupont canonical comparison. This found an important antecedent and led to explicit attribution in the paper.
5. Noncomputable Hodge structure and geometry; exact queries about strong transcendence in French and English. No exact published match to the assembled v-table was identified in the inspected results. This bounded search does not prove novelty.
6. Bhowmik–Pathak Theorem 1.11 and publication record; both accessible preprints were fetched and the specialization step compared.
7. Schneider 1937 elliptic integrals and algebraic period ratio; retrieved original scan through the Göttingen URL after a search-result URL failed.

## Classification of the selected results

| Paper claim | Classification after this review | What is not claimed |
|---|---|---|
| Joint-conjugate dimension table | Explicit algebraic formulation and proof assembled here; standard Tannakian, reductive and orbit ingredients | No priority claim; no numerical period conjecture proof |
| Real-model counterexample | Application of rational real Frobenius, with the Brown–Dupont antecedent credited | No claim that transported real Frobenius is new |
| Nonreal conjugate-isogenous elliptic example | Explicit worked example with classical isogeny and Schneider inputs | No new elliptic transcendence theorem |
| Ordinary Hodge–Tate counterexample | Application of the ordinary splitting already in Howe | No newly discovered p-divisible-group splitting theorem |
| Restricted Lie identity | Coordinate derivation from Cartier; exact all-prime proof supplied | Finite checks do not establish universal identities alone |
| Noncomputable Hodge pair | Explicit synthesis of rigidity and period computability | No characterization of the geometric image |
| Betti path and profinite shear examples | Explicit constructions with proofs; standard continuation and monodromy inputs | No classification of enriched integral realizations |
| CM ideal-class obstruction | Classical ideal arithmetic plus CM reciprocity, applied to labelled comparisons | No new CM reciprocity theorem |
| Bhowmik–Pathak specialization | Counterexample to the two consulted preprint statements; sufficient repair separately proved | No assertion about inaccessible published wording; no message sent |

No new transcendence theorem or priority claim is inferred.


### PAPER5_LITERATURE.md

# Paper 5 literature and primary-source audit

Search and inspection date: 2026-11-23 UTC. Search engines 1 and 2 were both used. Searches establish a documented search boundary, not an exhaustive priority theorem. Only primary papers, author/institutional copies and publisher/archive records support the manuscript's mathematical attributions.

## Exact mathematical sources

**Sturmfels and Ziegler, Extension spaces of oriented matroids**, Discrete & Computational Geometry 10 (1993), 23–45. DOI 10.1007/BF02573961. The publisher PDF was downloaded, hashed and read. Theorem 1.2 is on printed page 25. Lemma 3.2 (Babson's fiber criterion) is on page 29. The oriented-matroid-program graph is defined on pages 30–31; Proposition 3.9, page 33, characterizes Euclidean programs by absence of very strong components. Lemma 4.1 and the contraction comparison start on page 37; Proposition 4.2 and its point-fiber/upper-slice verification occupy pages 37–38. Corollary 4.5, page 40, supplies rank at most three. Printed pages 29,33,37,38,40 were visually inspected. The paper explicitly retains homotopy equivalence, not homeomorphism, for the extension complex.

**Guy David Bailey, Tilings of zonotopes: Discriminantal arrangements, oriented matroids, and enumeration**, University of Minnesota PhD thesis, March 1997. The institutional copy's filename contains Reiner, but its author is Bailey; Victor Reiner was the adviser. Theorem 4.1, printed pages 33–34 (PDF pages 40–41), states that an antisymmetric cocircuit signature is a localization precisely when every rank-two contraction restriction is one. Both pages were visually read. Bailey credits Michel Las Vergnas. The original Las Vergnas proceedings article is identified bibliographically but was not inspected, so no theorem number from the original is invented. The paper cites the exact inspected formulation.

**Gaku Liu, A counterexample to the extension space conjecture for realizable oriented matroids**, arXiv:1606.05033v2, 2016. The primary abstract was opened. It concerns a realizable uniform oriented matroid of high rank and corank three with disconnected extension space. It is cited only as a contrast; it does not weaken the rank-three theorem used here. No proof from this paper is used.

**Michel Hua, A bigon-free dichotomy for pseudoline arrangements, after a note of Grothendieck**, Hexagon 2611.00188v1, 7 November 2026. The primary abstract, references and provenance were opened. The result concerns absence of bigonal old pseudolines and forbidden configurations of the old arrangement; it does not state the extension-surface genus formula. Its source is Grothendieck folder 80; the paper under preparation uses cote154. Hua's record explicitly describes AI generation and human checks of that different paper. None of its check status is transferred to this paper.

The Richter-Gebert/Ziegler oriented-matroid handbook author copy was also retrieved as background. It is not substituted for the two precise results above and is not included in the bibliography merely to lengthen it.

## Manuscript source

Official archive index: https://grothendieck.umontpellier.fr/archives-grothendieck/ . Cote154 is served as https://grothendieck.umontpellier.fr/154.pdf . The archive index was opened and its document link verified. HTTP metadata gives 173374442 bytes; the PDF tree gives 176 PDF pages. Because this is a large image scan, bounded byte-range extraction is being used to inspect selected pages while a full download proceeds. All scan data remains temporary and is excluded from every deliverable.

The introductory quotation and the distinction between PDF page, archive label and handwritten footer are a separate gate. Until a direct visual reading is recorded, inherited references such as 154/005, 154/007 and 154/120 are not treated as current verified scan references.

## Query log and what it changed

Queries included the following exact phrases or close combinations:

- Sturmfels Ziegler extension spaces oriented matroids 1993 PDF.
- Grothendieck 154 positions relatives pseudo-droites surface Euler.
- "Grothendieck" "relative-position surface".
- "pseudoline" "extension" "genus" "Sturmfels".
- "rank three" "extension" "F(-1)" matroid.
- Grothendieck relative positions surface pseudoline.
- oriented matroid extension Euler characteristic cubes.
- extension space counting localizations.
- Las Vergnas rank two localization theorem.
- site:arxiv.org pseudoline surface extension counting.
- site:hexagonmath.org Grothendieck relative positions.
- site:eccc.weizmann.ac.il pseudoline matroid extension.

The ordinary searches led to the exact Sturmfels–Ziegler article and the Bailey statement. Stronger searches independently recovered the extension-space literature and Hua's nearby manuscript work. Additional results concerning double pseudolines, signotope extendability and counting arrangements concern different objects; they were not cited as support for this surface theorem. No inspected result stated the crosscap correction 4*sum(k*c_(2k+1)) for the reflection surface with exceptional sweeps, nor the pair of six-line genera 23/47. This is a limited negative search result. The mathematical paper does not turn it into a claim of absolute priority.

## Literature classification

| Claim | Classification justified here |
|---|---|
| Rank-three extension space has homotopy type S2 | Known: Sturmfels–Ziegler |
| Equivariant contraction comparison and needed fiber criterion | Known: Sturmfels–Ziegler; quotient argument supplied in this paper |
| Rank-two localization test | Known: Las Vergnas, exact inspected statement in Bailey |
| Cubical Euler count and fixed-vertex derivative count | Derived here from those known theorems; no identical formula located in the inspected sources |
| Reflection-surface local circuits and genus correction | Full proof included; no identical theorem located in the inspected search |
| Six-line 23/47 examples | Full fifteen-case proof and fresh exact computations; no identical pair located in the inspected search |
| Perfect-matching sign criterion | New derivation in this run from known localization criterion and elementary signed-graph consistency |

Hashes and exact uses are recorded in the paper's deposit/SOURCES.tsv. Downloaded papers, scans and extracted text are not part of the deliverable.

## Completed scan check

Selected original PDF pages5,7,39,120 were extracted without changing their content. The scan has 176 PDF pages, and archive footers4,6,38,119 respectively. PDF7 also has handwritten5, so handwritten numbering must not be inferred from the footer. The exact quotation on PDF120 was visually checked at180 dpi: “les sommets sont d’ordre 4”. Its handwritten page119 and archive footer119 agree. The adjacent equations 2s1=4s0 and chi=s2-s0 support the historical cell-count motivation. This corrects the earlier inherited shorthand154/120; it was a PDF locator, not an archive-footer locator. No extracted scan is delivered.


## 2026-11-23 — straight-line parameter compactification

Queries: “real blowup real projective plane connected sum real locus surface blowup Mobius band” and “oriented matroid extension space fixed realization straight extensions rank three real blow up”. The author manuscript of Frédéric Mangolte, *Real Algebraic Varieties*, translated by Catriona Maclean, 11 December 2019, Corollary F.3.2, printed p.465 / PDF p.475, identifies a real surface blowup with connected sum with the real projective plane and gives the Möbius-band proof. Statement and proof read. URL: https://www.i2m.univ-amu.fr/perso/frederic.mangolte/VAR-ENGLISH-2019-12-11.pdf . PDF SHA-256: f5f464826c5cdecc80f3f3c3bde8e74c3f044f2bf08cc568a8c7158765757e90 (5,291,274 bytes, 528 pages). The manuscript is cited, not copied into deliverables. The paper now uses this standard construction for six specified dual-plane points, and separately proves its cell counts and the failure of single-vertex closure for ordinary straight positions. No claim of priority is made.


### paper05_root_primary_comparison.md

# Primary input and post-freeze comparison for the surface

2026-11-23, 03:15 UTC. The blind note SHA506a78f0e2eeeb2e82495c8fbd7c527435bdf47f6d3fcf72475d3b287619c444 was frozen at03:13:24 UTC. Root then read the entire local surface proof and construction in the current Paper5, and the earlier reviewer’s full after-source definition audit.

The first-fan argument and maximum-nu corner classification agree with the independently reconstructed route. The source uses oriented-matroid program acyclicity; the independent note instead gives the relative disk-strip adaptation of Felsner’s Sweeping Lemma6.1 (printed pp.89–90), whose complete primary proof was read. Root’s connectivity proof sweeps a position toward a fixed old line; the source instead uses the connected cubical extension complex. Both routes require the explicit zero-preserving corridor definition.

One detail is more explicit in the target than in the frozen note: a new exceptional corridor cannot enter the tracked gap after resolving one or both of its two distinct zeros. After one resolution, its old apex blocks that side of the potential corridor. After both, a wall in that gap would have to contain both bounding old apices, which is impossible because their incident line sets are disjoint. This was checked after target access and is recorded as targeted supplementation, not as a retroactive addition to the blind note.

The source’s literal empty-fan wording wrongly excluded its own intermediate strands at a multiple vertex. Its corridor definition also lacked the required exclusion of off-wall zero packets, and it described the desired exceptional circuit as prescribed. These definitions are being corrected, with endpoint names stipulated and four-cycle conclusions proved. The old claim that disjoint original intervals alone prove polygon embeddedness is being removed; the four-distinct-face corner classification is the valid argument.

The independent constrained-pencil proof of the derivative identity uses cubical cuts of a finite strand order. The paper uses the restriction of the exact Sturmfels–Ziegler contraction map and unchanged Babson fibers. The latter must be assessed on its exact primary hypotheses; an arbitrary restriction of a homotopy equivalence would not suffice. The independent cut identification is currently receiving a second critical read. No blanket acceptance or novelty conclusion is made from agreement of routes alone.

## Searches and sources

Searched public primary sources for relative sweeps, wiring diagrams, rank-three extension spaces, and multiple pseudoline intersections. Opened the author-hosted Felsner–Goodman chapter and Felsner’s book. Read the latter’s full marked-arrangement sweep proof and the subsequent topological-order interpretation, printed pp.87–92. Read Sturmfels–Ziegler’s introduction and Theorem1.2, printed pp.23–25. The theorem states the homotopy type of S² for rank-three extension posets; it does not declare that complex to be a two-dimensional manifold.

### paper04_root_algebra_audit.md

# Root audit: Picard double fibre and the explicit Nori curve

Date: 2026-11-23. Worker: GPT-6 Astra, ChatGPT Pro mode; effort not exposed. This is a targeted primary-source and proof audit. The inherited Picard proof had already been read; no blind re-proof is claimed. For Nori the statement and primary Pauly proof were read before the new exposition was written. Known results are credited as such.

## Picard example

Read Raynaud's *Spécialisation du foncteur de Picard*, Publ. Math. IHES 38 (1970), 27–76, Definition 6.1.4 p.48 and Theorem 8.2.1 pp.66–67. The theorem's (N)* concerns absence of embedded special-fibre components, normality of the **total space** at generic special-fibre points, and f_*O=O. It does not require the double special fibre to be normal. For a locally factorial total space and perfect residue field, δ=d′ and all eight conditions in 8.2.1 are equivalent. Thus δ=2 rules out both scheme representability and separation of Pic0.

The quotient construction was checked against Stacks Lemma 66.14.2 in Section 66.14 (tag 07S5), and algebraic-space representability against Proposition 99.11.8 (0D2C). The action is free because translation by the nonzero two-torsion point has no fixed point. The quotient map is finite étale. Regularity and proper flat finite presentation descend. The generic curve becomes the elliptic curve after C((s))/C((t)). The divisor equality is div(t)=2C.

Universal H0 was proved directly on every R-algebra A, including nonreduced A: H0(Y_A)=A⊕As, the involution is +1 on A and −1 on As, and 2 is a unit. Torsor descent gives invariants A. This verifies the actual universal hypothesis of the Picard representability theorem.

## Nori example

Read all five pages of Pauly's author version, with Proposition 2.2 on p.2 and Proposition 4.1/proof on pp.2–4. Read Laszlo–Pauly Section 2.3, Lemma 2.1, author PDF p.3, for the normal form and abc≠0 ordinary smooth locus. Parameters a=b=1,c=alpha with alpha²+alpha+1=0 satisfy Pauly's relation and have abc≠0. The equation becomes z²+z=x+alpha/x+1/(x+1) after the stated shift.

Pauly's proof exhibits an open part of a projective line in the stable coarse moduli locus, with an étale cover carrying Frobenius-four-trivial bundles. Taking its generic point over K=algebraic closure of k(t) makes the moduli point transcendental, so the bundle cannot descend to k. Proposition 2.2 contradicts an isomorphism of the Nori groups. This is a concrete specialization of Pauly's known counterexample. The exposition specifies K explicitly rather than relying on the author-version Corollary 4.2's abbreviated quantifier; no correction to a published version is asserted without checking that version.

## Computations

New program: programs/surgery_algebra_certificates.py. Different method: matrix representation of F4, full polynomial-quotient verification of the Artin–Schreier transformation, derivation from the elliptic chord law, and an explicit Bezout identity for the fixed-point ideal. All mathematical assertions passed on the first execution; a JSON serialization issue for SymPy integers was then fixed. Final output: checks/paper4/root_algebra.json, PASS.

Original certificates were inspected and executed unchanged in an isolated temporary directory: A567_Nori_curve.py and B_finite_checks.py. Both exited zero. Fresh logs and exact source hashes are in checks/paper4/root_original_reruns.json. The second program checks additional unrelated items too; its success is not being counted as independent proof of those geometric inputs.

### paper06_literature.md

# Paper 6 literature and primary-source audit

Date: 2026-11-23. These are bibliography and priority-assessment records, not assertions that all external proofs have been independently re-proved. Downloaded primary files remain temporary and are excluded from every delivery.

## Searches and findings

Searches included “divided power polynomial category Grothendieck composition”, “cellwise dessins automorphisms holomorphic”, “Kleisli coKleisli Riehl category theory context”, “Grothendieck tensor divided-power category axioms”, and site-restricted Hexagon searches for cote 137. Primary journal, author and arXiv copies were then opened. The later exact searches in paper06_final_primary_comparison.md locate the known Mulase–Penkava combinatorial classification; no priority claim is made.

| Source | Exact material read | Use and outcome |
|---|---|---|
| Roby, *Lois polynômes et lois formelles en théorie des modules* (1963) | Theorem IV.1, printed p.266, PDF p.55 | Polynomial-law representability is known and credited. |
| Krause, *Koszul, Ringel, and Serre duality for strict polynomial functors*, arXiv:1203.0311v4 | Section 2, printed/PDF pp.3–5, especially the divided-power category and Remark 2.2 | The fixed-degree Hom category and its linear representations are known; they are different from degree-multiplying polynomial-law arrows. |
| Bühler, *Exact Categories*, ESI preprint 2063, 14 November 2008 | Embedding Theorem A.1, p.36; cancellation Proposition 7.5, p.17 | These are the exact external inputs to the admissible extension argument. Proposition numbering is version-specific. |
| Horel, *Binomial rings and homotopy theory*, author version | Introduction pp.1–3 | Derived homotopy recovery has established stronger models; the paper claims only the explicit information recovered from its test functor. |
| Riehl, *Category Theory in Context*, current author PDF | Definition 5.2.10, printed p.194 / PDF p.214 | The co-Kleisli associativity calculation is classical. The finite-bound and mixed-normalization application is written out here. Older secondary locators point to different numbering. |
| Chambert-Loir–de Frutos-Fernández, CPP2026 | Abstract and introduction, printed p.75 | Recent formalization concerns foundational polynomial laws and universal divided-power algebra; no formal verification of this manuscript is claimed. |
| Schneps, *Dessins d’enfants on the Riemann sphere* (1994) | Section IV, Example 3, author PDF pp.27–28 | The sextic-tree cubic is known and explicitly credited. |

The Hexagon version-2 exposition by Michel Hua, dated 5 November 2026, gives the walking-complex full-faithfulness and all-kernels equivalence on pp.165–166. Its homogeneous graded construction is on pp.167–168; mixed-degree composition is expressly outside that construction. Paper 6 now credits these known cases and presents its exact-kernel criterion as a refinement. The comparison does not claim to have replayed the exposition’s Lean code.

## Present theorem-level assessment

- Bare-axiom unit independence and the mixed-coherence counterexample: explicit manuscript-specific countermodels, independently reconstructed here; the searched primary sources do not supply these models. The final bounded primary comparison is recorded in paper06_final_primary_comparison.md; no priority claim is made.
- Finite coherence criterion: classical co-Kleisli mechanism; the finite-bound and prescribed mixed-component formulation is the contribution being explained, not the general category construction.
- Walking-complex theorem: full faithfulness and the all-kernels equivalence are known; the explicit necessary-and-sufficient collection of kernels isolates the refinement for a target lacking arbitrary kernels.
- Full natural divided-power reconstruction: an explicit comparison with the cosimplicial ring of functions, not a new replacement for established derived homotopy-recovery theorems.
- Cellwise holomorphic groups: the main classification has two independent complete arguments and finite certificates; its known Mulase–Penkava combinatorial core and holomorphic/low-valence extension are now separated in the final primary comparison.

## Retrieval identities

Exact URLs, byte lengths and SHA-256 identities are consolidated in SOURCE_REFERENCES.tsv and each provisional workbench SOURCES.tsv. The source filenames in temporary storage are not delivered. The author PDFs for Roby, Bühler, Krause, Horel, Riehl, Chambert-Loir–de Frutos-Fernández and Schneps were retrieved successfully. The Hexagon PDF initially returned HTTP 403 to the default client and was subsequently retrieved from the same public URL with ordinary browser request headers; its SHA-256 is 11d22eeee51d4bb73bd2bd899e4f0676d9ad286141b7fcfd8af148941f62208b, 3,234,186 bytes.


### paper05_E4_E6_targeted_comparison.md

# Marked surgery and cellular comparison: after-source review

Date: 2026-11-23. Reviewer: GPT-6 Astra, root instance, ChatGPT Pro mode. This is a targeted proof check after a frozen independent attempt, not a complete independent re-proof. The pre-proof note is `paper05_E4_E6_blind_obstruction.md`, SHA256 `35a522fa9e76c694bd0c27e8b6bd4475242e60229df28c20b4598c9b5de4f6c8`. It correctly identified the missing generic implication but did not derive the source's marked-sector construction. The generic counterexample in that note is not a counterexample to the oriented-matroid theorem.

## E4: the marked puncture comparison

I read the complete original E4 proof after freezing that attempt. I also read the exact primary input: Sturmfels--Ziegler, *Extension spaces of oriented matroids*, Lemma 3.2, printed page 29; Lemma 4.1 and Proposition 4.2 with its complete proof, printed pages 37--38. The journal PDF was retrieved from `https://link.springer.com/content/pdf/10.1007/BF02573961.pdf`, 1,208,264 bytes, SHA256 `8c2a94707acb7ae4cc44fa427d22221c31ea4add988ae57e4e6ed30826321560`. The primary file and extraction remain temporary.

Babson's two fiber conditions remain true on the full inverse image of every target subposet: both the point fiber and its intersection with an upper principal filter are literally unchanged. This justifies the source's use on closed suspension sectors and on meridians. It does not rely on restricting an arbitrary homotopy equivalence.

The stars at parallel extensions are actual open disks because those elements are minimal and their strict upper links are the rank-two circle. At a non-tip marked point, evaluation at the shared old vertex divides the link into two semicircles, with two zero directions; hence each sector sees a half-disk and the meridian sees an open interval. At the two tips the same cone description gives endpoint intervals and disk sectors. These are local statements about the entire complex, not only about a chosen subcomplex.

Every other marked point on a meridian separates the two tips. An old vertex w on its line h, different from g intersection h, exists by the non-pencil assumption. The evaluation at w has the marked h-extension as its only zero in that meridian: an ordinary line cannot meet h both at w and at g intersection h. Comparable sign vectors cannot take both positive and negative values at w; consequently affine extension of evaluation over each simplex has no additional zeros. The two tips have opposite values. This proves separation.

Cutting a regular interval point from a contractible finite CW complex gives two acyclic components, by relative homology, and each has trivial fundamental group, by the free-product van Kampen decomposition. Hurewicz and Whitehead make both contractible. Repeated cuts retain this property and give the linear separating order of the marked points between the tips. Removing a boundary half-disk from a contractible sector preserves its homotopy type: the removed closed half-disk strongly deformation retracts onto its frontier arc, fixing that arc, and the deformation extends by the identity. Several cuts have disjoint neighborhoods.

The punctured source and an explicitly punctured suspension sphere therefore have the same finite diagram of contractible sectors and disjoint contractible intersection components. Prescribed circle homeomorphisms extend first over the meridian intervals and then over sector disks. The finite homotopy-pushout gluing theorem applies because all inclusions are subpolyhedron cofibrations. Antipodal pairs of sectors and meridians are distinct; making one choice and transporting it gives an equivariant map with the prescribed boundary markings. It descends to a homotopy equivalence: the lifted map induces isomorphisms on all homotopy groups, and the quotient map is the identity on the two-element deck quotient. The fundamental-group short exact sequences and higher covering isomorphisms, followed by Whitehead, prove the assertion. Attaching Möbius bands preserves this pair equivalence.

Outcome: the marked comparison and its n+1-crosscap homotopy conclusion are accepted in this targeted audit. The source contains the additional structure absent from the invalid generic puncturing inference. No proof gap remains in this step after the stated primary input and finite CW facts.

## E6: selected ordinary squares and exceptional bands

I read the complete original E6 proof after the frozen attempt. In each ordinary cube, the coordinate-midplane subdivision has face centers as vertices. A small square is a two-coordinate interval of the face order; it is the union of the two order-complex triangles in its incidence diamond. The ordinary four-corner theorem identifies the selected squares exactly by adjacency of the two specified resolution ports in the complete boundary cycle. Distinct such diamonds are distinct small cells and meet along common faces. This is a literal cell inclusion, not an argument from Euler characteristic.

Replacing a whole parallel polygon by a Möbius band gives the same space as removing a small central disk and inserting a band: the remaining polygonal annulus is a collar. If C_i is the ordinary boundary cycle of that polygon, the quotient `(C_i x [-1,1])/((z,t)~(a_i z,-t))` is the band, with boundary parametrized by z at t=1. Its fiber edges pair opposite vertices, and its rectangles pair opposite edges. There are 2 n_i fiber edges and 2 n_i rectangles, with four distinct vertices in each rectangle because n_i >= 2. Their boundary labels agree exactly with the exceptional flag cycles. The ordinary selected squares and all these bands therefore give the entire dual surface as an embedded subcomplex.

It includes the complete one-skeleton. Each omitted two-cell adds its attaching relation to the fundamental group; all higher cells leave it unchanged. Thus the induced homomorphism is surjective and its kernel is the normal closure of the unselected diamond loops. Counting ordinary open k-cells by their unique containing original open j-cube gives `N_k=2^k sum_{j>=k} binomial(j,k)c_j = 2^k F^(k)(1)/k!`. In particular there are `2 F''(1)-F'(1)+b` omitted squares. This count agrees with the full one-skeleton and with the separate band counts.

The formula beta_i([z,t])=t h_i(z), with h_i antipodal-equivariant, is well-defined and collapses exactly the band core. The n cores are disjoint, so van Kampen gives the stated normal-closure kernel for blow-down. If all cubes have dimension at most two, every two-cube has its four small squares selected: its four resolution ports alternate, and a near-wall position has at most one zero, so no exceptional port intervenes. The inclusion is then equality. If any position has at least three zeros, partial resolution produces a three-zero position, the odd correction in the surface Euler formula is strictly positive, and the inclusion cannot be a homotopy equivalence to the n+1-crosscap homotopy type.

Outcome: the precise cellular replacement is accepted after-source. The refuted statement about selecting only some stable resolutions remains refuted: all 2^nu stable resolutions exist. The original misleading claim and its valid replacement must remain distinct in the register.


### paper05_polygon_targeted_comparison.md

# Polygon and straight-position comparison after frozen derivations

Date: 2026-11-23. Reviewer: GPT-6 Astra, root instance, ChatGPT Pro mode; effort not exposed. The independent P1--P4 and typed-arc derivations were frozen in `paper05_polygon_blind_derivations.md`, SHA256 `80800444e0927a389614bd7007e7b1f89d8576e6b99964087d587bc84d3c71db`. The new complete P5 witness was frozen at 03:38:58 UTC in `paper05_polygon_counterexample_blind_followup.md`, SHA256 `ff76bb6118723fdd267ad870af862e8b198bf10f943995dc7721fdaed92084ae`. The original P1--P6 and T4 proofs were opened only after those freezes.

## Proof comparison

The complete disk criterion agrees with the independent argument. Pair alternation forces consecutive disjointness classes and reverses their internal orders; the boundary collapse gives a disk, and opposite packet identification gives RP2 with the required transverse old intersections. The one-class exception is necessary. The source's polar-coordinate quotient description supplies an explicit realization of the boundary collapse used independently.

The word determines q and hence every entry of the extension chirotope. The local packet formula agrees exactly with the frozen normalized-horizontal calculation. Its dependence on a third line cancels as a square of a nonzero transverse determinant. The word expansion is therefore a total order, independent of that line. The full image criterion uses both the matroid-support axiom and sign Grassmann--Plücker relations, not the latter alone. I freshly read the exact primary formulation in Richter-Gebert--Ziegler, author preprint *Oriented Matroids*, Section 7.2.3, printed page 10, and its projective representation/equivalence discussion, Section 7.1.4, pages 6--7. PDF `https://science-to-touch.com/Articles/jrg/21_OrientedMatroids.pdf`, 344,894 bytes, SHA256 `156ea58f0be1b2a2a561d2f8840567d519622c85d4e9d0344874ecfdf8a5bf9b`. The manuscript includes the antipodal cellwise matching argument carrying a deletion representation to the fixed old arrangement.

The independently searched first six-line witness is exactly the source witness, up to cyclic permutation of coordinate columns in the moment-curve input. The order of signs tried differed, and the fresh program completed all 3,840 candidates instead of stopping at the first witness. That alone is a new implementation, not a different mathematical validity test. Therefore a second fresh program was added: it uses the independently implemented rank-two localization patterns on each old line, with no GP or signed basis-exchange validity decision. This different method gives 148 valid oriented stable words and 48 invalid words passing three local triangles; the witness fails the restrictions at old lines 5 and 6. The displayed three negative GP terms and all twelve sign comparisons were also worked by hand in the frozen note.

For weighted reconstruction, the initial independent note reconstructed vertices, arrows and rotations but did not fully derive the numerical corridor weights. After source access I checked the whole argument: a complete m_v-packet fan consumes m_v-1 boundary edges, the n-m_v transverse corridor strands create n-m_v+1 edges, and the modified parallel-end weight is m_v-1. Their sum is n. The criterion sigma_parallel <= sigma_N is precisely the rank-two upper link at the parallel position, so with the old vertex fixed the two near-line sides exhaust the possibilities. These weight calculations are targeted checks, not newly blind proofs.

The newcomer review correctly required two graphs to be distinguished. The specialization graph has ordinary and parallel vertices and one-zero-to-parallel arrows. The dual surface graph has only ordinary vertices and also stable exceptional sweeps. The revised text explicitly converts a parallel star to its full rank-two boundary/band data and thus recovers all exceptional sides and endpoint maps. No identification of the two graphs is asserted.

The typed tether/chord proposition is independently proved and agrees with the source's elementary proof. Its interpretation of the handwriting at cote 156-4/032 remains outside this review: direct retrieval of that scan is still in progress. The paper states the complete mathematical endpoint distinction without asserting that unverified archival reading. The source's separate doubtful negation on /045 is not resolved or used.

## Fresh computations and exact reruns

`relative_position_straight_dual.py` recomputed all 15 dual lines and the intersection multiplicities by integer cross products. Since this is the same broad method as the source, `relative_position_dual_rowspaces.py` separately recomputed them by rational Gaussian elimination, intersection row-space closures, and incremental region splitting. Both give 49 vertices, 117 edges, 69 regions, and 229 ordinary straight positions, with ordinary zero counts (69,117,42,1). This proves the fixed-realization count, not an unspecified additional straight-surface construction. The source paragraph's extra phrase about that surface's Euler characteristic is not imported without an exact construction.

All five auxiliary source programs were rerun byte for byte in a temporary copy: `polygons_l3_search.py`, `polygons_l3_verify.py`, `polygons_certificates.py`, `l4_endpoint_certificate.py`, and `counts_straight_dual.py`; all exited zero. The attachment has no original audit JSON for the last program, so its input was the newly regenerated exact six-vector input, and this is explicit in `certificates/paper05_original_auxiliary_reruns.json`. Source hashes and fresh output hashes are recorded there. No source code copy is included in a delivery; outputs and new programs are included.

Outcome: the polygon image, injectivity, packet rule and local-test counterexample have complete independent derivations plus subsequent agreement checks; the full weighted reconstruction has a complete targeted proof check, with its graph types clarified. The exact straight-position counts have two fresh methods and the source rerun. The elementary tether theorem is proved, with its archival attribution gate preserved separately.


### paper06_primary_literature_extension.md

# Paper 6: primary literature extension and exact source scopes

Date: 23 November 2026. The searches and readings below concern the positive polynomial-law model, cochain recovery, cellwise dessins, and exact Kummer images. They supplement `paper06_literature.md` and `paper06_additional_primary_sources.md`. Primary PDFs and text extractions remain outside the deliverables. The companion `paper06_primary_extension_manifest.json` records byte identities of successful downloads and exact attached-source identities; unsuccessful downloads are explicitly distinguished.

## Searches performed

The recorded search terms and retrieval outcomes identify these bounded searches; punctuation and spacing have been normalized in the table. Site-restricted results do not establish that an entire site has been searched. No result is labelled new solely because a query returned no match.

## Exact primary material read and its bearing

**Roby1963.** TheoremIII.3 and the statement/proof of III.4, printed pp262–263, correspond to PDF pp51–52. TheoremIV.1 and its proof, pp266–267, correspond to PDF pp55–56. The PDF p50 context was also read. These establish arbitrary-module representability, base change and the exponential decomposition. The bounded-law category in the paper is explicitly globally finite-degree; Roby's discussion on p267 allows more general locally finite decompositions and is not silently identified with that bounded category.

**Schneps1994.** LemmaI.1 is on author-PDF p2; the clean correspondence TheoremI.5 is on p8. The monodromy definitions on pp5–6 and the pre-clean covering discussion on p8 were read. ExampleIV.3 on pp27–28 contains the known cubic25t^3-6t^2-6t-2. Its p28 was also rendered and visually inspected. Our cubic is exactly eight times this at t=c/2. The new paper credits the cubic and its three-member orbit, and independently proves its normalization; it does not claim that every printed factor in a different normalization is identical.

**Wendl, version3.3, 7May2015.** This PDF has two printed pages on each PDF sheet. Theorem4.2.1 and its immediate discussion on printed pp151–152 are PDF sheet82. The quotient classification and pointed-automorphism corollary on printed pp153–154 are PDF sheet83. The disk/plane uniformization and the rank-one plane quotient are the precise inputs; no two-up offset is concealed.

**Cañas–Hidalgo–Turiel–Viruel2022.** Primary repository PDF, nine pages. Read Introduction pp1–3, Theorems1–2 on p3, and the dessin automorphism definition in Section2.3 p4. Their countable-group realizations concern the usual automorphisms, which may permute cells. This supplies relevant context but neither proves nor contradicts fixed-cell rigidity.

**Horel, arXiv2211.02349v2, 23March2025.** Read the model structure Theorem3.7 p7; the derived-adjunction construction in Section4 p9; Theorem5.4 and its proof p13. The source's numbered remark had shifted: the derived-forgetful caveat is Remark3.11 p8 in this exact version. The paper cites the checked version-specific statements and derives mapping recovery using their adjunction. The primary theorem is credited, not reproved as new.

**Mandell2006.** The complete opening pp213–216 (PDF1–4) was read: Main Theorem, Theorem0.1, Theorem0.2, and the S2/S3 non-fullness example. These distinguish object detection and faithfulness from fullness. No unexplained assertion that E-infinity cochains lose objects is made.

**Toën2020.** Read the Introduction, Theorem4.1 on PDF/printedp11, Corollary5.1 and its proof p14, and Corollaries5.4–5.5 with the continuation on pp16–17. The extra completed homotopy groups of derived integral points are explicitly distinguished from Horel's binomial realization. This is known context, not a new theorem in this paper.

**Milne, Algebraic Number Theory v3.08, 19July2020.** The S-unit theorem5.11 and proof on printedp90 are PDFp92. The preceding ordinary-unit discussion on printed pp88–89 was also read. This is the only finite-generation theorem needed for the lattice saturation step.

**Perucca–Sgobba2019.** Their primary author PDF's indexed text was read at Theorem1 p1, Theorem13 and proof pp6–8, and Theorem15 p7 (Schinzel's radical criterion). Direct repository downloads failed, so there is no invented PDF hash. The degree bound in these statements is unconditional; any GRH hypothesis elsewhere in their density discussion is not transferred to it.

**Schinzel1977 and corrigendum1980.** The primary1977 cached text was located, but Theorem2's displayed formula was corrupted by OCR. A screenshot request did not return inspectable image bytes. The1980 corrigendum was not read. The paper therefore states explicitly where it uses the radical formulation quoted in Perucca–Sgobba, and its own cohomological argument supplies a complete proof of the bounded obstruction needed here. No claim is made to have visually checked the original formula.

**Perucca, Linear relations among radicals, arXiv2511.02498v2, 9November2026.** Read pp1–3 and13–14 in full. Theorem26 gives finite control of the degree/index ratio at all radical levels for a finitely generated subgroup over a number field, with explicit cyclotomic factors. It is stronger context for the all-level behavior; our explicit saturation bound is not advertised as a replacement for that theorem. The journal search result gives online publication8May2026 and issue dateNovember2026; the paper cites the exact arXiv version actually read rather than conflating those dates.

**Fantechi–Ricolfi, Motivic aspects, BLMS2025.** Read primary journal HTML AppendixB and author-PDF pp27–30. TheoremB.1 on p27 credits Rydh's support morphism. The following pages construct polynomial laws and divided powers, with their use in the geometric support map. This is context for why scalar-extension naturality matters; Roby's original theorems remain the algebraic primary references.

## Present literature decisions

The representation theorem, strict-polynomial-functor definition, co-Kleisli mechanism, known all-kernel walking-complex equivalence, Dold–Kan formulas, perfect-complex criteria, Horel/Mandell/Toën recovery results, Schneps's cubic, and general Kummer openness are credited as known. The paper separates its explicit conditions, countermodels, normalizations, and comparisons from those known inputs. The final terminology search and exact primary comparison are now recorded in paper06_final_primary_comparison.md. It identifies the Mulase–Penkava known combinatorial core and gives theorem-by-theorem status decisions without a priority claim. The 2026 Kummer follow-up and2025 geometric application have been incorporated in the relevant sections.

## Direct archive follow-up

At04:53UTC the complete cote138 PDF became available. Root visually read PDF107–112 (archive sheets106–111) and129 (archive128), checking every B2.5 locator and the cellwise uniqueness question. The exact readings, qualifications and byte identity are in paper06_cote138_direct_reading.md. This closes the historical-locator gate; it does not resolve an unrelated literature-priority question.

The first fourteen searches identified small-codimension critical-point papers by Andrica–Funar and Funar–Pintea–Zhang, Meigniez's submersion paper and Ferry's noncompact-fibre paper. None of the returned results identified an exact closed-six-manifold proper Hurewicz example with a nonmanifold fibre. This is a bounded negative search observation, not a completeness or priority claim. The Hexagon queries returned no relevant theorem in the viewed result batches; a returned twin-helices item concerned a different subject and is not cited as relevant context. The arXiv/OpenAI and ECCC queries returned no relevant exact theorem in those batches. No assertion about all AI-laboratory work follows.

The graph-contraction query found related moduli-of-graphs lectures by Caporaso, Tosteson's graph-poset work and contraction-category arXiv:1907.11234. Those primary texts have not yet been read for the conditional-meet claim and remain leads, not settled matches. A 2026 graphical-moduli item appeared with a different deletion–contraction question; its result is not imported.

The later searches located the primary Cacciatori–Pigola paper, Pushkar–Rudyak's h-cobordism theorem, and Farber–Schütz's closed-one-form theorem. These establish concrete comparison statements below. The author pages of Meigniez and Ferry were opened; the former lists the2002 paper, and the latter did not provide the1991 item among its displayed downloads. A MathOverflow answer was used only as a lead to the primary Meigniez/Ferry URLs, not as authority for a technical claim.

## Exact primary comparisons

1. **Andrica–Funar, On smooth maps with finitely many critical points**, JLMS(2)69(2004),783–800. Read opening Theorem1.1 and hypotheses on printed783–784, and reference list799–800(PDF17–18). The displayed small-codimension theorem requires target dimension at least two, so its scope does not decide a six-manifold mapping to a circle. Its h-cobordism discussion points to Pushkar–Rudyak, reference25, which was followed to the original article. No claim about every theorem in the18-page paper is made.
2. **Funar, author manuscript smallv4.pdf** (2019). Read introduction and Theorem1.1, PDF1–2. The stated classification uses target dimension k at least two and m at most2k−1. The6-to1 example is outside these hypotheses. This is a scope comparison, not a reproof of the classification or a claim about every other section.
3. **Funar–Pintea–Zhang, Examples of smooth maps with finitely many critical points in dimensions(4,3),(8,5)and(16,9)**, Proc.AMS138(2010),355–365. Read opening Theorem1.1 on355–356 and the complete Proposition4.1 proof with following remarks on363–364(PDF9–10). Proposition4.1 obtains a zero-or-one-critical-point smooth map by composing a smooth homeomorphism with a smooth bundle after an exotic-sphere connected sum. The proof's homeomorphism preserves the topological type of every fibre; it does not produce a nonmanifold fibre. The published result is credited for the critical-point context, and the distinct Hurewicz-plus-nonmanifold assertion is stated separately.
4. **Cacciatori–Pigola, Hurewicz fibrations, almost submetries and critical points of smooth maps**, author version arXiv:1601.07763v1,28January2016; Forum Math.29(2017),751–760. Read Theorems1.1–1.2 and Corollaries1.3/1.6 on author-PDF1–2. Their obstruction assumes an aspherical total space and gives acyclicity restrictions on the universal cover of the base; the differential corollaries have target dimension at least two. It does not preclude the sphere-type fibre construction over a circle. No reproof of their global theorem is claimed.
5. **Pushkar–Rudyak, On the minimal number of critical points of functions on h-cobordisms**, Math.Res.Lett.9(2002),241–246. Read the full opening theorem and definitions on241(PDF1). A nontrivial compact connected smooth h-cobordism of dimension at least six requires at least two critical points for a regular boundary-valued function, and the bound is sharp. The nontrivial Whitehead-torsion hypothesis is essential to its comparison; it is not a prohibition on every isolated critical point on a closed manifold over a circle. Later proof pages were retrieved but not audited here.
6. **Farber–Schütz, Closed1-forms with at most one zero**, Topology45(2006),465–473; author version dated31August2004. Read Theorem1 on author-PDF1 and the following integral-class attribution onPDF2. Every nonzero real degree-one cohomology class of a closed connected smooth manifold has a smooth closed representative with at most one zero. In an integral class, integrating periods gives a circle-valued map with at most one critical point; this last implication is elementary and is explicitly an inference, not a quoted separate theorem. The theorem does not assert the full Hurewicz property or nonmanifold fibres. The integral case is credited in that source to Farber's earlier Theorem2.1; its original proof has not been audited here.
7. **Funar, Almost fibrations research project,2018**, two-page author proposal. Read both pages, including its references. Used only as a citation trail to Farber–Schütz and the relevant small-codimension literature; the proposal is not a proof input and its historical state-of-the-art statements are not treated as current2026 facts.

### paper06_final_primary_comparison.md

# Paper6: final targeted primary comparison and theorem-status decisions

Date23November2026. Root searched and read the following material between05:17 and05:22UTC. This audit changes the credit split for the dessin theorem: its combinatorial core is known, while the full holomorphic group and low-valence extension have their own proofs. No priority claim is made.

## Exact searches

System1 queries: `"cellwise" "dessin" automorphism`; `"holomorphic" "fixes every" "edge" dessin`; `"Grothendieck" "divided-power categories" coherence`; `"tensor divided" "polynomial" "coherence"`. These did not return an exact match; a relevant primary ribbon-graph paper by Chapman, Mulase and Safnuk led to the edge-action terminology. Its introduction and reference context were opened, but it is not a new proof input.

System2 queries: `"ribbon graph" automorphism "fixes every edge"`; `"dessin" "cellwise" group`; `"ribbon graphs" "ineffective" automorphisms`; `"divided power" category "mixed" composition axioms`. The first led to the metric-ribbon-graph action; the last identified Richter's paper as relevant chain-level context. Unrelated political uses of divided power and unrelated graph papers were not treated as mathematical evidence.

System1 queries: `Mulase Penkava ribbon graphs automorphisms "exceptional"`; `Mulase Penkava "fixes every edge"`; `Ikonicoff divided power algebras operads composition relations arxiv`. This located Mulase–Penkava's exact classification. The later System2 query `Ikonicoff "Divided power algebras" operad arxiv` located the primary Glasgow/arXiv source. Earlier recorded Hexagon/ECCC and author/journal/arXiv searches remain part of the audit; these new queries are complementary terminology checks, not claims of exhaustive coverage.

## What the primary sources establish

**Mulase–Penkava1998:** read journalPDF6–9, printed880–883; visually inspected880–881, including both exceptional-graph figures and formulas. Also read the author-version definitions and classification, arXiv math-ph/9811024v2, pp3–8. Definition1.9 and the classification on880–881 concern the kernel of ribbon-graph automorphisms acting on edges. Our further requirement of fixed vertices removes their two-vertex family; fixed faces removes the odd-edge one-vertex family. The surviving even-edge one-vertex family is exactly the finite combinatorial exception in our theorem. This is a known specialization, now explicitly credited in title, abstract, Section1 and proof. The sphere's continuous holomorphic kernel and valence-one/two cases are outside that graph-action classification and remain proved here. The primary article's terminology is used precisely; an automorphism commuting with the Belyi map is not conflated with every holomorphic cellwise automorphism.

**Richter2009:** read introduction and Definition2.1 on237–238, then Theorem7.1, Corollary7.2, Definition7.4 and Theorem7.5 on248–250(PDF12–14). These give the transported monoidal chain category, its relation to simplicial commutative algebras, and a divided-power chain-algebra construction. The paper's natural-test reconstruction is a different assertion, and is not described as the first occurrence of chain-level or homology divided powers. The full Richter proof is not claimed as rechecked.

**Ikonicoff, arXiv1712.03694v2:** read opening TheoremsA/B onp2 and contextpp1–3, the reduced-operad definition and monad statements onp9, and Definition3.1.4/Theorem3.1.8 with the intervening verification onpp12–13. The reduced hypothesis is P(0)=0; its full substitution relation is imposed. These established operadic structures do not assert that Grothendieck's reduced list of homogeneous diagrams forces the missing constant/mixed comparison. The finite-coherence theorem remains an explicit version of the classical co-Kleisli mechanism, with the specified data and finite bounds.

## Decisions for all eight opening results

| Opening result | Literature category and exact credit split |
|---|---|
|Theorem1.1, bare data countermodels|New manuscript-specific countermodels in this audit: unit invertibility and mixed composition are separate. Roby, Krause and Ikonicoff establish richer positive structures; Hua's version2 expressly restricts its construction to the homogeneous scope. No published theorem is alleged refuted.|
|Theorem1.2, finite coherence|Known co-Kleisli mechanism, with a new explicit bounded and prescribed-multigrading formulation. Riehl's exact definition is credited; the formulation is proved for the stated tensor data.|
|Theorem1.3, disk restriction|Known full faithfulness and all-kernel case, credited to the opened Hexagon exposition; the exact essential-image matrix-kernel criterion is the refinement proved here for arbitrary additive targets.|
|Theorem1.4, natural divided-power action|New explicit natural-test reconstruction statement in this audit, distinguished from classical divided powers on chains/homology and from Horel/Mandell/Toën derived recovery. No claim of replacement or stronger homotopy detection.|
|Theorem1.5, rank/perfection|Binomial-rank boundedness is an explicit Dold–Kan/Nakayama consequence; derived perfection is the known pseudo-coherent fibre criterion, with exact Stacks credit.|
|Theorem1.6, cellwise groups|Known Mulase–Penkava combinatorial classification specialized to fixed vertices/faces, plus the holomorphic-kernel and low-valence extension proved here. The generic label 'new classification' is not used for its known core.|
|Corollary1.7, homotopy recovery|Known consequence of Horel's derived adjunction, with Mandell and Toën comparison scope explicit.|
|Theorem1.8, Kummer image|Explicit character-kernel and scalar-quotient formulas proved here; bounded saturation/openness is classical context, with Perucca–Sgobba and the November2026 Perucca follow-up credited.|

These categories settle the paper's working literature-status gate relative to the recorded primary comparisons. They are not a proof that an unlocated publication cannot contain a further match. The paper makes no claim of priority and gives exact credit to every located match. Its mathematical arguments and already frozen independent proofs are unchanged by these bibliographic conclusions.

# Paper 1: final comparative literature supplement

Date: 2026-11-23, 05:30–05:45 UTC. Reviewer: GPT-6 Astra, root instance; effort not exposed. This supplements, and does not replace, PAPER1_LITERATURE.md and paper01_primary_literature_extension.md. A new contribution here means the precise assertion proved in this paper after comparison with the named sources; it is not a claim that an exhaustive search has established priority.
