These follow the five graph searches and two terminology probes already logged in the earlier extension. The direct graph-title search located the Wang–Zhou preprint and its 2026 journal publication. The more general descent searches located ordinary sheaf gluing, a schematic-space cylinder/descent paper, and a topos van Kampen abstract. The current Hexagon search led to a directly relevant published tube counterexample, described below. The ECCC and arXiv/OpenAI batches contained no exact relevant result that was identified and read. That bounded observation is not a claim about everything on those sites or about all AI laboratory research.

An OurBigBook page under the account name Codex, dated 7 October 2026, was opened. Its local-sheaf-topos criterion concerns a point whose only neighbourhood is the whole space, not the membership-groupoid criterion here. The account name does not establish authorship by OpenAI. It is excluded from the paper's comparison rather than being cited as a laboratory result without evidence.

## Graph-poset primary sources

1. **Abramovich–Caporaso–Payne, The tropicalization of the moduli space of curves**, Ann. Sci. ENS 48 (2015), 765–809. Root read complete PDF pages 13–18, printed pages 775–780. Section 3.2 specifies weighted stable graphs, ordered legs, half-edge automorphisms, and contraction with the first Betti number of a contracted fibre included in its new vertex weight. Section 3.3 gives the closure/contraction relation. Proposition 3.4.1 and Sections 4.2–4.3 distinguish the stratum quotient and the generalized cone complex with its automorphisms. This is the established geometric and categorical context. It does not assert the conditional-meet classification of labelled graph types. The paper must preserve the distinction between an orbit poset and the category of specified contractions and automorphisms.

2. **Wang–Zhou, Möbius Inversion and Duality for Summations of Stable Graphs**, arXiv:2401.11717v1, 22 January 2024; journal citation Acta Math. Sinica, English Series 42 (2026), 269–292, DOI 10.1007/s10114-026-4262-2. Root opened the official journal landing page, which confirms the published citation, but read the arXiv version, not the journal PDF. Read opening definitions on PDF pages 1–3, all of Section 3.2 and its continuation on pages 8–12, and the entire appendix on pages 20–23. Appendix pages 21–22 were also inspected visually. Definition 3.1 orders stable graphs by reverse contraction. Proposition 3.1 and Theorem 3.2 develop the generalized rational zeta/Möbius inversion; Lemma 3.4 computes the interval coefficient to the corolla. The legs in that version are unlabelled, and the moduli objects are quotiented by the symmetric group. Its small-genus appendix gives familiar finite graph diagrams but not the classification in the present paper. In particular its genus-one three-leg graph count must not be compared directly with our 23 labelled types. This is recent direct context, not evidence for or against the labelled conditional-meet theorem beyond the assertions actually read.

3. **Tosteson, Stability in the homology of Deligne–Mumford compactifications**, arXiv:1801.03894v5, 15 November 2021. Root opened the author's publication page and read PDF pages 8–10 in full. Proposition 3.5 gives the unique genus-zero reduction by spanning forests; Warning 3.6 says that this reduction is not order preserving for the original contraction order. Proposition 3.7 and Remark 3.8 instead construct an appropriate coarsened order. This precise neighbouring result concerns a quotient order and does not provide conditional meets in the original labelled contraction poset. It is cited for that distinction.

4. **Tosteson, Representation stability for the Kontsevich space of stable maps**, arXiv:2201.05100v2, 6 July 2022. Read PDF pages 4–6, including Definitions 3.1, 3.3, 3.4, finiteness in Proposition 3.5, saturation and spanning-forest discussion, and the statement and beginning of the proof of Proposition 3.9. The remainder of that proof was not read. This concerns decorated stable-map graphs and representation stability. It is a contextual lead, not an input to the classification. A wording issue in one displayed contraction formula has not been adjudicated against the journal version and is not asserted to be an author correction.

5. **Proudfoot–Ramos, The contraction category of graphs**, arXiv:1907.11234v2, 17 July 2020. Read PDF pages 1–5 completely, including Theorem 1.1 and the definitions in Section 2.1. Its contractions have connected contractible fibres and preserve the ordinary first Betti number. It does not use the same weighted stable-graph contractions, in which loops may be contracted by increasing vertex genus. Its categorical Noetherian result is not the conditional-meet assertion. The definition comparison prevents conflating these two subjects.

The Caporaso Handbook author PDF returned HTTP503 after the search tool timed out; no primary reading is claimed. The successful ACP reading provides the needed primary moduli context. The source metadata manifest records all these successes and failures without copying a PDF or extract into the deliverables.

## Ordinary descent and the recent manuscript comparison

**Stacks Project, Lemmas 6.33.2 and 6.33.4, Tags 00AL and 00AN.** Root opened both complete statement pages and read the matching-sections proof of Lemma 6.33.2 and the equivalence proof in Lemma 6.33.4. The second, étale-space proof of 6.33.2 was not needed. The known theorem is the equivalence between sheaves on a space and sheaves on an open cover with overlap isomorphisms satisfying the triple cocycle. Paper1 credits it explicitly. The paper's local-family argument identifies a particular open cover; its displayed mapping-category argument treats the synchronized pro-object. Neither presents ordinary sheaf descent as a new theorem.

**Hua, Énoncés des notes de travail de Grothendieck démontrés en Lean 4, et questions ouvertes**, Hexagon2611.00125v2, 5 November 2026. The primary version page was opened and the PDF bytes are those already identified in SOURCE_REFERENCES.tsv, SHA256 11d22eeee51d4bb73bd2bd899e4f0676d9ad286141b7fcfd8af148941f62208b. Root read PDF/printed page286, the entire relevant page339 and page340, including all displayed assertions and their prose proofs. The page286 statement about a punctured tubular neighbourhood concerns a normal-crossings divisor and reports a homotopy-colimit reading of cote147; it does not contain a graph-meet classification. Page339 treats the arbitrary-index finite-comparison formulation and the mixed-tube indices. **Counterexample70.3 on page340 already gives the failure of the successor-pair diagram for the linear three-stratum plane**, and the following theorem proves the depth-one topological gluing assertion. Its stated computation/formalization boundary is retained: the ordinary final-topology counterexample is designated Lean-verified by its author, while the homotopy Euler calculation is explicitly handwritten. We read the proof, not its Lean implementation. The new paper credits this known successor-pair obstruction and distinguishes its all-pair sheaf obstruction and connected-stratum four-dimensional example. It makes no correction claim about Hua's exposition and no unverified formal-certificate claim. This is concrete relevant recent AI-assisted work, with attribution to the named author and the actual source disclosure.

**Sánchez González, arXiv:2301.03123v1.** Root opened the primary PDF and read the introduction/definitions on pages1–5, Theorem6.10 and the following Theorem6.11 statement and qualified proof discussion on pages17–18, Corollary6.14, and the étale fundamental-groupoid/fundamental-group theorems and following remarks on pages23–24. Those results concern schematic finite spaces, flat immersions, cylinder constructions and algebraic van Kampen descent. They are not the all-sheaf pointwise membership-groupoid test for a general topological open diagram. Theorem7.8 was not checked, and no claim about its proof is made. The author's expressly one-direction proof discussion is not silently converted into an author-error allegation. This paper is a bounded neighbouring comparison, not a proof input in the tube paper.

The 2003 topos van Kampen article was seen only at the publisher abstract, not in full, and remains a recorded search lead. No exact primary theorem is imported from that abstract. Translated SGA4 search snippets were similarly not used as a substitute for a primary theorem reading.

## Exact division for the nine principal assertions

| Printed main assertion | Classification and established input | What the present proof supplies |
|---|---|---|
| Theorem1.1, open-tube descent | Known ordinary descent, Stacks00AL/00AN; the synchronized formulation is a specific application and deduction | The least-index cover proof and the finite-degree 2-Pro comparison, including the infinite-family convention |
| Theorem1.2, insufficiency of prescribed pieces | The successor-pair linear obstruction is known in Hua70.3, p340; the all-pair sheaf assertion is a new contribution relative to the sources inspected | Full compatible sheaf data obstructing gluing even with all prescribed pair/triple pieces, and the connected-stratum R4 example |
| Theorem1.3, membership criterion | Ordinary descent and fundamental groupoid gluing are known mechanisms; the precise iff criterion for the stated T1 open diagram is the formulation proved here | Sufficiency by étale-space descent and all three necessity obstructions, with the T1 skyscraper condition explicit |
| Theorem1.4, stable-graph conditional meets | Stable-graph strata and contraction posets are established in ACP and Schmitt; Wang–Zhou treat a related unlabelled poset; Tosteson treats a quotient order | Complete classification for individually labelled legs, with a general tree proof, complete finite positive cases and infinite negative families |
| Theorem1.5, exodromy truncation | Known consequence of LurieHA A.9.3/A.9.4 and the universal property of categorical truncation; Treumann supplies the stack context | The exact truncation index and a comparison of the information at each level, under the known theorem's hypotheses |
| Theorem1.6, finite comparisons, shape and local filling | Shape adjunction, Ex-infinity and local filling are established in HTT, DHI, Hoyois, BHH and BS at the locators in PAPER1_LITERATURE.md | The finite-comparison equivalence and explicit all-index formulations under the stated hypercompletion convention; no novelty claim for the underlying convergence theorem |
| Theorem1.7, exit deployment | A consequence under explicit recursive geometric hypotheses of the conical/exit machinery of AFT, BGH and Volpe | The complete flag-space identification and relative induction retaining outer faces; no unrestricted exit equivalence without these hypotheses |
| Corollary1.8, global tame-pair pushout | Known tameness/holink mechanism in Quinn, with a precise prospace formulation here | Compatible shrinking maps and path homotopies in the exact metric/global forward-tame setting; separate statement-only audit is still in progress at this note's timestamp |
| Theorem1.9, proper singular-fibre Hurewicz examples | New combined construction relative to the sources inspected; Mazur/Zeeman, Milnor and Ferry are established inputs; independent proof uses Ungar | Closed PL/smooth six-dimensional examples, a nonmanifold fibre, full Hurewicz lifting, and exactly one smooth critical point; Farber–Schütz and Funar–Pintea–Zhang are credited for their different one-critical-point conclusions |

This table settles the positive matches and the exact claimed additions against the named sources. It does not treat unsuccessful searches as a mathematical proof of originality. Published-statement corrections, if any are established, belong in CORRECTIONS_FOR_AUTHORS.md and not in the paper. None is newly asserted by this supplement.

### paper03_E_literature_06.md

# Paper 3: primary inputs for four E assertions

Actual reading and retrieval record, 23 November 2026. The machine-readable companion `paper03_E_primary_manifest_06.json` contains exact file hashes and paths. Third-party files remain temporary and are not copied into the paper package. Named foundational theorems are imported in their stated scope; their complete underlying theories are not claimed as fresh proofs.

## Deligne–Milne: cofinal algebraic quotients and fibre functors

The retained text is the corrected typescript dated11 February2022: <https://www.jmilne.org/math/xnotes/tc2022.pdf>. This pass reused its already retrieved binary and read pp18–21 and26, including Proposition2.6 and Corollary2.7 on algebraic quotients and Proposition2.21(a), with its proof, on faithful flatness for a full tensor subcategory. Theorem2.11 on p21 was inspected in context. Theorem3.2 on p31 and the degree-one Riemann statements6.20–6.21 on p65 had been read in earlier logged passes and are reused as exposed inputs. The original1982 scan is not silently substituted for the2022 locator convention.

Application: finite algebraic quotient systems for the Betti group and Tate kernel, the quotient onto the elliptic GL2 group, and the affine tensor-Isom torsor. The arbitrary-index invariant Lie lifting argument is supplied in the paper; it is not ascribed to an unquoted inverse-limit theorem.

## Arapura: smooth proper transport

Freshly retrieved author notes dated16 July2003: <https://www.math.purdue.edu/~arapura/preprints/sheaves.pdf>, 143PDFpages. Read PDF98, printed97, Theorem12.1.1 (Ehresmann’s theorem for a proper smooth submersion), and PDF101–102, printed100–101, including the local-system/path-groupoid definitions and Theorem12.3.1. These locators distinguish PDF sheets from printed pages.

Application: transport on each smooth proper spread, functorial in its cohomology local system. The measure-zero cone construction for one path avoiding all countably many bad loci is proved in the new text. No countability restriction is imported into the uncountable example: there one explicitly avoids the common set L(i). No Legendre coefficient from the adjacent lecture section is used.

## Wibmer: the exact complex-coefficient statement

Freshly retrieved final journal PDF: <https://link.springer.com/content/pdf/10.1007/s00031-022-09767-z.pdf>, DOI <https://doi.org/10.1007/s00031-022-09767-z>. The final issue is *Transformation Groups*30 (2025), pp447–454; the first online date is26 August2022. The binary has8pages. This pass read PDF1–4 (printed447–450): Theorems1.1–1.2 on p447; the torsor convention and reduction via Deligne–Milne3.2 on p448; the finite-intersection/topological setup on p449; and Proposition2.3 and the beginning of its proof on p450. It does **not** record a full reading of pp451–454 or an independent reproof of Wibmer’s theorem.

Application: every affine group torsor over an algebraically closed coefficient field has a point, with no finite-type or cardinality assumption. Thus two neutral rational fibre functors have an isomorphism after extension to C. This is a known theorem, not a new result of the paper. It gives no Z-point and does not turn arbitrary nonempty integral finite-stage comparison sets into a compatible family.

## Stacks Project: standard algebraic inputs

The following official pages were opened and their stated proofs or reductions inspected; the six HTML files were then freshly saved and hashed:

- Lemma10.47.8, Tag037P, <https://stacks.math.columbia.edu/tag/037P>: relative algebraic closedness implies geometric irreducibility; the proof uses irreducibility of the minimal polynomial after field extension.
- Section10.45, Tag05DU, <https://stacks.math.columbia.edu/tag/05DU>: Lemmas10.45.2 and10.45.6 give perfectness in characteristic zero and geometric reducedness of reduced algebras over a perfect field.
- Lemma39.8.2, Tag047N, <https://stacks.math.columbia.edu/tag/047N>: locally algebraic groups in characteristic zero are smooth; its reference to Lemma33.25.1 was also opened, but that latter HTML was not separately retained.
- Lemma29.35.3, Tag01V8, <https://stacks.math.columbia.edu/tag/01V8>: flat, locally finitely presented maps with smooth fibres are smooth. Applied to a faithfully flat algebraic group map, whose geometric fibres are translates of its smooth kernel, this proves the needed differential surjectivity.
- Lemma32.10.1, Tag01ZM, <https://stacks.math.columbia.edu/tag/01ZM>: finite presentations, maps and equalities descend through affine inverse limits. Its displayed proof was read.
- Lemma32.8.9, Tag0C0C, <https://stacks.math.columbia.edu/tag/0C0C>: smoothness eventually descends. Used after spreading a projective presentation; properness is already supplied by that projective model.

The characteristic-zero resolution theorem and the ordinary functoriality of cycle classes/proper pushforward remain explicit classical inputs, not fresh foundational proofs. The paper explains their exact use in constructing flat correspondence maps. It is unnecessary to resolve a simultaneous infinite collection: each finite list has its own good open, and the common path lies in all of them.

## Reused exposed arithmetic and realization inputs

The non-CM GL2 calculation for the rational test curve is the earlier checked Section4 argument. Schneider’s1937 resultIIc, Section1, p5, had already been checked and is now applied to the explicit post-source ratio i*cuberoot(2). The real degree-one realization is the earlier checked Proposition9.3. The negative uncountable example restricts to the previously checked full isogeny obstruction in Section10.2. These are dependency reuses, not new independent checks of their whole proofs in this cluster.

The frozen derivation had chosen a transcendental j-value by a nonconstant arc and countability. The explicit algebraic, nonquadratic period ratio is a later source-informed refinement. Both routes are sufficient, but the record does not backdate the stronger explicit choice into the frozen attempt.

No novelty or priority claim follows from these searches, these exact-input comparisons, or agreement with the source manuscript.

## Input and inference distinctions

The ring-class theorem identifies a field, while the new labelled-operator proof separates its places. The generalized-dihedral criterion is known; the transfer computation checks that its hypotheses are met with **actual** complex conjugation. The elementary-2 limitation is a quotient-group deduction and concerns that CM construction, not all motivic tests.

The real CM test combines the Riemann bilinear criterion, known CM algebraicity, the explicitly calculated type traces and the proved Artin-block descent. Its field labels are pointwise labels, and its single Tate-valued polarization supplies a common similitude sign. Forgetting those labels would invalidate the deduction.

For the quartic comparison the named input is precisely Milne9.10, which supplies a rational homomorphism. The all-prime lattice test and the quotient property of multiplication by m make it an actual isomorphism. This closes the retained construction without claiming the stronger model assertion that was unverified in the frozen note. Milne9.11(c) gives the exact cyclotomic multiplier. Higher correspondences use the previously checked SGA4 ExposéXVI Th4.1 and standard Galois-equivariance of cycle classes; no fresh whole-SGA reread is claimed.

The source's citation to Serre's Hilbert-class-field example is not needed by the retained commutator argument. The paper does not assert that exact field identification, and no new Serre primary verification is claimed here. Similarly Deligne's restriction-of-scalars convention is not required as an imported theorem: the proof uses the explicitly defined underlying scheme and checks all diagonal blocks, module actions and correspondences directly.

Finiteness I remains an arithmetic theorem credited to its primary antecedents. The finite Hom-basis skeleton is the categorical consequence proved here. It has a different domain from the full fixed-k correspondence diagram, in which arbitrarily large finite extensions create geometrically disconnected objects of increasing rank. The projective Tate condition is a necessary consequence on that larger domain, not a sufficiency theorem or an answer to the universal Artin-image problem.

No claims about the current literature beyond these precise checked sources are inferred from a broad or title-only search. The primary action manifest records targeted readings only.

## Intrinsic partition: the whole mathematical quantifier

Both the independent Boolean fixed-point construction and the manuscript's
descending-dimension construction cover every closed saturated union. In
the latter construction, strata of dimension k are open in the closed
unfrozen remainder, and the boundaries removed by the finitely many
singularity tests have smaller dimension. Each connected component is
frozen before its individual closure is used in the next refinement.
The closed remainder cannot later accumulate onto a frozen stratum.
These are the necessary frontier invariants; taking components only after
finishing a partition would not suffice.

For a closed saturated Y and a final stratum S, its germ is either S's
regular germ or the germ of the union of the higher-dimensional closures
approaching S. That finite union was tested before S was frozen. This is
why the theorem's quantifier is stronger than a condition only on separate
stratum closures and is nevertheless proved.

The Boolean intersection of two admissible partitions is again admissible.
Closure saturation makes its atoms locally closed by the strict definable
frontier-dimension inequality. The singular locus of an atom's closure
cannot contain its dense atom. The closed local-dimension superlevel sets
are saturated in both partitions, so atoms have one manifold dimension.
Individual definable components of saturated sets are also saturated when
the original strata are connected. This property passes to the Boolean
intersection. A partition with the fewest strata is therefore coarser than
every other admissible partition, not merely minimal under refinement.

The root freshly inspected Shiota's primary arXiv:1002.1508v3, printed
pp.5–6: Theorem 2.1 is the compact marked-family Hauptvermutung, and
Theorem 2.2(1)–(2) includes compact marks and a simplex-preserving
self-homeomorphism. Taking the function to be zero and marking the compact
chart ball D proves the local bridge. Subdividing at its center p makes
p a fixed vertex. Pointed compact Hauptvermutung then gives a PL Euclidean
germ. The whole modeled set need not be compact or locally closed. No
global noncompact uniqueness theorem is substituted. The manuscript
clarifications make the definable-strata convention, empty case, chart
interior and compact marks explicit.

Dimension layers are relatively clopen unions of their finitely many
k-strata; hence selected unions are pure k-manifolds. The lexicographic
refinement has the stated exact differences and block endpoints. Naturality
transports the admissible class using the local PL-regularity equivalence.
No stronger closed-union axiom is inferred for merged dimension layers.

The new compact two-dimensional counterexample was checked separately.
Three discs share a singular circle S; a sphere attached along a proper
closed subarc contributes local link ranks 2, 3, and 4 along S. Tubes away
from S connect the regular pieces without changing those germs. Thus both
strata X minus S and S are connected; {X minus S,S} is coarsest in both
classes because every admissible partition must saturate Sing_PL(X)=S.
Local homology cannot vary along a CS stratum, so this canonical partition
is not CS. The rational moment-curve embedding into R^5 is justified by
affine independence of any six vertices. This is a proof of a distinction
from the inspected CS notion, not a claimed reading of King's original.

King's exact original theorem/page is still unavailable. Root repeated the
exact-title and intrinsic-stratification searches on 2026-11-23; the author
bibliography and publisher bibliographic record were available but did not
give the original full text. The mathematical statement can be accepted
at its explicit scope while this bibliographic comparison remains an
independent paper-level gate. There is no priority claim.

## Dimension five and the additional real ranges

The original Burghelea–Lashof n>5 input concerns the base dimension; root
does not reinterpret it as a cylinder inequality. The missing base-five
case uses Kirby–Siebenmann's relative sliced structure-space classification.
The downloaded 1977 original scan has SHA256
9e83ee5de874e6cc9a55f91960e685d0aad09919eb6894be06e3f6f25db7980b,
at https://www.maths.ed.ac.uk/~v1ranick/papers/ks.pdf.

Root read Essay II Theorems 2.1–2.2, printed pp.73–74; Essay V (1.5)
and its relative paragraph, p.229; (4.3)–(4.5), p.244; §§5.1–5.2,
pp.246–247; Theorem 5.5(I), pp.251–252; and Theorems 5.8–5.9,
p.253. Root additionally rendered and actually viewed the original pages
229, 244, 251, and 253, checking the inequalities and the full diagrams.
These are statements about simplicial spaces and Kan fibrations, not only
sets of concordance classes. The parameter retract/horn condition is not
silently applied to an entire simplex boundary.

For N=B times I, dim B=5, interior and boundary dimensions are 6 and 5,
both avoiding 4. The rank-six lift space relative to a fixed bottom germ
is weakly contractible because that germ is a homotopy equivalent
subspace. The upper boundary is free. Its additional rank-five compatible
lift has contractible homotopy fibre, since the actual stabilization
TOP_5/PL_5 to TOP_6/PL_6 is a weak equivalence. The relative fibre-product
classification therefore contracts the entire structure space. The orbit
fibration gives the PL–TOP automorphism equivalence, including components.

The explicit map lambda_s(t)=min(t+s,(1+t)/2), 0<=s<=1/4, compresses a
bottom-fixed concordance into [s,1] and extends it by the identity below s.
Its two-variable inverse is piecewise affine. Both seam continuity and
joint parameter continuity follow, and the construction preserves an
existing fixed bottom collar, the identity family, and relative parameter
faces. It therefore passes between the germ and pointwise-bottom models
in both categories. Finite disjoint unions give finite products.

For bases of dimension at most two the same relative argument uses the
contractible unstable TOP_j/PL_j fibres for j<=3 on p.253. The real
PL-to-definable-to-TOP triangle then has both maps weak equivalences in
base dimensions <=2 or >=5 by the accepted all-field comparison. In
particular the full A.114 splitting is valid, with zero TOP kernel; its
circle-family assertion follows from exact based PL replacement. A.155's
weaker real-model transfer statements follow as consequences. Neither
A.131 nor A.161's additional algorithmic presentation claims is certified
merely by this dimension repair.

## The stronger multiplicative existence obstruction

The staged theorem proves the full extra clauses of B.031 and B.035, not
only noninjectivity. For b outside the finite normalization T, clear its
negative height-one valuations by c so that d=bc lies in T and (c,d)=T.
In the higher-dimensional case membership in R^sharp forces every negative
valuation to occur at a height-one maximal ideal. Chinese remainder
uniformizers are units at all other maximal ideals, so 1/c and each needed
uniformizer inverse lie in T[b]. This avoids the invalid inference that a
negative valuation alone gives a global inverse.

A nonzero conductor element a controls coefficients as well as values.
The integer-valued indicator H at the chosen DVR branch can be chosen in
T[pi^(-1)][X]. Then g=a(H-H(0)) has values in R and coefficients in
aT[b], which is contained in R[b] and hence in the stated coefficient
ring L. This latter inclusion is essential to the overring-sensitive
claim. The source finite-residue digit construction supplies the indicator;
no approximation with uncontrolled coefficients is substituted.

For a purported finite r-variable separated expression, r=v_0(a)+1,
clear all factor denominators by e and remove its pi valuation to get
e' in T. Chinese remainders give x in e' p_1 congruent to one at the
chosen branch. With u=ax in R, every factor difference h(u)-h(0) belongs
to m, even when the second maximal ideal p_1 has higher dimension.
The r-fold difference consequently belongs to m^r. The chosen indicator
instead makes its value g(u^r) have valuation v_0(a)<r. This contradiction
is exact. If every element had a binary multiplicative expression,
iteration would give the forbidden r-variable expression. The conclusion
is that some element has no binary expression, not that none do.

## 3. Clause-by-clause comparison

### 3.1 Clause 1: actual integral splitting, not merely generic or rational splitting

The live calculation at 1627 agrees exactly with the independent invariant and point-count calculation. The live endomorphism argument at 1636 and the immutable dependency A.4.20 at 741 show why non-CM survives algebraically closed characteristic-zero extension.

The target uses a different integral splitting proof from the independent freeze. The source gives the complete Kummer model at A.4.21, line 759. For \(N=5^n\), its components have equations \(x^N=q^a\), and group addition across a carry multiplies the coordinate by \(q^{-\lfloor(a+b)/N\rfloor}\). The proposed point \(a\mapsto(q_n^a,a)\) therefore respects addition:
\[
 q_n^a q_n^b q^{-\lfloor(a+b)/N\rfloor}
   =q_n^{a+b-N\lfloor(a+b)/N\rfloor}.
\]
The transition to level \(5N\) sends \((x,a)\) to \((x,5a)\), and \(q_{n+1}^{5a}=q_n^a\). Thus this is a compatible integral section at every level. Since \(q\) is a unit and its roots lie in \(\mathbb C_5\), those roots are units of the valuation ring. The construction really occurs over \(\mathcal O_{\mathbb C_5}\).

The freeze's proper-extension argument independently proves the same integral result without choosing Serre–Tate coordinates. These are two valid routes, not a rational splitting substituted for the assertion. The multiplicative summand is the Hodge–Tate line by functoriality of the exact sequence, as both texts say.

**Verdict:** full agreement; no correction needed.

### 3.2 Clause 2: all primes, Frobenius convention, and the extra supersingular statement

The live proof uses the closed coframe \(\omega,\eta=-dt-\xi\); the frozen proof first works on the elliptic function field. They are equivalent. In the live coframe,
\[
 \omega(D_a)=1,\quad\eta(D_a)=a,\quad
 \omega(V)=0,\quad\eta(V)=1,
\]
and Cartier contraction gives \(\omega(D_a^p)=H_p,\ \eta(D_a^p)=-K_p\). These determine
\[
 D_a^p=H_pD_a-(K_p+aH_p)V
\]
with the requested sign. The source additionally writes the two intermediate elliptic identities used in the freeze at 1279–1283.

Perfection of \(k\), \(p>3\), the numerator-degree bounds, and the \(p^{-1}\)-linear convention are present. The proof has no dependence on the finite certificate calculations mentioned afterward. The restriction-stability criterion follows immediately from the coframe or restriction to \(k(E)\). A short explicit derivation would improve visibility but is not a missing mathematical step.

The target also proves an assertion not needed in the frozen theorem: \(H_p,K_p\) cannot both vanish. I checked that addendum rather than importing it into the independent freeze. The second-kind form \(\xi\) has principal part \(z^{-2}dz=d(-1/z)\) at infinity, with \(z=-2x/y\). Removing it supplies a de Rham Čech cocycle. Its pairing with \(\omega\) is nonzero because
\[
 \operatorname{Res}_O((1/z)\omega)=1.
\]
Hence \([\omega],[\xi]\) form a de Rham basis. The degree-one conjugate spectral sequence on a smooth proper curve gives the indicated surjection to \(H^0(E^{(p)},\Omega^1)\): its only potentially relevant outgoing differential would land in \(H^2(\mathcal O)\), which vanishes on a curve. Cartier on the cocycle agrees with the displayed rational-form Cartier value, since it kills the exact transition. The two images therefore cannot both be zero. This validates the additional supersingular conclusion \(H_p=0\Rightarrow K_p\neq0\).

**Verdict:** full agreement for the requested universal formula and exact criterion; the target's additional ordinary/supersingular classification is also justified.

### 3.3 Clause 3: the explicit-machine mismatch and the all-\(\mathbb C\) exclusion

The packet's instruction movement list is left/right. The live manuscript at 1885 and immutable C.1.5 at 33 instead use left/stay/right. This changes the specified number; it is not merely different notation for the same enumeration.

One can see the difference already at index three. In the \(r=1\) block, the first three tables differ only in the last, blank-symbol instruction. With two movements, the third instruction in the ordered instruction list writes \(0\), enters halt state \(1\), and moves left. All nonblank rows in that third table move left in state \(0\), so on input three the machine reaches a blank and halts. With three movements, the third blank instruction writes \(0\), remains in state \(0\), and moves right; every row stays in state \(0\), so that machine cannot halt. At indices one and two neither model's machine halts. Consequently the two base-four parameters already differ at their third digit; the largest possible remaining tail cannot cancel that difference. In fact their difference, two-move minus three-move, is at least
\[
 4^{-3}-\sum_{n>3}4^{-n}=\frac1{96}.
\]

The theorem itself is existential and the live three-move construction is valid. Its enumeration is effective and contains the diagonal machine produced from a hypothetical decider. The same halting contradiction and digit-recovery argument apply. Thus the live parameter is noncomputable. Everything after that point in the proof uses only \(a>0\) and noncomputability. The entire polarization and geometric-exclusion argument applies unchanged. This paragraph is a **post-freeze check of the live parameter**, not a claim that the freeze had already treated that exact number.

The live/source density argument for an algebraic point on the analytic component is valid: an étale chart has a local analytic inverse, and the preimages of algebraic coordinate points are algebraic because the chart is quasi-finite. The algebraic-period and rational-basis arguments then force a computable line ratio.

There is no replacement of algebraic projectors by arbitrary Hodge projectors, no restriction of the alleged realization to \(\overline{\mathbb Q}\) at the outset, and no appeal to countability alone.

For the polarization comparison, the live \(Q\)-matrix is
\[
 E_{\mathrm{live}}=\begin{pmatrix}0&1\\-1&0\end{pmatrix},
\]
which gives the correct positive value in the packet's convention. Immutable C.1.4 instead uses

and \(h(z)|_{V^{p,q}}=z^{-p}\bar z^{-q}\). In that convention the Weil matrix is

\]
and the positive real form is \((2\pi i)^3q(x,Cy)\). Thus the exact translation is \(q=-(2\pi i)^{-3}Q\). ### 3.4 Clause 4: one whole comparison and a genuine obstruction for another pair

The source/live positive example uses the polygon from \(e\) to \(e+1\); the freeze uses a horizontal path at transcendental imaginary height. Both paths avoid all algebraic branch and bad values. The live proof at 2194 expressly checks compatibility when enlarging the finite field and finite collection, so it supplies one family of maps for the whole diagram. It is not merely the assertion that each finite subdiagram has some comparison.

Cup products, Künneth maps, and cycle actions are addressed explicitly. Duality appears explicitly in immutable E.2.1 at 137. Trace compatibility follows because smooth complex fiber orientations transport continuously. The phrase about “specified projector lattices” is valid for lattices obtained functorially from the geometric lattice, such as its intersection with a rational projector image. It should not be read as an assertion about unrelated lattices independently prescribed at the two embeddings.

For the negative example, the live/source factorial element is a valid alternative to the prime-component element used in the freeze. Its residue compatibility is exact. The inequality \(0<s_n<n!/2\) starts at \(n=4\); induction uses \(s_{n+1}=s_n+n!\). It excludes both positive and negative diagonal integers. The closure of the integral Legendre powers gives \(u(2a)\), and an automorphism of the full torsion field extends to the algebraic closure.

The quotient-isogeny argument is fully present. It uses all cyclic finite subgroups, which already suffice: the three cyclic summands generated modulo \(n\) by \(e_1,e_2,e_1+e_2\) force scalar reduction. Equality of quotient lattices follows from the finite-index domain lattice and naturality, rather than from an assumed ability to compare isogenies only up to rational scale. The final implication \(2a\in\mathbb Z\Rightarrow a\in\mathbb Z\) in \(\widehat{\mathbb Z}\) is valid: the integer is even in \(\mathbb Z_2\), and multiplication by two is injective on the whole product.

The finite-comparison paragraph is also sufficient. A finite extension of \(\overline{\mathbb Q}(t)\) has a geometrically integral curve model; both embeddings give complex points in every required nonempty open, and that smooth open curve is path connected. Transport along a suitable path compares the finite diagram. It need not be the lift of one loop selected uniformly for every finite extension.

Finally, E.1.3 and the live path-tower paragraph are separate illustrations, not substitutes for the actual abelian obstruction. Their integer winding congruences are correct. The transition path maps need not be surjective, whereas their completed counterparts have compatible profinite values.

**Verdict:** both full quantifier scopes are established for the actual labelled diagrams.

### 3.5 Clause 5: labelled CM conjugacy and the exact module scope

The source/live ideal arithmetic and endomorphism obstruction agree with the freeze. The norm argument excludes a generator. The equality \(I^2=(2)\) removes the ambiguity between the two inverse-ideal class conventions, so the short live reciprocity paragraph is already sufficient to justify the pair up to the required labelled analytic isomorphism.

The longer frozen idele construction makes the choice completely explicit: take \(z_2=1+\sqrt{-5}\), \(z_\ell=1\) for \(\ell\neq2\), and use Milne's \(\operatorname{art}=\operatorname{rec}^{-1}\). Then \(K_0\cap z\widehat{\mathcal O}=I\). The theorem transports the labelled \(K_0\)-action; since the chosen automorphism fixes \(K_0\), it does not relabel \(a\) as \(\bar a\).

The local module statement can likewise be made explicit:
\[
 I_\ell=\mathcal O_\ell\quad(\ell\neq2),\qquad
 I_2=(1+\sqrt{-5})\mathcal O_2,\qquad
 \frac2{1+\sqrt{-5}}=\frac{1-\sqrt{-5}}3\in\mathcal O_2.
\]
The current appeal to invertibility of ideals in the maximal quadratic order is correct. The resulting rational and local comparisons are exactly the labelled \(\mathcal O\)-module isomorphisms in Theorem 1.3(5). They are not, solely from this ideal arithmetic, a stronger claim about isometries for additional fixed polarizations or a simultaneous comparison of an entire larger category.

**Verdict:** complete agreement at the stated module and labelled-endomorphism scope.

## Literal fixed-base defect: confirmed in the released manuscript

The independent freeze noticed that the statement packet defines a cone parametrization only as carrying the base onto the actual outer face. The live definition in `main.tex`, lines 115–122 at first access, has exactly that setwise wording. No identity-on-base normalization occurs in that definition. Consequently the literal space of all parametrizations of a fixed cone is not the contractible group in A.101.

Fix one normalized parametrization of a cone with base Y. Restriction of arbitrary base-setwise parametrizations to Y gives a split map to `Homeo_def(Y)`, split by coning. Its kernel is the group of homeomorphisms of CY fixing the vertex and every base point; the Alexander contraction contracts that kernel. Thus the strongest statement retaining arbitrary setwise base parametrizations is

\[
\operatorname{Par}_{\rm setwise}(C,Y,o)\simeq\operatorname{Homeo}_{\rm def}(Y),
\]

whereas the subspace of base-identity parametrizations is contractible. These are statements about the stipulated simplicial finite-family models.

For an explicit strict example, use the smaller centered interval in a larger interval, marked at its midpoint, with the two endpoints as base `Y=S^0`. Identity and reflection give two components of the raw parametrization space; a continuous family cannot interchange the two discrete base points. In the actual torus example, base maps inducing different automorphisms of `H_1(T^6;Z)` likewise lie in different components. Therefore A.111's assertion that the space of rigidifications of a fixed cone is contractible is false with the literal raw setwise definition. This defect is independent of, and does not refute, the derived strict-cone choice calculation `BP_def(T^6)`.

The same missing normalization appears inside the displayed A.105 straightening. At live lines 902–904 the proposed cone replacement is `psi' C(f|Y) psi^{-1}`, claimed to agree with f on Y. If `a=psi|Y` and `a'=psi'|Y'` are arbitrary base self-homeomorphisms, its boundary restriction is `a' (f|Y) a^{-1}`, which need not equal `f|Y`. A coordinate-corrected replacement would use `C((a')^{-1}(f|Y)a)` instead. Thus the printed formula is justified by the intended base-identity normalization, not by the literal definition as released.

### Proposed strongest corrected conventions, staged only

The smallest coherent repair is to require every chosen cone parametrization `psi:CY -> C` to satisfy `psi(y,1)=y` for every actual outer-face point y. The A.101 base-fixing phrase should explicitly mean pointwise. The fixed-cone clause in A.111 should explicitly say “base-fixed rigidifications.” No cone existence is lost: any existing setwise parametrization is normalized by precomposition with the cone of the inverse base restriction.

Under this repair the A.105 replacement agrees exactly on the base, the discrepancy belongs to the Alexander-contractible pointwise kernel, the blow-up collar has its prescribed identity boundary identification, and the A.109 attachment map has its asserted exact inverse on mapping spaces. Cone maps between normalized objects may still induce arbitrary homeomorphisms of their outer faces; normalization does not impose pointwise boundary-fixing on all categorical morphisms. The five equivalences and six torus failures are justified for this corrected normalized category. A separate TeX fragment records this proposed repair; no live manuscript edit has been made.

### Raw-category consequences: two interpretations must be separated

The phrase “a rigid cone map is the cone of its boundary map” is ambiguous before normalization. The printed A.105 replacement uses the actual outer-face map, which is the interpretation directly supported by that formula. Under this literal interpretation a rigid map satisfies

\[
f|_C=\psi' C(g)\psi^{-1},\quad g=f|_Y,
\qquad\text{hence}\qquad a'g=ga.
\]

If the target parametrization is normalized, so a' is the identity, bijectivity of g forces a to be the identity too. A raw object with a nonidentity base restriction therefore cannot be isomorphic, even in the homotopy category, to a normalized object. In fact all simplices of a mapping space to a normalized object are empty. As attachment and collapse produce normalized cones, the raw A.109 cone-attachment and A.105 rigid blow-up equivalences fail essential surjectivity in this interpretation. Forgetting cone coordinates also fails full faithfulness: two rigidifications of the same strict interval cone, one normalized and one exchanging its two base points, have no rigid map between them but have the identity map after forgetting. Thus the corresponding equivalence entries of A.112 cannot simply be endorsed for this literal raw category.

If instead boundary maps in the word “rigid” are expressly taken in parametrized coordinates, the correct equation is

\[
f|_C=\psi' C\bigl((a')^{-1}ga\bigr)\psi^{-1}.
\]

With `psi_bar=psi C(a^{-1})` and the analogous target normalization this becomes `f|C=psi_bar' C(g) psi_bar^{-1}`. Normalization then gives an equivalence with the normalized category, and all categorical deductions survive. However the raw fixed-cone space of parametrizations remains equivalent to `Homeo_def(Y)`, so even this coordinate interpretation does not make its contractibility assertion true. The proposed explicit pointwise definition is therefore the coherent correction of both issues. This correction is made after target access and is not attributed to the frozen statement packet.

## Empty boundary: packet omission, already resolved in the released paper

The independent freeze correctly needed a convention for empty boundary, but the target comparison resolves it without a manuscript repair. Live lines 219–220 explicitly define boundary collapse as `X cup_B {*} `, adjoining an isolated point when B is empty. Lines 1014–1017 repeat the precise convention

\[
X/B=(X\amalg\{o\})/(B\sim o),\qquad X/\varnothing=X\amalg\{o\}.
\]

The immutable A.109 proof also states it. Together with `C emptyset={o}`, this makes the empty collar and isolated vertex cases correct in A.101, A.105, A.108 and A.109. The issue is a missing statement-packet convention, not a failure of the released theorem.

### A.102: full family and outer-edge check

The source first chooses one positive epsilon valid for the entire compact parameter family, not one epsilon for each fibre. The inverse image of the reference collar interior is open in the total domain and contains its compact bottom face; definable compactness gives the uniform positive separation. Therefore `k_p=c^{-1}d_{p,epsilon}` is a total collar family inside the reference cylinder, missing its top.

For the source's `G_{p,s}` formula the negative-strip and small-collar clauses agree at u=0, since `k_p(b,0)=(b,0)`. The small-collar clause agrees with the identity on its outer face v=1. The two nontrivial images have radial intervals `[0,s/(1+s)]` and `[s/(1+s),1]`; they partition the small collar. This verifies both bijectivity and the claimed inverse by the same finite radial reparametrizations. The open-interior assumption on total spaces ensures continuity at the changing collar boundary; definable compactness also gives total inverse continuity.

At s=1, composing with `F_s` really gives `K_{p,1}(b,t)=k_p(b,t)` for t at most one half. Gluing by the identity outside the reference collar is justified because its top is fixed. The two shrinking/expanding paths retain strict extensions, and ambient homeomorphisms preserve such extensions. Thus this proof handles finite compact parameter families, not merely individual collars. Its use of Armstrong is a formula comparison: Armstrong's printed theorem is about manifold collars, whereas the finite formula itself has no manifold step and the manuscript checks it directly in its more general setting.

The frozen proof is independently stronger at the level of displayed construction: it provides one natural contraction with a stationary reference collar and a terminal interval on which an entire exterior extension is constant. That terminal interval permits exact extension from closed parameter subpolyhedra using a neighborhood retraction and cutoff. The target's claim of contractibility does not require a chosen global contraction on an infinite-dimensional mapping space, and no such definable finite-dimensional space is asserted. The two proofs establish the same simplicial assertion by different constructions.

### A.106: parametrizable quotient, not an unspecified topology of images

Precomposition by `P_def(B)` preserves strictness. An outer extension of the original collar is glued to the cylinder map's top boundary map; the top is preserved setwise by the definition of this group. Two total parametrizations of the same collar and outer face have a unique transition map in that group. Thus the setwise quotient in each simplicial degree is exact.

The target's horn filler is valid: a finite simplicial horn is a PL retract of the simplex, so a jointly continuous definable family on the horn pulls back to one on the full simplex. The total inverse and open-interior conditions are preserved by pullback. For the quotient lifting problem, a chosen representative over the full simplex differs from each prescribed horn lift by compatible group elements; filling that group horn adjusts the representative. The free-action argument in the freeze proves the same conclusion without requiring this fibration presentation. Neither argument replaces the stipulated parametrizable-family quotient by a Hausdorff or compact-open quotient on a raw collection of subsets.

### A.107 and A.108: general pairs, noncompact finite atlases, and isolated points

Brown's local-collar argument starts with arbitrary metric spaces, not with a manifold condition; the manifold applications come later. Its finite gluing part uses distance functions, closed shrinkings, spindle heights, finite pasting, and the inverse of an explicit affine collapse away from its collapsed lower region. These steps have definable versions over any real closed field. In particular, none of Lemmas 1–4 invokes a countable limiting composition. A finite atlas needs only finite induction on Lemma 4. The independent exterior-pushing proof separately handles the danger that a constant support height on a noncompact chart accumulates at its frontier; its variable radius and closed support inside the bicollar prevent that failure.

For compact X and B, restricting a global open collar to a closed interval strictly below its terminal parameter supplies both a closed embedded image and a further positive exterior extension. The target explicitly retains both restrictions. For conical neighborhoods, the global barycentric coordinate of the marked triangulation vertex is continuous even off the closed star, since it is zero there. A positive truncated level therefore has open cone interior and an exterior collar from a second positive level. This does not assert that the whole closed star, or a whole closed cone with no external shell, is strict.

### Intrinsic outer boundary in the torus examples

The source's phrase “intrinsic boundaries of their manifold loci” is used only for the torus examples. On `T^6 × I`, a definable pair homeomorphism preserves the manifold boundary; preserving the designated bottom setwise then preserves the other end. On `CT^6` with its vertex fixed, the punctured cone is `T^6 × (0,1]`, whose top face is its manifold boundary. This gives the restriction maps used in A.104, A.111 and A.112.

Over a non-Archimedean real closed field the invariant behind this statement is definable local homology, computed from finite triangulations, not ordinary singular homology of the order topology. Interior manifold points have nonzero top local homology over F2 and boundary points have zero top local homology. This is preserved by definable homeomorphisms and by coefficient extension of the finite polyhedral model. The freeze explicitly supplies this interpretation. No comparable intrinsic-boundary claim is made for arbitrary singular links; the general-category equivalences retain their chosen outer-face data instead.

## Corrected torus input chain and what was actually inspected

### Mathematical deduction

For a compact smooth d-torus with d at least six, its second homotopy group is zero. Igusa's corrected exact sequence therefore has only the F2-valued second obstruction between its K3 correction and its second Whitehead group. To apply it one must eliminate the correction, not quote the old split formula.

Put `R_d=Z[t_1^{±1},...,t_d^{±1}]`. The Laurent fundamental theorem splits `K_3(R_d)` into constant K3, one-coordinate K2 classes, two-coordinate K1 classes, and three-coordinate K0 classes. The splittings are multiplication by the coordinate unit classes. Since `K_0(Z)=Z`, `K_1(Z)=Z/2`, and `K_2(Z)=Z/2`, and the unit from stable homotopy sends eta to minus one and eta squared to the nonzero Steinberg symbol, framed coordinate circles, two-tori and three-tori hit all nonconstant summands. The constant summand is removed in the reduced Whitehead term. Hence the reduced `Wh_3(Z^d)` vanishes, or equivalently the reduced Grassmann correction on all of K3 vanishes.

The same Laurent decomposition in degree two is generated by the constant symbol `{-1,-1}`, mixed symbols `{-1,t_i}`, and symbols `{t_i,t_j}`. These are killed in the second Whitehead quotient by signed monomial matrices. Hatcher–Wagoner also explicitly record vanishing for free abelian groups. Thus `Wh_2(Z^d)=0`. Conjugation on the group ring is trivial, and the remaining second obstruction is exactly `F2[Z^d]/F2{1}` as an additive group. This proves the whole displayed component group, not just one nonzero element.

Burghelea–Lashof supply the unstable component comparison from smooth to PL concordances and the PL–TOP comparison in the safe range d greater than five. No stable-range theorem for high pseudoisotopy homotopy groups is being substituted for a statement about components. The all-field definable step in the independent reconstruction uses only the already accepted A.115 coefficient-extension zigzag and A.160 jointly PL-to-jointly-definable comparison. Their proofs were not part of this fresh task. The live torus proof uses Shiota component statements instead; the permitted stronger interfaces independently justify the same conclusion and do not require a direct map from non-Archimedean families to real TOP families.

## The eleven arrows and exact repaired scope

For the remainder of this section, cone parametrizations restrict to the identity on the actual outer face. This is the proposed corrected convention, with the raw-category qualifications already recorded above.

| Arrow | Exact mapping/object argument | Result |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Top PB to PBCR, attach a collar | Restrict to the exterior or uniquely extend by the product boundary map; trimming recovers a compact bordant pair. | Equivalence; actual mapping simplicial isomorphisms. |
| Top PB to EPCR, attach a cone | Restrict to the exterior or uniquely cone its boundary map; strict exterior collar makes trimming bordant. | Equivalence; actual mapping simplicial isomorphisms. |
| Top PB to bottom PB | Identity. | Equivalence. |
| Top PB to EP, collapse | At `(T^6×I,T^6×0)`, upper restriction has kernel `P_def(T^6)` and the cone restriction kernel is Alexander-contractible. | Failure; mapping homotopy fibre `P_def(T^6)`. |
| PBCR to EPCR, collapse | Product-preserving maps descend uniquely and radial maps extend uniquely over blow-up; strict outer collar retained. | Equivalence. |
| PBCR to PBC | On the split torus cylinder, rigid maps give the section of `P_def(T^6) ⋊ G_outer`. | Failure on components. |
| EPCR to EPC | Replace inner map by cone of its actual boundary map and contract the pointwise-base discrepancy. | Equivalence on every mapping space and essentially surjective. |
| PBC to EPC | Outer shell is unchanged; lower-cylinder kernel `P_def(T^6)` becomes a contractible cone kernel. | Failure; mapping homotopy fibre `P_def(T^6)`. |
| PBC to PB | Compress bottom-fixed maps into the upper half, showing rigid-to-all inclusion is a homotopy equivalence; two-out-of-three with the failed rigid section. | Failure in the torus cylinder. |
| EPC to EP | Proper subcone stabilizer has kernel equivalent to `P_def(T^6)` over the outer-boundary homeomorphism group; target kernel contracts. | Failure; the connected derived choice component is `BP_def(T^6)`. |
| Bottom PB to EP, collapse | Same cylinder calculation as the upper collapse copy. | Failure. |

The compression used for the ninth arrow is continuous on the whole compact parameter family, including compression parameter zero: the lower seam is fixed pointwise and the rescaling maps and their inverses are continuous. It preserves the rigid subgroup throughout when started there. Thus it supplies a genuine homotopy equivalence of the relevant simplicial spaces, not a claim that an arbitrary ambient collar-preserving group is globally contractible.

For the derived cone-choice calculation, the chosen cone is a proper radial subcone in a larger closed cone. Restriction to the unchanged outer base has a section. The inclusion of the stabilizer surjects on pi0 of the full pointed automorphism group, so its classifying-space homotopy fibre is connected and identifies the stated component. The groupoid-component identification uses actual homeomorphisms; no parametrized isotopy-extension theorem for a separate topology of subsets is assumed.

Finite collections of pairwise disjoint compact labeled pieces are handled by applying the same finite formulas separately, with disjoint exterior margins. Keeping every other piece fixed retains the torus obstruction whenever one such piece is allowed. Nothing here asserts failure at every bordant pair or for every link.

## Completion verdict before staged page QA

The original frozen mathematical reconstruction and the target proofs agree on the full strict-family, compactness, parameter, and torus scopes, subject to the explicit cone-base repair. The empty-boundary concern is resolved by an existing live convention. The fixed-base concern is a real literal defect affecting the fixed-cone claim and, under the actual-boundary interpretation, the raw rigid categories. It cannot be dismissed as an omitted proof step. The normalized definition and the stronger raw-parametrization product statement correct it without discarding the unparametrized objects or the advertised torus obstructions.

The classical torus chain now has direct post-freeze inspection of the actual 1984 correction as well as the exact 2025 and K-theory locators. The remaining inputs explicitly used as accepted foundations are A.115, A.160, definable triangulation and local homology, definable finite normal shrinking, and elementary simplicial/group-classifying-space facts. This comparison is not a fresh proof of those accepted interfaces or of the entire pseudoisotopy theory.

## Staged repair and PDF review

The parent staged the normalized definition, explicit pointwise clauses, a split product proof for arbitrary fixed-cone parametrizations, and the 1984 primary citation in `tmp/paper02_collar_normalization`. The worker read the entire TeX diff. After parent authorization, the worker changed only the final explanatory sentence to: “Its morphisms are not required to fix the outer faces pointwise.” This avoids asserting that every abstract outer-face homeomorphism extends to a whole ambient object.

The first plain `pdflatex` attempt failed because the local format file was not on the TeX search path. That failed attempt is preserved in `worker_wording_build_1.txt`. The parent supplied workspace-only TeX runtime overrides. Three subsequent `pdflatex -interaction=nonstopmode -halt-on-error main.tex` passes all exited zero; `worker_wording_build_record.json` records the exact environment and commands. The final log has no overfull/underfull boxes, undefined or multiply-defined references, or rerun warnings. The portable build instructions were not changed.

The final stage remains 85 pages. Its source SHA256 is `8162c1afd4a2ffc76bfed338af631a553e242d1ee75ced3fd4aec2d8a752b25f`; its PDF SHA256 is `12b517678140b8c80ee8be0f5581dbd15a1935e96b90ac45b0cf1a4618c328eb`. Independent pixel comparison at rendering matrix 1.25 found changes on exactly pages 2, 3, 9–73, 84 and 85 (69 pages), while the other 16 pages are pixel-identical to the baseline. This rendering check is a layout/content-preservation check, not evidence for the mathematical theorems.

The worker actually viewed all 18 contact sheets in `qa_final_worker`, covering every changed page, and additionally viewed individual pages 9, 10 and 84 at full rendered size. The new definition, pointwise clauses, fixed-cone product proof, and 1984 citation were checked for mathematical meaning and reader clarity. All affected headings remain attached to substantive following text; no clipped formulas, missing references, overlapping text, or other visible layout defect was found. The bibliography prints the 1984 source on page 84 with pages 104–172, and the torus proof cites Theorem 8.a.2 on pages 149–150. The raw-parametrization distinction is now explained in the paper rather than silently normalized away.

The full stage review is separately recorded in `paper02_collar_normalization_worker_review.md`. No live manuscript/PDF or global acceptance ledger was modified by this worker. The corrected staged pair is ready for the parent's coordinated release; the mathematical and visual acceptance reported here applies to the exact staged hashes above.

# Complete post-freeze comparison: B.064 and B.082

## 1. Verdict and exact scope

The independent frozen reconstruction proves every statement clause of
B.064 and B.082 under the exact stated hypotheses. Comparison with the
complete immutable proofs and the released manuscript found no mathematical
counterexample, omitted necessary hypothesis, incorrect total-degree index,
or substantive sign error in those target theorems.

The two total-differential conventions are explicitly isomorphic. After
transport, the frozen and source transgressions are equal, not merely equal
up to sign. Their displayed surjections from the tensor-map kernel to
\(K_1\) differ by the natural automorphism \(-1\) of \(K_1\). This has no
effect on the kernel extension, its vanishing criterion, or either
existence test.

The frozen proof contains one transcription typo in the display of
\(D^2\), identified by the parent reviewer after the freeze. It is recorded
in Section 4 below without changing the frozen file. It is not a missing
argument or a change in the differential.

The audited mathematical scope is:

* every clause of B.064 and B.082, including their complete proofs;
* the corresponding live Theorems 9.1 and 1.9 and their full proofs;
* the live flat specialization, Corollary 9.4;
* the source's flat connecting-map comparison with B.080;
* the source-only checks B82.20--B82.24, including the precise portions
  of B.067, B.079, B77.4, and B78.7 that they use;
* the hypotheses and content of the primary Stacks results actually
  cited for these arguments.

The neighboring Noetherian-domain classification, Theorem 1.10 and Part II,
is not a premise of B.064 or B.082 and is outside this comparison. In
particular its torsion-free and Noetherian assumptions have not been
silently imported into the universal criterion. The contextual references
to Elliott, Hua, and recent domain examples are not mathematical inputs
to the two target proofs. No claim about bibliographic priority is made.
The numerical certificates mentioned in the auxiliary forest construction
were not rerun and are not needed for any assertion checked here.

No live manuscript, PDF, immutable source, or global record was edited.
All supplements first developed after source access are contained in this
comparison note.

## 4. Frozen-note transcription erratum

In Section 4 of the frozen proof, the display immediately following
(4.2) contains the literal expression

    a,du-d(au-dv)

in its second component. The comma is a transcription error. The
component is

\[
 a(du)-d(au-dv).
\]

Thus the full calculation is

\[
 D^2(u,v,w)
  =\bigl(0,\ a(du)-d(au-dv),\
             b(au-dv)+d(bv+dw)\bigr)=0.
\]

The defining formula (4.2), the subsequent cycle equations, every
transgression formula, and the exactness calculations already use this
correct composition. The frozen bytes and their original hash remain
unchanged. This post-freeze erratum is confined to this comparison.
