| C is a subring | Section 1.3 | Degree alignment gives sums; products give products when nonzero; zero is handled explicitly |
| Multivariable coefficients belong to C | Section 1.4 | Base-N exponent encoding is an explicit version of the source's separated powers |
| Binary tensor injectivity forces R to C epic | Sections 2.1--2.2 | The tensor (cp) tensor p minus p tensor (cp), followed by top-coefficient extraction, gives c tensor 1 equals 1 tensor c |
| C=R gives every ordinary polynomial tensor isomorphism | Section 1.4 | The multivariable coefficient assertion identifies every target module with R in the corresponding variables |

The source and live proof use the phrase “Cramer's rule.” Its valid
interpretation here is the adjugate identity, not division by the
Vandermonde determinant. The independent proof makes that distinction
explicit. There is no counterexample arising from zero divisors.

For \(c=0\), a leading coefficient is included by the statement's
express convention. If \(cc'=0\), closure under products therefore
does not require the degree of a product to be the sum of the degrees.
The independent proof also treats the zero ring separately. These are
clarifications of the arbitrary-ring proof, not extra hypotheses or
repairs to its conclusion.

## 6. Clause-by-clause audit of B.082

### 6.1. Definitions, the arithmetic test, and the degree-zero reduction

| Source clause | Independent proof | Finding |
| --- | --- | --- |
| C and F are formed from the original inclusion | Sections 1 and 3 | Exact agreement |
| E consists of the indicated evaluation-modulo-R functions | Section 3 | It is treated only as an R-module; no unjustified C-module structure is used |
| B82.2 is equivalent to epicness | Section 2.3 | A complete independent finite-relation proof is given |
| Failure of B82.2 precludes uniqueness | Section 2.2 | A nonzero tensor-kernel witness is detected by coefficient extraction |
| U, V, W and a, b have the prescribed order and signs | Section 4 | Both models use a=(1 tensor e,e tensor 1) and b=(e tensor 1,-1 tensor e) |
| ba=0 | Section 4 | It holds strictly on the complexes |
| Z0 equals C[X,Y] | Section 3 | Epicness makes C tensor C=C, and right exactness makes V0=W0=0 |

The source matrix proof uses a single common finite index set, removes
its distinguished row and column, and then appends a one-by-one block.
The independent proof instead keeps the first-factor relation
coefficients as a separate row, removes only the column corresponding
to 1, negates that row, and pads a rectangular matrix to a square one.
Both are valid finite presentations of the same tensor equality.

In particular the source's calculation

\[
 Y'M'Z'=m_{00}-c
\]

is correct: \(M'Z'\) has entries \(-m_{i0}\), while removal of the first
row changes the other coordinate equations only by entries in R.
The block diagonal matrix with blocks \(-M'\) and \(m_{00}\) produces
c exactly.

The source's use of labels for \(s_0=1,s_1=c\) is harmless even when
values repeat: one may use an indexed generating family with repeated
values, as the frozen proof does explicitly. Alternatively \(c\in R\)
has its immediate one-by-one certificate.

### 6.2. Transgressions, exact sequences, and the final criterion

| Source clause | Independent proof | Finding |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Existence and linearity of delta0 and delta1 | Section 6 | Explicit representatives and lifts are given |
| Independence of representatives and finite lifts | Section 6 | The difference is an image of the appropriate b map |
| Independence of free resolutions and the lift of epsilon | Sections 5.4--5.5 | Strict simultaneous comparisons and compatible homotopies are explicitly constructed |
| H0 of the total complex is A tensor A, with the correct edge map | Section 5 | Proved by an adapted-resolution row reduction |
| Negative total homology vanishes | Section 5.3 | Proved by explicit cycle elimination |
| Image theta equals kernel delta0 | Section 7.1 | Both inclusions are proved by lifts |
| Delta0 is surjective and coker theta equals T1 | Section 7.2 | A W1 cycle has total degree -1 and bounds |
| Exact sequence 0 to K2 to ker theta to K1 to 0 | Sections 7.3--7.5 | Every map and its kernel are calculated explicitly |
| Uniqueness is equivalent to K1=K2=0 after the matrix test | Section 8 | Immediate from the proved exact extension, in both directions |
| The two arrays are exactly f(X+Y) and f(XY) | Section 8 | The binomial theorem over a commutative ring is sufficient |
| Existence is exactly the two delta0 vanishing conditions | Section 8 | It follows from image theta=kernel delta0 |
| No tensor power of A is needed to define or implement the invariants | Section 9 | Only F,E,epsilon and their free relations are used |
| Homological depth at most three | Section 9 | Incoming degree-three boundaries for degree-two homology are explicitly included |
| No uniform terminating algorithm and no binary-to-plethory inference | Sections 8--10 | Both limitations are preserved exactly |

The live statement adds the complete chain formula for the canonical
maps directly on page 6. This makes explicit a definition located in
the proof of the immutable statement. It does not change the theorem.
Sections 7--9 below verify that formula against the independent one.

## 7. Complete sign and total-degree comparison

Use the same vertical complexes in both models, writing their
horizontal maps as \(a=h_0\) and \(b=h_1\).

The frozen proof puts \(\mathcal T_{p,q}\) in total degree \(q-p\) and
uses

\[
 D_{\mathrm f}=(-1)^p d+h.
\]

The immutable source and live release use the same total degree and

\[
 D_{\mathrm s}=d+(-1)^q h.
\]

Define

\[
 J_{p,q}=(-1)^{pq}\operatorname{id}_{\mathcal T_{p,q}}.
 \tag{C.1}
\]

Then \(J D_{\mathrm f}=D_{\mathrm s}J\). On a vertical arrow, which
lowers q, both sides have scalar \((-1)^{pq}\); on a horizontal arrow,
which increases p, both sides have scalar \((-1)^{pq+q}\).
This verifies the chain isomorphism on every component.

For total degree n the three coordinates have vertical degrees

\[
 n,\quad n+1,\quad n+2.
\]

Consequently the source differential, written on those coordinates,
is

\[
 D_{\mathrm s}(x_0,x_1,x_2)
   =\bigl(dx_0,\
           (-1)^n ax_0+dx_1,\
           (-1)^{n+1}bx_1+dx_2\bigr).
 \tag{C.2}
\]

This gives the following exact index checks:

| Use | Total degree | Coordinates and equations |
| --- | ---: | --- |
| A cycle representing A tensor A | 0 | x0 in T00, x1 in T11, x2 in T22; dx1=-a x0 and dx2=b x1 |
| A representative of a T1 cokernel class | -1 | A pure vertical cycle in T21 |
| A primitive witnessing a relation on a pure T22 cycle | 1 | z in T01, w in T12, t in T23; dz=0, dw=a z, and the last component is b w+dt |

Thus B82.15, B82.18, and the live equations (66) and (69) have the
correct indices and signs. In particular the degree-three term is
\(\mathcal T_{2,3}\) in a total degree-one primitive; it is not
another Tor group that could simply be omitted.

### 7.1. Both transgressions agree exactly

The frozen definition chooses \(v\) with \(dv=az\) and sets
\(\delta_q[z]=[bv]\). The source chooses \(w\) with
\(dw=(-1)^{q+1}az\) and sets
\(\delta_q[z]=[(-1)^{q+1}bw]\). Choose

\[
 w=(-1)^{q+1}v.
\]

Then the source value is

\[
 [(-1)^{q+1}b((-1)^{q+1}v)]=[bv].
\]

This is also precisely the action of J on the lift in column 1,
vertical degree q+1. Column 0 and column 2 are unchanged by J.
Therefore both \(\delta_0\) and \(\delta_1\), their kernels, and
\(K_2=T_2/\operatorname{im}\delta_1\) agree with their frozen
counterparts under the ordinary Tor identifications.

### 7.2. The only residual quotient-map sign

In total degree zero, J sends

\[
 (u,v,w)\longmapsto(u,-v,w).
\]

Thus the map to \(K_1\) obtained by taking the normalized middle
coordinate is the negative of the frozen map \(\kappa\). The map
from \(K_2\) represented by a pure last-coordinate cycle is unchanged,
as are the edge map theta and the map delta0.

The two short exact sequences therefore agree with the identity on
\(K_2\) and on \(\ker\theta\), and with \(-1\) on \(K_1\).
This is an explicit convention change, not a discrepancy in exactness
or canonicity. In characteristic two even this quotient-map sign is
the identity.

## 8. Audit of the source's alternative total-complex proof

The frozen proof uses an adapted resolution, exact horizontal rows,
and explicit elimination of the final two columns. The source instead
uses the two-column complex \(L\) of \(P\to Q\).
Its degree formula

\[
 L_n=P_n\oplus Q_{n+1}
\]

is correct, because the Q column contributes q-1 to total degree.
The evaluation sequence makes \(H_0(L)=A\) and all other homology
zero. Since \(\varepsilon:F\to E\) is surjective, the map
\(L_0\to L_{-1}=Q_0\) is surjective. Q0 is free, so this map splits.
Removing the corresponding contractible pair leaves a nonnegative
projective resolution of A; its degree-zero term is projective and
its higher terms are free. Tensoring the decomposition with itself
is legitimate: tensoring any contractible complex with another
complex remains contractible by its tensor homotopy.

The source's second sign map, from \(L\otimes L\) to its three-column
model, is also correct. For first-factor column p1, first vertical
degree a, and second vertical degree b, its multiplier is
\((-1)^{p_1b}\). The four kinds of differential have the following
signs after transport:

| Differential | Transported sign |
| --- | --- |
| First vertical differential | 1 |
| Second vertical differential | (-1)^a |
| First horizontal differential | (-1)^(a+b) |
| Second horizontal differential | (-1)^(a+b-p1) |

The last sign equals \((-1)^{a+b}\) times the minus sign on the
second summand of h1 when p1=1. These are exactly the source's
vertical Koszul signs and horizontal signs. Thus its proof identifies
the entire total homology with \(\operatorname{Tor}_n(A,A)\) for
nonnegative n and with zero in negative degrees, as asserted.
The frozen proof only needs the degree-zero and negative assertions,
but its identification with \(K\otimes K\) also gives the positive
Tor identification by the same ordinary double-resolution argument.

Both identifications give the actual polynomial tensor map as edge
map: the augmentation of L, or the inclusion of K in P in the
adapted model, induces \(A\hookrightarrow F\). No arbitrary
automorphism of \(A\otimes A\) is being inserted at that step.

## 9. Choice independence: precise comparison of the proof obligations

The source and live proof fully check the two elementary choices
inside a fixed resolution:

1. Changing the lift by a vertical cycle changes the answer by
   \(\operatorname{im}b_{q+1}\).
2. Replacing z by z+ds allows the corresponding change of lift by
   \((-1)^{q+1}as\), and ba=0 removes its contribution.

These checks are correct. The statement also fixes a functorial
resolution, so its definition is unambiguous even before discussing
other choices of resolution.

The source's sentence about standard comparison maps is compressed:
independent arbitrary comparison maps for P and Q need not commute
strictly with the chosen lifts of epsilon. One must either account
for the intervening homotopies or choose a comparison target that
allows strict simultaneous lifting.

This issue was already resolved before target access in frozen
Sections 5.4--5.5. The proof there constructs a degreewise surjective
adapted target \(P'\to Q'\), with exact kernel resolution \(K'\).
For every n its map

\[
 P'_n\longrightarrow
 \{(x,z):dx=0,\ e'x=dz\},\qquad p\longmapsto(dp,e'p)
\]

is surjective: first lift z using e', then correct the remaining
cycle in K'. Freeness of the source Pn therefore gives strictly
commuting comparison maps \(e'f_P=f_Qe\).

The same lifting argument one degree higher gives simultaneous
homotopies with \(e'h_P=h_Qe\). The tensor homotopies

\[
 H_{XY}(x\otimes y)
  =h_Xx\otimes f_Yy+(-1)^{\deg x}g_Xx\otimes h_Yy
\]

commute with a and b. With the column multiplier \((-1)^p\), they
are homotopies of the frozen total complex. Conjugating by J gives
the corresponding statement in the source's convention.

This proves invariance under a change of either free resolution,
the chain lift of epsilon, the adapted target, or the comparison
maps. The exact-sequence maps also commute with these comparisons.
There is therefore no remaining choice-independence gap in the
combined independent verification. No post-source repair was needed:
the entire strict comparison construction is in the immutable
independent freeze.

For publication, expanding the source's compressed comparison
sentence by this lifting argument would make the proof more
self-contained. The theorem and the fixed-resolution definition
do not require a change.

## 10. Flat specialization and the B.080 connecting convention

When C is flat, F=C[X] is flat. Hence every positive Tor group having
an F factor vanishes, and the definitions give

\[
 K_1=0,\qquad K_2=W_2=\operatorname{Tor}_2(E,E),\qquad
 T_1=W_1=\operatorname{Tor}_1(E,E).
\]

This proves the live Corollary 9.4 directly from the universal theorem.
No flatness of A is needed.

There are two names for the final map in the compared documents:

* Source B.080 defines \(\partial_{80}\) using its long-exact-sequence
  convention and B80.9.
* Live Corollary 9.4 expressly defines
  \(\partial_{\mathrm{live}}=\delta_0\), by the chain formula (63).

Under the source's usual double-resolution augmentation,

\[
 \delta_0=-\partial_{80},\qquad
 \partial_{\mathrm{live}}=-\partial_{80}.
 \tag{C.3}
\]

Here is the chain check. For this comparison only, use the flat
resolution of E with degree-zero module F and positive degrees
resolving A. Represent \(H\in F\otimes F\) in degree zero.
In the source convention, choose
\(w=(w_1,w_2)\) with

\[
 dw_1=-H,\qquad dw_2=-H,
\]

where \(w_2\in Q_1\otimes F\). The cycle representing delta0 is
\(-h_1w\). Under augmentation of the second Q factor to E, the
first component disappears and this cycle becomes

\[
 (1\otimes\varepsilon)w_2\in Q_1\otimes E.
\]

Lift it back using w2. Its differential is -H. The usual connecting
map \(\tau:\operatorname{Tor}_1(E,E)\to E\otimes A\) consequently
takes delta0(H) to the negative of the element obtained from H in
\(F\otimes A\) and then mapped to \(E\otimes A\).
That latter element is exactly the argument of \(\tau^{-1}\)
in B80.9. This proves (C.3).

The first map of the flat exact sequence is likewise interpreted
with the declared tensor-order convention for the two connecting
maps. One cannot compare it by ignoring the interchange of the
two resolved factors. To check it, write \(Q_0=F\) and
\(Q_n=G_{n-1}\) for \(n\geq1\), where G resolves A with augmentation
\(\alpha:G_0\to A\). A cycle in \((Q\otimes Q)_2\) has components
\(t_{20},t_{11},t_{02}\). Its equations are

\[
 (d_Q\otimes1)t_{20}=(1\otimes i\alpha)t_{11},\qquad
 (i\alpha\otimes1)t_{11}+(1\otimes d_G)t_{02}=0.
\]

After augmenting the second Q factor, the first connecting map
represents its image in \(\operatorname{Tor}_1(E,A)\) by
\((1\otimes\alpha)t_{11}\). To apply the next connecting map using
a resolution of the fixed module A, the pair
\((t_{11},t_{02})\) is the corresponding cycle in \(Q\otimes G\).
Its other augmentation, followed by the ordinary interchange of
the module and complex factors, is represented by \(t_{02}\) in
\(G_1\otimes E\). Lifting to \(G_1\otimes F\), the second displayed
equation gives boundary \(-(\alpha\otimes\alpha)t_{11}\), after
putting the two A factors back in their prescribed order.

The total-complex injection gives the same value. The component
with two \(Q_1\) factors acquires a minus sign under the source's
\((-1)^{p_1b}\) isomorphism. In the split resolution L, the
augmentation-compatible projection of each such factor to \(G_0\)
has a minus sign; their product is plus. The net value is therefore
again \(-(\alpha\otimes\alpha)t_{11}\). The other two components
contain an \(L_{-1}\) factor and project to zero.

Thus the source's reduction to B.080 is consistent, including its
initial Tor2 map with the stated resolution-and-connecting
convention. The change of sign at its final map does not signal a
different obstruction.

The extra B.080 distinction between the binary test and full
bivariate compositeness is also valid: evaluation on \(R^2\)
vanishes on the tensor image, so it factors through the cokernel
\(\operatorname{Tor}_1(E,E)\). Its kernel gives the extra existence
obstruction for all integer-valued bivariate polynomials. Neither
the source nor the live universal theorem identifies that stronger
condition with the two specified binary substitutions.

## 11. Source-only nonflat positive check: B82.20--B82.23

These checks are not additional hypotheses of B.082 and are not part
of the statement-only packet. They were examined after the freeze.

### 11.1. Exact dependency on B.067

The forest construction has

\[
 S=\mathbb F_2[x_v,y_v:v]/
   (x_v^2,y_v^2,\ y_v-x_vy_{v0}y_{v1}:v),
 \qquad R=\bigoplus_{d\leq0}S_d,
\]

where \(\deg x_v=-1,\deg y_v=1\). Its finite-stage maps replace a
boundary y by a product of three distinct variables from disjoint
new sets. They send distinct squarefree monomials to distinct
squarefree monomials, proving injectivity of the direct system.
Hence each yv remains nonzero and the inclusion is proper.

Both augmentation ideals consist of square-zero elements; both
spectra have one point and residue field \(\mathbb F_2\).
Products from arbitrarily many fresh tree components give nonzero
powers of the maximal ideal N of R. A finitely generated ideal of
square-zero elements is nilpotent, so R is not Noetherian.

The displayed tensor shuffle for yv is valid because
\(x_vy_{v0},x_vy_{v1}\in R\). Together with the generators xv in R,
it proves \(S\otimes_R S=S\).

For the conductor assertion \((R:_S N^k)=R\), a nonzero
positive-degree part of c involves only finitely many components.
Multiplying by a product of k degree-zero monomials from fresh
components preserves its nonvanishing and its positive degree.
The tensor-product decomposition over the field \(\mathbb F_2\)
justifies this nonvanishing even though the rings have zero divisors.

With \(Q=X^4+X^2\), every R-value is a root of Q. Monic division
reduces integer-valuedness to a degree-less-than-four remainder.
Evaluations and first derivatives at 0 and 1 belong to R exactly
when that remainder preserves R; the derivative implication uses
the conductor calculation. The Hermite matrix is invertible over
\(\mathbb F_2\). Therefore

\[
 A=R[X]_{<4}\oplus Q S[X].
\]

This verifies exactly the dependency used to obtain B82.20. The
source's multivariable extension uses finite differences, the same
conductor calculation for \(N^{|J|}\), and tensor products of the
Hermite matrix; these steps are also valid. Thus the positive
polynomial tensor assertions do not presume flatness.

Finally, if S were flat over R, the surjection of the one-point
spectra would make it faithfully flat, and the already proved
epimorphism would be an isomorphism. This contradicts the nonzero
positive-degree elements. Hence S is nonflat, as advertised.

### 11.2. The Tor computation itself

Set \(D=S/R\) and \(M_q=\operatorname{Tor}_q(S,S)\).
The monic basis in B82.20 gives E as four copies of D, with epsilon
the quotient on those four positions and zero elsewhere.

The long exact sequences for \(0\to R\to S\to D\to0\), using
the freeness of R and the isomorphism \(S\otimes S=S\), give

\[
 M_q\simeq\operatorname{Tor}_q(S,D)
       \simeq\operatorname{Tor}_q(D,S)\quad(q\geq1).
\]

They further give

\[
 0\longrightarrow M_1\longrightarrow
 \operatorname{Tor}_1(D,D)\longrightarrow D\longrightarrow0
\]

and isomorphisms \(M_q\simeq\operatorname{Tor}_q(D,D)\) for \(q\geq2\).
The maps from the two mixed groups to \(\operatorname{Tor}_1(D,D)\)
agree on M1 by the natural square induced by the two quotient maps.

For a pair of low basis indices, a1 is the diagonal and b1 is the
difference followed by the displayed injection. Its middle homology
is zero and its last cokernel is D. For exactly one low index, a1
is an isomorphism onto the sole mixed summand and W1 is zero.
For two high indices, both V1 and W1 vanish. The map b2 is
surjective on every pair that has a W2 term. Thus

\[
 K_1=K_2=0,\qquad T_1\simeq D^{16}.
\]

No positive Tor group \(M_q\) has been assumed to vanish.
The explicit identities for Q(X+Y) and Q(XY) are correct in
characteristic two and put both arrays for QG into the ideal
generated by Q(X),Q(Y). Their low-low remainder is zero.
The arrays for ordinary polynomials have low-low coefficients in R.
Both delta0 conditions therefore hold. This confirms every
mathematical assertion in B82.20--B82.23.

## 12. Source-only flat negative check: B82.24

Here \(R=k+tV\), \(V=l[[t]]\), \(K=l((t))\),
\(k=\mathbb F_q\subset l\), and l is infinite. These meet the
hypotheses actually used for the monic coefficient formula B77.4;
the B78.7 formulation gives the same formula.

For \(P=X^q-X\), the basis \(X^iP^j\), \(i<q\), is monic.
The exact integer-valued module is

\[
 A=\bigoplus_{i<q}RX^i
   \ \oplus\
   \bigoplus_{\substack{i<q\\j\geq1}}
               t^{1-j}V X^iP^j.
 \tag{C.4}
\]

For clarity, its necessity can be checked just in this discrete
valuation case. Subtract the degree-less-than-q interpolation
polynomial on k. For a remaining finite expansion, put
\(\delta=\min(v(a_{ij})+j)\) and substitute \(X=b+tz\).
After dividing by a coefficient of valuation delta, the residue is
a polynomial in z with zero constant term. Some choice \(b\in k\)
makes it nonzero, by the invertible q-point Vandermonde matrix.
If delta is negative, a residue avoiding its roots gives a value
outside V. If delta is zero, a nonconstant polynomial on the
infinite field l would take values in a finite scalar multiple of k,
which is impossible. Thus delta is at least one, giving every
coefficient condition \(a_{ij}\in t^{1-j}V\).
Sufficiency follows from \(P(R)\subset tV\).

The polynomials \(t^{-n}vP^{n+1}\) for \(v\in V\) show that C=K.
Also K is the fraction field and a localization of R: for
\(x\in K\), a sufficiently high power of t makes both the
denominator and its product with x lie in R. Thus C is flat and epic.

Taking F/A in (C.4) proves B82.24 exactly. In particular
\(N=K/V\) occurs as a direct summand of E, at i=0,j=1.
For \(\beta\in l\setminus k\),

\[
 \xi=\beta\otimes1-1\otimes\beta\in V\otimes_R V
\]

is nonzero: reduction gives the same nonzero expression in
\(l\otimes_k l\), detected by a k-linear functional that is zero
on 1 and one on beta. It maps to zero in \(K\otimes_R V=K\).

The two exact sequences for \(0\to V\to K\to N\to0\), with K flat,
identify

\[
 \operatorname{Tor}_2(N,N)
   \simeq\operatorname{Tor}_1(N,V)
   \simeq\ker(V\otimes_R V\to K\otimes_R V).
\]

The final isomorphism is onto the indicated kernel. Therefore xi
has a unique nonzero preimage. The split maps between N and E
make its image nonzero in \(\operatorname{Tor}_2(E,E)=K_2\).
The coefficient projection onto the P summand identifies the
corresponding tensor-kernel obstruction, with the stated connecting
convention, with

\[
 \beta P\otimes P-P\otimes\beta P.
\]

Its nonvanishing and its zero polynomial image also follow directly
from (C.4). This checks B82.24 and the source's conclusion that the
flat cusp is rejected at uniqueness. No Noetherian hypothesis on
this cusp is used.

## 13. Primary dependency audit

The following primary sources were opened after the independent
freeze, on 2026-11-23. They are HTML theorem pages; they have stable
tag and lemma locators rather than PDF page numbers.

| Primary source | Exact hypotheses and content used | Fit to the target |
| --- | --- | --- |
| [Stacks Lemma 10.107.11, tag 04VY](https://stacks.math.columbia.edu/tag/04VY) | An arbitrary ring map and an element of its target; tensor equality is equivalent to a finite coefficient matrix certificate with row and column products in the image of the base | Applies to the inclusion R to C with no extra assumptions |
| [Stacks Remark 10.107.12, tag 04VZ](https://stacks.math.columbia.edu/tag/04VZ) | Restates that certificate as YXZ with YX and XZ over the base image | Exactly the form of B82.2 |
| [Stacks Lemma 10.75.5, tag 00M3](https://stacks.math.columbia.edu/tag/00M3) | Arbitrary modules over a ring; its proof uses the free double resolution and functorial comparison | Supports the vertical Tor identifications without finiteness assumptions |
| [Stacks Lemma 10.75.2, tag 00M0](https://stacks.math.columbia.edu/tag/00M0) | A module and a short exact sequence of modules; long exact Tor sequence | Applies to every evaluation and coefficient sequence used here |
| [Stacks Lemma 10.75.8, tag 00M5](https://stacks.math.columbia.edu/tag/00M5) | A module is flat exactly when its positive Tor functors vanish | Used only when flatness is actually assumed or established |
| [Stacks Lemma 10.39.16, tag 00HQ](https://stacks.math.columbia.edu/tag/00HQ) | A flat ring map is faithfully flat when its spectrum map is surjective | Applies conditionally in the forest nonflatness contradiction |
| [Stacks Lemma 10.107.7, tag 04VU](https://stacks.math.columbia.edu/tag/04VU) | A faithfully flat ring epimorphism is an isomorphism | Completes that contradiction |

The release bibliography points to the containing sections
[04VM](https://stacks.math.columbia.edu/tag/04VM) and
[00LY](https://stacks.math.columbia.edu/tag/00LY), which do contain
the cited lemmas. The matrix proof includes the final elementary
block-matrix step that the primary lemma leaves as an exercise.

Lemma 10.75.5 is stated as Tor symmetry; the cited double-resolution
argument is in its proof. The target does not import a sign convention
from that statement. It specifies its own Koszul differential and
checks its total-complex signs explicitly. Thus the citation supports
the algebraic computation while the manuscript's own formulas fix
the signs.

The Stacks Noetherian finiteness lemma 10.75.7 is not used. Nothing
in this dependency chain forces the arbitrary rings or modules to
be finite, Noetherian, reduced, flat, or torsion-free.

## 14. Final assessment and proposed changes

**Required mathematical changes to the live B.064/B.082 material:
none found.** The coefficient reduction, both transgressions,
kernel and cokernel assertions, and exact binary criterion agree
with the independent deduction and hold over the stated arbitrary
commutative rings.

The following editorial actions could improve precision, but do not
change the theorem:

1. Describe the Vandermonde step as multiplication by the adjugate,
   avoiding an unintended suggestion of division by a determinant.
2. Expand the compressed resolution-comparison sentence using the
   strict adapted-target lifting argument already present in the
   frozen proof.
3. When comparing the flat source theorem to the release, retain
   the convention declaration
   \(\partial_{\mathrm{live}}=\delta_0=-\partial_{80}\).
4. Preserve the frozen original and attach the Section 4
   transcription erratum rather than altering its checksum.

The source-only forest and cusp computations were verified after
the freeze and are recorded as supplements, not retroactively
claimed as independent pre-freeze work. They reinforce the intended
scope: nonflat coefficients can pass the criterion, while flat
epic coefficients alone do not ensure tensor injectivity.

All main-theorem obligations from the statement-only task are
closed. The frozen proof, its provenance, and the three target
hashes were preserved throughout this comparison.



### paper04_collar_radius_source_comparison.md

# Paper 4: collar, radius, and integral scope comparison

## Status and exposure

This is a targeted review by the Paper 4 integration worker, not a fresh-worker main-theorem gate. The worker previously handled source ingestion. A local statement-first derivation was frozen at 2026-11-23T09:58:27Z in `paper04_collar_statement_first_derivation.md`, SHA256 `7a05cc429fb2401be90321860b961973aca3e9d5a7f5a2ff4d1ac2b80f577a83`, before opening the corresponding collar proofs during this attack. That freeze proves the qualitative equation, shape derivative and high-frequency sign mechanism. It does **not** prove the fixed numerical parameters.

After freezing, the complete proofs in `part_a2_collar_general.tex`, `C_A2e_collar.tex`, and `part_a567_collar_explicit.tex` were read. The original fixed example uses the same exact separated mode that the frozen local derivation had found; this agreement is recorded after source access and makes no novelty or priority claim. The source's alternative approximate-mode/H2 estimate and its other weak-barrier/H1 argument were also checked, but the revised paper uses the exact-mode estimate.

## Mathematical results of the comparison

The curvature convention is fixed by the outward unit normal. The general equation is Delta u = exp(2u)-1, with outward derivative equal to signed outward geodesic curvature. The second run's inward-curvature convention has the opposite sign and is equivalent. Uniqueness follows by integration against the difference, with a positive zeroth-order coefficient. Gauss--Bonnet gives the area; conformal scaling gives the boundary lengths. Nonnegative boundary curvature, positive somewhere on a connected component, gives strict positivity there.

For a graph r = epsilon a(s), the exact signed-curvature formula gives first derivative a-a''. The Neumann operator Delta-2 is coercive and has no constant kernel; its boundary regularity gives the Holder-space inverse needed for the implicit-function argument. Differentiating the pullback operators gives no added first-order term because the zero-parameter solution is identically zero. These are the full hypotheses of the source shape statement, not merely a formal interior derivative.

In the conformal collar coordinate tau = arctan(sinh r), the mode (k-tan tau)exp(-k tau) solves the separated equation exactly. A fixed cutoff leaves an exponentially small residual away from the boundary. The homogeneous-Neumann correction is bounded in sup norm by half that residual. Thus the oscillating negative boundary value grows like -k/2 while the fixed background remains bounded. Fixing a sufficiently large frequency and then shrinking epsilon gives the source's arbitrary thinness and every prescribed C^m approximation. The retained old area is smaller than the uniformized area, forcing a positive value as well.

The fixed pant proof was checked separately after source access. Its cutoff is C2, which suffices for the classical comparison inequality; the actual cut boundaries are analytic. The collar containment, derivative constants, cutoff residual 40600102300 exp(-100), relative boundary error 1/10+10^-12, and final value -9991 epsilon/20 all hold. The comparison sign is correct: U-u has (Delta-c)(U-u) <= 0 and nonnegative outward derivative, so its negative part vanishes. No numerical PDE solve is used.

The complete original radius statements and relevant proofs in `part_a2b_secondary.tex` and `part_a2_limits.tex` were compared. The old paper already proves the uniform weighted length-area deficit and the full fixed-compactification kernel limit. Its conservative half-width collar estimate was insufficient for the exact displayed A2b-recession bound. The revision uses the full classical collar width. The recession proof is shortened without narrowing its quantifiers: the support-gap inequality at x0+tp, uniform coordinate upper bounds, and support continuity give the directional derivative limit for every p >= 0, including zero coordinates; Cesaro integration gives the potential limit.

The analytic-closed-disc characterization follows from simultaneous slight enlargement and the complete radius-image theorem. It identifies vectors and their unique canonical representatives, not arbitrary discs with canonical discs. The equal-three-length circular-lens limit and its radius 4/(3 sqrt(3)) were omitted before this revision. The critical graph, proper disc coordinate, sector map, and exact derivative are now supplied. The one-dominant and two-dominant examples and the non-Cartesian-product conclusion were already present; their scope was checked against the original statements.

The complete first-run integral gluing proposition and both runs' eigenmodule counterexamples were read and compared. The categorical gluing proof covers arbitrary left modules over possibly noncommutative rings, with no finiteness assumption; multiplication by the central integer two is the exact torsion condition. The original Dedekind sufficient-hypothesis statement does not exclude characteristic two, but its supplied proof invokes diagonalizability and therefore does not cover that characteristic. The new paragraph repairs the proof: N = sigma-1 is nonzero square-zero of generic rank one, and its finite torsion-free rank-one kernel is invertible over a Dedekind domain. The two eigenspaces coincide there; no direct-sum claim is made. The matrix-factorization counterexample and the connected-two-invertible argument were both checked in full.

## Exact scope holds

The normal-collar rows `RESEARCH_SA22::A2-collar` and `RESEARCH_SA22_run2::A:collar` do not explicitly state connectedness in their individual bodies. If “surface” allows disconnected surfaces and cuts are selected only on some components, their global strict-positivity clause is false on an untouched component. For example, take the disjoint union of two hyperbolic pants and remove a positive-width collar only from the first. On the second the compatible metric is unchanged, so u=0. The connected statement, and the componentwise statement with at least one positive cut on each component, are proved. These two rows are held for the exact convention/quantifier clarification; they are not silently accepted in a wider sense. The strict new-boundary length bound is explicitly included in the revision.

The arithmetic nodal tensor row `RESEARCH_SA22_run2::A:nodal-q-test` remains outside this batch. The current conditional Kummer argument does not prove graphic reconstruction or the claimed complete twist normal form. The 352-row inventory is an inventory, not an acceptance of the 299 initially unmapped assertions.

### continuation_A117_literature.md

# A117: outer-face literature comparison and search record

23 November2026. Root owns this ongoing main-result screening. The precise theorem is the compact-open quotient topology on locally parameterized strict-cone outer faces, with standard component weak type BP_TOP(B), and a rational semialgebraic essential torus loop whose non-filling conclusion retains the stated finite face witnesses over every real closed field. It is not a statement about every Hausdorff-continuous family of unparameterized images. Retaining a face parameterization instead gives the classifying space of the shell group fixing both ends.

The primary mathematical dependencies have been separately read and receipted: Chernavskii(1969), Theorem1 pp1247–1248 and covering-isotopy statement p1250, includes compact jointly continuous families of pointwise locally flat embeddings with the required supported extension; Chapman–Ferry(1979), Proposition7.1 p605, recognizes the proper completely regular finite-dimensional-base family as a bundle; Edwards–Kirby(1971), Corollary1.1 p63, supplies local contractibility of the homeomorphism group. The torus component calculation is the already checked explicit result in the paper, with its exact unstable category range. Hardt's finite trivialization and Basu–Pollack–Roy's finite first-order real-closed-field transfer supply the alternative independent finite-witness route. The independent reconstruction and original proof comparison are distinct records, and no novelty is inferred from reconstructing either proof.

### continuation_A124_five_surjectivity_literature.md

# Five-dimensional components: further primary reading and a smaller proposed gate

Date: 23 November 2026, research between 17:48 and 18:06 UTC. This is root's source-exposed research, not a statement-only independent reconstruction. The new proposition remains held until that separate reconstruction and target comparison are complete. Original A.124 remains unaccepted.

## Exact source findings

Hatcher, A. E., & Wagoner, J. B. (1973). *Pseudo-isotopies of compact manifolds* (Astérisque, Vol. 6). Société mathématique de France. Original scan: https://www.numdam.org/issue/AST_1973__6__1_0.pdf . Root read the extracted original text at printed pp.130,184,209–213,219–220,225–230,244. The following complete original page images were actually viewed:184,213,219,220,227,228,229. Other generated page images are not included in this viewing claim.

Theorem2 of ChapterVI, p.213, proves surjectivity of the first obstruction for dimensions at least five. The suspension remark on p.130 identifies its stabilization. ChapterVII Proposition5 and its construction, pp.227–228, realize arbitrary marked intersection coefficients when 3≤i≤n−2. The endpoint n−i=2 is treated explicitly on p.228: the inward push direction gives an integer loop in S¹ whose stabilization realizes either parity in π1O. The choice of Whitney disk realizes the π2 coefficient, and the construction can be repeated for finite sums. This is a realization statement, not classification of all five-dimensional lenses.

The warning immediately afterward, p.229, is mathematically relevant: in dimension five the refined invariant records integer framing and linking between fundamental-group-labelled components. Equality of the stabilized Z/2 coefficient is therefore insufficient, by itself, to identify unstabilized lens families. The simply connected special discussion on pp.219–220 and the dimension-six extension mentioned on p.230 do not supply the general dimension-five injectivity assertion.

Igusa, K. (2021a). *Second obstruction to pseudoisotopy I* (arXiv:2110.09659v1). https://arxiv.org/pdf/2110.09659v1 . Root read Section1.3 and Theorem1.8, Lemma1.10, and Propositions1.11–1.13 on pp.8–10. The lens classification is stated in dimensions at least six; the dimension-five integer-framing issue is explicitly separated. It is not a source for an unstated dimension-five completeness theorem.

Igusa, K. (2021b). *Second obstruction to pseudoisotopy in dimension 3* (arXiv:2112.08293v1). https://arxiv.org/pdf/2112.08293v1 . Root read Sections4.1–4.2, pp.8–9, including the positive-suspension convention and λ₀ˢ=(−1)ᵏλ₀. Positive suspension preserves the relevant lens indices and labels and adds a trivial framing direction. This is the compatibility used in the proposed realization argument.

Igusa, K. (1984). What happens to the formula of Hatcher and Wagoner when the first Postnikov invariant of M is nontrivial? In *Algebraic K-theory, number theory, geometry and analysis* (Lecture Notes in Mathematics, Vol.1046, pp.104–172). Springer. https://doi.org/10.1007/BFb0072020 . Section8.a and Theorem8.a.2, pp.149–150, give the corrected complete sequence in dimensions at least six. The already checked original chapter is used only for the six-dimensional target, with the entire χ quotient, not for a hypothetical dimension-five exact sequence.

Igusa, K. (2025). *Generalized Grassmann invariant—redrawn* (arXiv:2502.19147v2). https://arxiv.org/pdf/2502.19147v2 . Root read all pp.23–27 again. The official arXiv landing page was checked on23November2026 and still listed version2, submitted23March2025, as the latest version. Theorem7.1 states the desired dimension-five result with π2=0, but the introduction calls the application a partial proof. In Theorem7.5 on p.27 the difficult null-deformation implication refers to the thesis and the under-revision second obstruction sequel. Root also checked that the purported easier implication invokes deformation of equal-invariant lens families. That additional step cannot be silently supplied by the realization theorem above. The complete thesis/sequel proof was not retrieved by the new searches.

## Search and failed approach

Fresh searches used both search engines for the original thesis, the dimension-five sequel, and the exact geometric statement. The official arXiv record and the Brandeis repository record were opened; the author's Selected Papers page timed out. No author was contacted. The raw search bundle already saved as `continuation_external_primary/A124_root_followup_1748_search.json` remains outside the deliverable tree. This followup neither treats a failed page retrieval as proof of nonexistence nor claims that the stated five-dimensional theorem is false.

An attempted short route to the full isomorphism using only the easier half of Theorem7.5 stalled at the hidden lens-completeness step. It is recorded as a failed proof approach. The published six-dimensional corrected sequence, arbitrary five-dimensional realization, and suspension compatibility suggest a different conclusion that does not use that step.

## Proposed surjectivity and exact remaining gate

The held proof first lifts the Wh2 value of a target component to dimension five by Hatcher–Wagoner VI.2. It then corrects the residual kernel element using the **six-dimensional** corrected sequence. Every representative of the latter's Wh1 coefficient quotient is the stabilization of a five-dimensional marked-lens construction, using VII.5 at n=5,i=3. This would prove that positive suspension π0P(M5)→π0P(M5×I) is surjective for every compact smooth five-manifold, with no π2 hypothesis. Naturality would transfer it to PL and the stipulated definable models. It would identify the complete stable presentation E(K) as a quotient, with kernel undetermined.

The held statement packet was frozen at17:56:04.130237UTC, SHA256 `5f6654116dcbb8bfd3645cbd2acf6c0f2cf4d1c5eb948346abfaae9d028881a8`; the separate held proof was frozen at17:56:04.131037UTC, SHA256 `db6da665cc427ad444598b4b40c53267fddad7a202b55dbd733e89073f29502f`. # Standard spheres and simply connected three-manifolds: literature classification

23 November 2026. The statements are classical consequences, not assertions of priority. The standard-sphere component conclusion follows from Kuiper–Lashof II's whole-product PL bundle/concordance model (1966, pp243–244), Burghelea–Lashof II's unstable fiber formula (1974, p43, equation1) and low-degree stabilization (p37, formula3), and the stable low-degree calculation recorded by Kirby–Siebenmann (1977, AnnexC, p318). The exact source versions, actual download addresses, hashes and inspected portions are in `continuation_paper02_A128_A129_primary_receipts.json` and the two integer-witness A128 source receipts. The independent geometric proof uses different classical inputs, with its own explicit source record, and is not described as a new sphere calculation.

The simply connected closed three-manifold consequence additionally uses compatible smoothing (Kirby–Siebenmann, EssayI,p39), Perelman's elliptization conclusion (2003, §1.4,p3) and the actual PL-approximation statement and proof of Shalen (1984,p34,§9pp75–79). Baro–Otero's explicitly inspected arXiv version1, Corollary4.3,p10, transfers the fundamental-group hypothesis to the real finite model. The deduction does not use a dimension-three smooth/PL pseudoisotopy comparison. These are named known results with full logical deductions at the required scope; no full fresh proof of the Ricci-flow theorem or of all classical bundle theory is claimed.

The root read the complete targeted review and complete frozen independent sphere comparison before integrating the result. The latter distinguishes the separate geometric proof from the post-freeze reading of the target's PL/O calculation. The recognition corollary is a checked consequence, not a second statement-only reproof. No dedicated original numerical certificate exists for these purely topological component assertions; finite computation is not offered as a substitute.

### continuation_A150_A152_literature.md

# Scalar-clock and sphere regularization: literature check

Checked 23 November 2026 by root, GPT-6 Astra, inherited effort. These records support a held review of A.150 and A.152; neither original identity is accepted by this note.

## Searches performed

Two-engine public searches used the exact Valette title, generic combinations of `definable`, `increasing`, `Lipschitz`, `reparametrization`, `compact families`, and `Hausdorff`, and the exact Kocel-Cynk–Pawłucki–Valette title. Further searches explicitly restricted the generic o-minimal/Lipschitz terms to Hexagon and ECCC. The Hexagon results returned broad archive listings, without an exact matching statement in the returned material; the ECCC query returned no relevant theorem. These limited results are not a claim that no matching work exists. The journal, arXiv, and Valette's author page were opened, along with original Czapla and Smale PDFs. The Valette author page identifies the published 2023 chapter and links its arXiv version. Forward context found in those results concerns Lipschitz stratification and analytic applications; no source opened in this search states both of the precise relative regularization assertions under review.

## Exact primary inputs and status

1. Valette, G. (2023). Regular vectors and bi-Lipschitz trivial stratifications in o-minimal structures. In J. L. Cisneros-Molina, L. D. Tráng, and J. Seade (Eds.), *Handbook of geometry and topology of singularities IV* (pp. 411–448). Springer. https://doi.org/10.1007/978-3-031-31925-9_7. Published metadata was verified on Springer's chapter page, which dates first online publication to 7 October 2023. The actual mathematical text read was the author's arXiv:2110.05759v7, dated 29 September 2022, 29 pages. Proposition 5.7, printed pp. 25–26, gives a finite parameter partition with joint Lipschitz bounds on compact subsets when every spatial slice is Lipschitz. The introduction to Section 5, p. 22, explicitly exempts this proposition from the polynomial-boundedness restriction. Root read the complete proposition and proof and viewed all three pages. Its conclusion is local over parameter strata; the finite endpoint variation clock is an additional argument in the original A.150 proof. The proposition does not itself assert a homeomorphic clock fixing a prescribed finite set.

2. Czapla, M. (2012). Definable triangulations with regularity conditions. *Geometry & Topology, 16*, 2067–2095. https://doi.org/10.2140/gt.2012.16.2067. Root read and viewed printed pp. 2082–2084, containing Theorem 3.12 and its complete proof, and Remark 3.14. The theorem permits compatibility with finitely many definable subsets and has a locally Lipschitz triangulation map; the compact case in Remark 3.14 is globally Lipschitz. The source's introduction on p. 2068 explicitly allows arbitrary o-minimal real closed fields. The original A.152 proof uses the compact real case and a marked singleton or boundary image. It does not assume a relative Lipschitz triangulation extending prescribed boundary coordinates.

3. Smale, S. (1957). A Vietoris mapping theorem for homotopy. *Proceedings of the American Mathematical Society, 8*(3), 604–610. Root read all seven article pages as full-page images in the public Ranicki scan. The Main Theorem on p. 604 has proper surjectivity, local compactness, separable metrizability, local connectivity, and connected-fibre hypotheses. Its degree-n surjectivity is stated in based form as Theorem 9 on p. 608; the approximation argument uses Theorem 6 on pp. 606–607. The finite compact definable joins in the original A.152 argument meet these hypotheses over the real field. No fibration or continuous-section conclusion is imported.

4. Kocel-Cynk, B., Pawłucki, W., & Valette, A. (2014). A short geometric proof that Hausdorff limits are definable in any o-minimal structure. *Advances in Geometry, 14*(1), 49–58. https://doi.org/10.1515/advgeom-2013-0028. Journal metadata and the abstract were opened at the publisher. The indexed text of the authors' manuscript dated 5 September 2012 states on p. 1 a bounded definable Lipschitz bijection resolving a bounded compact-set family in the Hausdorff metric. The publisher did not provide full-text access in this retrieval; opening the author-posted Academia page timed out. Root does not claim a fresh complete proof reading of this paper. The new root derivation of A.150/A.152 instead uses the already accepted all-field compact-resolution theorem inside Paper 2, whose distinct 2019 differential input has its earlier primary receipts.

## Relation to the held replacement proofs

The original A.150 proof is a complete real-field argument using Valette's generic Lipschitz proposition, derivative selection, an explicit finite-variation clock, and a dense set of generic vertical lines. The root replacement obtains a scalar clock from finitely many monotone restrictions of affine paths in the accepted compact cube resolution. It appears to extend to arbitrary o-minimal real closed fields; this stronger scope remains subject to separate independent review.

The original A.152 proof uses the real Hausdorff resolution, finite joins, Smale's mapping theorem, and Czapla's triangulation. The root replacement obtains the sphere assertion from the accepted relative cube theorem by an explicit Lipschitz ball quotient and by collapsing a collar before descent. Both arguments preserve the basepoint; the cube assertion preserves its entire boundary. These are relative consequences of specified inputs, with their full assembly written out. No priority claim is made. Neither Hausdorff regularity nor the parameter map gives a matching homeomorphism between fibres.

## Original certificate inventory

The complete source trees were searched for A150, A152, A.150, and A.152 in Python programs, test/certificate files, and JSON. The returned Python references concern record integration and PDF layout, not numerical or formal certificates for either assertion. Both original proofs are analytic/topological and contain no reported numerical computation requiring rerun. The new ball-quotient identity and interval estimates are proved algebraically in the held derivation; no finite computation is substituted for the universal assertions.

## Primary comparison

Baro, E., & Otero, M. (2010). On o-minimal homotopy groups. *The Quarterly Journal of Mathematics, 61*(3), 275–289. https://doi.org/10.1093/qmath/hap011

Root freshly opened the journal record and the author's publication page, https://www.ucm.es/ebaro/investigacion, and opened the original preprint https://arxiv.org/pdf/0810.0365. The exact cached v1 PDF, downloaded earlier in this continuation, has 219,703 bytes and SHA256 `92f166a29a807fbaed516697bc2c0e16cfaefc8ce6c4b85ccac1350ccc614cbb`. Its own page numbering is used here: Theorem 3.1 on p. 5 compares relative homotopy classes of semialgebraic pairs; Theorem 4.1 on p. 9 compares their semialgebraic and definable homotopy groups; Corollaries 4.3–4.4 on p. 10 give real comparison and invariance under elementary extension/expansion. Root read the complete pages 9–11 and the exact Theorem 3.1 statement and surrounding reduction on p. 5. The original preprint is distinguished from the journal pagination. This note does not claim a fresh whole-paper or full-proof reading of Theorem 3.1.

These are homotopy results for finite-dimensional definable sets and systems. They do not by themselves replace a family of homeomorphisms by a PL family while preserving invertibility and parameter projection. The present corollary therefore invokes the independently proved concordance comparison, whose hypotheses enforce those conditions, and the effective code theorem already in the paper. The general invariance theme is classical; its exact application to this specified concordance group is written out.

May, J. P. (1967). *Simplicial objects in algebraic topology*. Van Nostrand. The already checked 1982 reprint is used for exact locators: Theorem 16.6, p. 65; Theorem 17.1, pp. 67–68; Proposition 17.2 and both propositions numbered 17.3, pp. 68–69; Proposition 17.4, p. 69. # Projective concordances and joint odd germs: primary audit in progress

23 November 2026, 19:35–19:46 UTC. Root froze a statement-derived reduction before opening the complete original A162 proof. The entire 376-line original was then read in two bounded calls. The target's geometric extension mechanism addresses the principal gate in that reduction: a free involution on the complementary four-dimensional annulus is a product, while relative parameter lifting is supplied separately by a normalized Edwards–Kirby correction on its free quotient. The original does not infer a parametric theorem from the individual equivariant annulus theorem.

## Exact primary readings completed

Boyle, K., Chen, W., & Conway, A. (2025, December 27). *Involutions on S⁴* (arXiv:2512.22724v1). https://arxiv.org/pdf/2512.22724v1

The exact 25-page v1 PDF was freshly downloaded at 19:43:06.937647 UTC: 573,022 bytes, SHA256 `4a2a689d492c87f94caea386b3635a46d62f95be401a97ffcfd9122da23c3834`. Its entire displayed proof was read: Livesay identifies both quotient boundaries as RP³; the quotient is an h-cobordism; Kwasik–Schultz supplies its product structure; the universal cover lifts that product. Theorem 1.4 on p. 15 uses the nonequivariant four-dimensional annulus theorem and Theorem 2.3 in the zero-dimensional-fixed-set case. The rest of that page was read but is not needed here. No whole-paper or recursive audit of all their cited foundations is claimed.

Pedersen, E. K. (1975). The topological category. In D. Burghelea, R. Lashof, & M. Rothenberg, *Groups of automorphisms of manifolds* (Lecture Notes in Mathematics, Vol.473, Appendix2, pp.142–152). Springer. The original p149 explicitly records component reduction to an absolute three-handle stage, using the same reduction as in the PL category. Root and the worker inspected that original page. Theorem3.3 and its preceding paragraph, p17, specify the extension map and its hypotheses. ### continuation_integer_literature.md

# Integer-valued polynomial literature audit — continuation from checkpoint 13

Date: 2026-11-23. Checker: a separate GPT-6 Astra Pro worker, with inherited effort not separately reported. This is a primary-source literature and targeted proof audit, not an independent proof of the proposed freeness theorem. No target manuscript proof was needed for these findings. Downloaded source PDFs and text extracts remain outside the deliverable tree in `reference_cache/`.

## 1. Exact Elliott conjectures and their logical status

Elliott (2011), *Integer-valued polynomial rings, t-closure, and associated primes*, Communications in Algebra, 39(11), 4128–4147, DOI `10.1080/00927872.2010.519366`. The publisher confirms the bibliographic data. The inspected author preprint is `https://arxiv.org/pdf/1105.0142v1`; its body has 20 printed pages despite the abstract page's stale “18 pages” comment.

| Locator in inspected preprint | Verified content |
|---|---|
| Conjecture 2.10, p. 11 | Existence of a local Noetherian one-dimensional analytically irreducible domain with nonflat Int(D). |
| Proposition 2.14(1), p. 12 | For D=F₂+tF₄[[t]], with V=F₄[[t]] and M=tV, the proposed nonmembership is (X²+X)/t not in V Int(D), equivalently X²+X not in M Int(D). |
| Conjecture 2.15, p. 12 | Conjectures Proposition 2.14(2), or even (1), hence nonflatness for this D. |
| Proposition 2.16(1), p. 12 | For D=F₂[[t²,t³]], V=F₂[[t]], M=(t²,t³), the analogous proposed nonmembership uses denominator t². |
| Conjecture 2.17, p. 12 | Conjectures Proposition 2.16(2), or even (1), hence nonflatness. |
| Proposition 2.13, p. 12 | If V=I⁻¹ is an overring for a finitely generated ideal I, flatness implies Int(D,V)=V Int(D). |

The propositions assert **implications**, not the truth of their numbered antecedents. Witnesses negating (1) refute the conjectured antecedents; they do not refute Propositions 2.14 or 2.16. Cite the conjectures themselves. The proposed general freeness theorem would refute Conjecture 2.10 and both specific conjectures, subject to its proof audit.

## 2. Later recollections are not the original source

Elliott, *Presentations and module bases of integer-valued polynomial rings*, `https://arxiv.org/pdf/1109.3921`, p. 2, recalls the two explicit nonflatness conjectures and directs the reader to the earlier paper. It is not their originating locator.

Elliott, *Integer-valued polynomials on commutative rings and modules*, `https://arxiv.org/pdf/1608.00171` (v1; submission dated 2016, PDF's internal date July 10, 2018), pp. 1–2: page 2 recalls the author's conjecture that nonfree examples exist, and more generally that some domains fail the indicated plethory/universal-property conditions. This is a historical statement, not a present-day open status after Ma (2026). Page 2's implication from freeness cites *Idempotent plethories*, which requires the version caution below.

## 3. Problems 19–21 and safe positive implications

Cahen, Fontana, Frisch, and Glaz (2014), *Open problems in commutative ring theory*, in *Commutative Algebra: Recent Advances in Commutative Rings, Integer-Valued Polynomials, and Polynomial Functions*, pp. 353–375, DOI `10.1007/978-1-4939-0925-4_20`. Inspected authors' version is dated December 23, 2013, at `https://www2.math.uconn.edu/~glaz/My_Articles/OpenProblemsInCommutativeRingTheory.Springer14.pdf`.

- Problem 19a, p. 9: universal D-flatness; 19b, p. 9: universal D-freeness.
- Problem 20, p. 9: injectivity and surjectivity of the canonical n-variable tensor map. Its preliminary sentence expressly records the free-module sufficient case and cites Elliott's 2007 *Universal properties* paper.
- Problem 21, p. 10: existence of a unique D–D-biring structure compatible with D[X] inclusion. Its following sentence records the sufficient hypothesis that all tensor maps are isomorphisms, citing Elliott's *Birings and plethories*.

Thus the requested positive answers for the proposed one-branch class follow through established implications, which should be credited separately from the freeness result. The word “D” in the locally-free alternative of Problem 20 appears to be a typographical omission of Int(D); use only the unambiguous free-module alternative here.

## 4. Exact nonwithdrawn biring reference

Elliott (2010), *Birings and plethories of integer-valued polynomials*, Actes des rencontres du CIRM, 2(2), 53–58, DOI `10.5802/acirm.34`. Publisher PDF: `https://www.numdam.org/item/10.5802/acirm.34.pdf`. Author version: `https://arxiv.org/pdf/1109.3848v3`.

- Theorem 4, printed p. 54 (preprint p. 3), gives the criterion under torsion-freeness of tensor powers through arity 4.
- Corollary 5, printed p. 54 (preprint p. 3), gives the unique compatible biring for a polynomially composite domain.
- Theorem 12(1), printed p. 56 (preprint p. 6), says compatible biring implies **weak** polynomial compositeness.
- Theorem 12(2), printed p. 57 (preprint p. 6), gives the converse with torsion-free tensor powers through arity 4.
- Proposition 13 and Corollary 14, printed p. 57 (preprint pp. 6–7), give the compatible plethory extension.

This note is a published summary. Its exact hypotheses support the requested consequences; the present paper should retain its self-contained coefficient/basis proof. Do not replace weak polynomial compositeness with all-arity tensor isomorphisms in a purported equivalence.

## 5. Ma's two-branch nonflat example

Ma (2026), *A counterexample to Problem 19 on integer-valued polynomial rings*, `https://arxiv.org/pdf/2604.05922v2`; v2 dated May 27, 2026 and latest listed version on inspection.

Theorem 1.1, p. 1, gives nonflatness. Lemma 3.1, pp. 2–4, proves that T is the semilocal PID obtained from F₂[t] by inverting elements outside (t)∪(t+1), and D=F₂+t(t+1)T is a one-dimensional Noetherian local domain with normalization T, conductor M=t(t+1)T, and M⁻¹=T. Both maximal ideals of T contract to M: this is exactly a two-branch example. Proposition 4.1, pp. 4–5, proves (X²+X)/(t(t+1)) lies in Int(D,T) but outside T Int(D). Theorem 1.1's proof and Corollary 5.1, p. 6, conclude negative answers to both parts of Problem 19.

Targeted check: choosing u=t(t+1)^(n+1) makes every finite family's h(u)-h(0) lie in M, but u has t-valuation 1. Any claimed X²+X∈M Int(D) would force u∈M², contradiction. The proof checks. The author credits Rethlas and reports human verification (p. 6, §6).

## 6. Jiang et al.'s same-domain biring obstruction

Jiang, Li, Sun, Wang, Xiao, and Yu (2026), *On some open problems in commutative algebra resolved by Rethlas*, `https://arxiv.org/pdf/2605.25259v2`; v2 dated May 28, 2026 and latest listed version on inspection. The downloaded PDF's internal date is May 29, 2026. The arXiv HTML conversion instead displays August 24, 2026: this is a conversion discrepancy, not the PDF's date. Retain the explicit arXiv version.

Problem 2.4, p. 3, restates Problem 21. Theorem 3.4 and its proof (§3.4), pp. 7–8, use exactly Ma's two-branch D. With m=t(t+1), q(X)=(X²+X)/m, g=q²+q, they prove g∈Int(D), but g(XY) is outside the image of the binary tensor map. Consequently D is not weakly polynomially composite and has no compatible biring. The explicit nonsurjectivity also answers the surjectivity part of Problem 20 negatively for this ring.

Targeted check: for u=t(t+1)^(n+1), g(u²)∈M\M² while any finite separated sum has mixed difference at (u,u),(u,0),(0,u),(0,0) in M². The contradiction checks. The proof uses Elliott Theorem 12(1) at its correct scope. The introductory “if and only if all tensor maps are isomorphisms” is stronger than that theorem and should not be repeated without a separate proof. No converse counterexample is asserted here.

## 7. Version caution: Idempotent plethories

The primary arXiv record `https://arxiv.org/abs/1505.06493` explicitly marks v4 withdrawn on June 10, 2025. The author's comment is “I found some critical errors that I was unable to fix.” This is a literature-status event, not a proof that every theorem in the earlier paper is false. Do not cite unexamined equivalences there as established inputs. The 2010 biring theorem and the elementary free-basis argument suffice for the requested positive tensor/biring consequences.

## 8. Classical inputs checked here, separately audited by root

Stacks Project, Tag 032Y, Lemma 10.162.10(4), `https://stacks.math.columbia.edu/tag/032Y`, states finiteness of normalization in the total fraction ring for any analytically unramified Noetherian local ring; (5) specializes to domains. Analytic irreducibility makes the completion a domain, hence reduced, so the finiteness implication is immediate. Locality of the normalization and the DVR conclusion are additional steps, not part of the assertion of Tag 032Y itself. Root is checking those steps and Kaplansky's original theorem independently.

## 11. Newton basis attribution

The greedy valuation ordering used to construct a basis of B=Int(R,V) is Bhargava's v-ordering construction. An exact accessible statement is Chabert (2014), *Integer-Valued Polynomials: Looking for Regular Bases (a Survey)*, author PDF `https://www.lamfa.u-picardie.fr/chabert/graz.survey.pdf`, Definition 4, pp. 7–8, and Proposition 11, p. 8 (§4.2). Under the §4 notation (V a DVR, S a subset of V), the normalized Newton polynomials associated with a v-ordering of S form a V-module basis of Int(S,V). This matches S=R exactly. The result does not require finite residue fields; those matter in subsequent approximation steps.

Proposition 11 credits Bhargava's 1997 paper, *P-orderings and polynomial functions on arbitrary subsets of Dedekind rings*, DOI `10.1515/crll.1997.490.101`. The original full text was not recovered in this search: publisher/AMS attempts returned 403 and the old digitization link returned 404. Chabert's exact statement is verified, rather than assigning an unverified theorem number to Bhargava. The original bibliography has an endpoint discrepancy: Chabert and Princeton's institutional record give 101–127, while publisher/digital-library metadata give 101–128. Retain this access limitation if citing exact pagination without the original in hand.

Chabert's published chapter is pp. 83–111, DOI `10.1007/978-1-4939-0925-4_5`, confirmed on the publisher page. The theorem locator above deliberately uses the inspected 27-page author version, not inferred printed pagination. Web refs: `turn197205725view2`, `turn612998081view3` (Proposition 11), `turn242776321view1` (DVR notation), and `turn700111314view1` (publisher metadata).

## 12. Arbitrary-rank Milnor patching

Chen and Le (2020), *Deriving Milnor's theorem on pullback rings*, `https://arxiv.org/pdf/2004.05899v1`, Theorem 2.3, p. 4 (also unnumbered Milnor theorem, p. 1), explicitly states the equivalence between all projective modules over a pullback ring and gluing triples of projectives, when one map to the overlap ring is surjective. It separately states the finitely generated restriction. The upper-case category Proj is explicitly all projectives, so the result covers infinite rank without a new extension. The gluing isomorphism is part of the data. The direct block proof in the manuscript can be retained as a proof of this special case with clear classical attribution.

The source credits Milnor (1971), *Introduction to Algebraic K-Theory*, §2; Herbera and Příhoda (2011), `https://arxiv.org/pdf/1105.3627v1`, p. 2, specifically cite Milnor Theorems 2.1–2.3 for all projectives. Milnor's publisher table of contents places §2 on pp. 19–24. The original theorem's exact page was not read, so use the section/theorem citation together with the verified Chen–Le locator rather than inventing a page. The publisher frontmatter itself shows 1971 (despite the current sale page's 1972 metadata).

Herbera–Příhoda (2009), `https://arxiv.org/pdf/0903.2965v1`, Theorem 4.7 and Remark 4.8, pp. 18–19, provide corroboration about countable generation. Their abbreviated monoid formulation is not needed here; retain the explicit gluing triple from Chen–Le, which avoids suppressing the gluing isomorphism. Weibel's Chapter I, Theorem 2.7, is a convenient finite-projective formulation but should not alone be quoted as an arbitrary-rank statement.

Web refs for root to reopen: `https://arxiv.org/html/2004.05899v1`, `turn578176009view1` and `turn242776321view0` (Theorem 2.3); `https://arxiv.org/html/0903.2965v1`, `turn578176009view0`; Milnor frontmatter `https://www.degruyterbrill.com/document/doi/10.1515/9781400881796-fm/pdf`, `turn581791189view0`. The Chen–Le downloaded PDF is dated April 14, 2020; its HTML also displays the conversion date August 24, 2026, confirming that HTML dates require care.

## 13. Further prior-art checks and citation chaining

On 2026-11-23 the forward-reference check reached Tamoussit and Tartarone (2023), *Essential properties for rings of integer-valued polynomials*, New York Journal of Mathematics 29, 467–487. Its introduction and theorems concern essential-type and valuation-localization properties; the inspected passages do not assert the proposed nonnormal one-branch freeness theorem. It is adjacent literature, not a substitute proof.

Elliott (2009), *Some new approaches to integer-valued polynomial rings*, DOI `10.1515/9783110213188.223`, §6, contains a generic evaluation pullback construction for Int(E,D). Search-indexed text identifies Proposition 6.1 and the ensuing discussion on printed p. 233, which notes that the evaluation map is generally not surjective. This is a relevant precursor to the conductor-square method. The publisher metadata were retrieved, but a primary full-text copy of this chapter was not opened; therefore the exact theorem statement/page remains an access gate, and no claim that it proves the proposed finite-conductor result is made. Metadata likewise differ on terminal page 237/238.

Additional dated queries: `Bhargava p orderings 1997 490 101 pdf regular basis theorem`; `"Integer-Valued Polynomials: Looking for Regular Bases" pdf`; `"Milnor" "Constructing Projective Modules" "2.1"`; `"Big projective modules over noetherian semilocal rings" arxiv`; `"integer-valued" "freeness" "conductor"`; `"Universal properties of integer-valued polynomial rings" "free" "Proposition"`; and author-hosted Chabert publication listing. Search results were used to identify sources; the exact verified statements above came from the source PDFs. No search failure establishes novelty.

## 14. Older normalization and approximation sources

Cahen, Grazzini, and Haouat (1982), *Intégrité du complété et théorème de Stone-Weierstrass*, Annales scientifiques de l'Université de Clermont-Ferrand 2, tome 73, série Mathématiques 21, 47–58, provides an exact primary source for the locality passage. In `https://www.numdam.org/item/ASCFM_1982__73_21_47_0.pdf`, §2, unnumbered Proposition, printed p. 49 (PDF p. 4), assumes a one-dimensional Noetherian local domain with finite normalization and proves that domain completion implies local normalization; its proof continues on p. 50. No finite residue field assumption occurs in this proposition. Section 4's Proposition p. 52 and collected Theorem p. 53, under the additional finite residue field assumption, give the Stone–Weierstrass characterization. This complements Stacks032Y's finiteness result. The exact source is open (`turn302758921view0`), and its scan was downloaded and text extracted.

Cahen and Chabert (1999), *Skolem properties and integer-valued polynomials: A survey*, in *Advances in Commutative Ring Theory*, Lecture Notes in Pure and Applied Mathematics 205, 175–195. Chabert's author page confirms these metadata (publication item63, `turn811953965view1`). The inspected author PDF, `https://www.lamfa.u-picardie.fr/chabert/skolem%28a%20survey%29.pdf`, Proposition 4.3, pp. 18–19, proves uniform density of Int(E,D) in the continuous functions on the compact closure of E, for one-dimensional local Noetherian analytically irreducible D. The proof uses polynomial approximations to clopen indicators and passage through the finite DVR normalization. This is relevant classical credit for the approximation ingredient, not a claim about freeness. Web refs `turn642129194view1`, `turn445644703view1`.

Rush (1999), *Generating ideals in rings of integer-valued polynomials*, Canadian Mathematical Bulletin 42(2), 231–236, DOI `10.4153/CMB-1999-028-0`, Theorem 2.1 and proof p. 233, likewise use polynomial approximation to clopen characteristic functions under these analytic irreducibility hypotheses, citing Cahen–Chabert (1997), Proposition XI.2.4. The theorems concern numbers of generators of ideals of Int(R), not D-module freeness of Int(D). The primary publisher PDF was inspected (`turn642129194view0`).

The 2026 update on Chabert's author page and its cited arXiv:2611.00906v1 (Xuyang Chen, submitted October31,2026) were also checked on 2026-11-23. The cited new results concern stacked bases over number-field rings, maximal ideals of valuation-domain integer-valued polynomial rings, and matrix polynomial closures. The abstract was read; this is a scoped contemporary check, not a full-paper priority clearance (`turn847064692view0`).

## 15. Elliott's 2013 cusp announcement and the subsequent publication trail

All checks in this section were made on 2026-11-23, after the three independent arbitrary-base proof freezes described in Section 17 below. The MathOverflow page was first identified and opened by root; this worker subsequently opened it independently.

Elliott's question *Polynomials mapping the twisted cubic power series ring to itself*, originally posted September 12, 2011, contains an addition dated December 26, 2013 stating that Int(F₂[[T²,T³]]) is free and announcing an intention to write the proof in a paper. The page itself does not contain that freeness proof. It also contains user2035's September 12, 2011 answer giving a polynomial counterexample to the derivative condition in the original question. The announcement establishes clear prior public credit for the cusp freeness claim; the special case must not be presented as a new result or as a conjecture still open in 2026. ## 16. Classical ring-epimorphism inputs and a post-freeze historical example

Ferrand (1967–1968), *Monomorphismes de schémas noethériens*, Séminaire Samuel, Algèbre commutative 2, exposé 7, 1–25, Lemma 2.1 on printed p. 7-05 (PDF page 6), states that a local ring epimorphism from a complete Noetherian local ring to a local ring is surjective. The target is not assumed Noetherian in the lemma. The primary scan and its proof were inspected at https://www.numdam.org/article/SAC_1967-1968__2__A7_0.pdf (refs turn190885388view0 and turn782938510view1). This is the precise classical input used in the complete local coefficient-ring classification of the third independent freeze.

Ferrand (1972), *Monomorphismes et morphismes absolument plats*, Bulletin de la Société Mathématique de France 100, 97–128, DOI 10.24033/bsmf.1732, introductory assertion 0.2.2 on p. 98, gives related henselian-local epimorphism background. The inspected PDF is https://www.numdam.org/item/10.24033/bsmf.1732.pdf (turn190885388view1). The frozen argument cites the exact 1968 lemma and does not need a stronger inferred version of the 1972 statement.

Stacks Project, Tag 0BCR, Lemma 31.17.1, states that the punctured spectrum of a Noetherian local ring is affine if and only if its dimension is at most one: https://stacks.math.columbia.edu/tag/0BCR (turn782938510view0). The elementary ring-epimorphism properties used with it were checked at Tag 04VM, https://stacks.math.columbia.edu/tag/04VM (turn936116366view0). These two pages and both Ferrand PDFs were cached with exact hashes and retrieval times in reference_cache/arbitrary_base_background_20261123.json.

Only after the third freeze, a separate Elliott MathOverflow question dated January 25–26, 2014 was opened: *Tensor powers of an algebra all isomorphic*, https://mathoverflow.net/questions/155667/tensor-powers-of-an-algebra-all-isomorphic (turn852337551view1). Jason Starr's final answer edit gives the injective ring epimorphism Z → Z[1/2] × Z/2, whose target is not a subring of Q. This is prior background illustrating why coefficient torsion cannot be dismissed merely from the solid-algebra condition. The page gives no arbitrary-base integer-valued-polynomial classification and was not an input to any frozen derivation.

Elliott, J. (2013, December 26). *Polynomials mapping the twisted cubic power series ring to itself* [Addition to MathOverflow question 75202, originally posted September 12, 2011]. https://mathoverflow.net/q/75202

Root opened the complete primary page. Elliott announces cusp freeness in the dated addition; no proof accompanies that announcement. The manuscript now credits that history explicitly. Follow-up searches have not yet located the promised written proof; this is an access/search status, not a statement that no such paper exists.
