### continuation_low_components_literature.md

# Exact classical sources for exceptional component calculations

Search and reading date:23November2026UTC. This record supplements the statement-only proof and subsequent targeted comparisons; it makes no priority claim. The sphere, projective-plane and simply connected five-manifold conclusions are consequences of classical topology, with the separate relative PL–definable comparison giving their field interpretation.

The original Cerf1971 conference summary, Theorem1 on printedp76, supplies the closed simply connected smooth pseudoisotopy theorem in dimension at least five. Publisher metadata at https://link.springer.com/chapter/10.1007/BFb0068612 confirms the chapter's page range76–82. The independently downloaded original Cerf1970 paper was subsequently read at Definition2 onp7 and Theorem0 onp8. Connectivity of the full pseudoisotopy group, rather than just the endpoint consequence, is the input. The source reviewer additionally viewed the final theorem onp156. Download receipts and hashes are in the separate root bibliographic addendum and source-reference table.

Kalliongis and McCullough(1992), Lemma4.1.1 and complete proof onp110, supplies the generator of the full-boundary-fixed homeotopy group of the projective-plane cylinder. Freeing the upper end contracts that rotation. Laudenbach(1974), ChapterI§1,pp7–9, supplies the category comparison; the inverse bottom family makes the resulting PL isotopy relative to the bottom. Epstein(1966), Theorem6.4(a),p101, supplies the initial correction of the upper map. The complete original target uses a separate all-PL annulus-cutting proof, checked in the comparison. Its source-error discussion is kept out of the publication proof.

## Burghelea–Lashof endpoint verification

The author-upload HTML at https://www.academia.edu/103489177/The_homotopy_type_of_the_space_of_diffeomorphisms_II was read at the definitions and Theorem6.1,pp41–43, and the split sphere–disk–rotation fibration onp43. The displayed theorem omits a dimension qualifier, while its proof uses Theorem5.1. This is not used as an unexamined all-dimensional comparison.

The first-page original image was obtained from the public image URL actually present in that author's page:
https://0.academia-photos.com/attachment_thumbnails/103483017/mini_magick20230617-1-1f35xq.png?1687039874 . Root visually inspected the original printedp37. Theorem5.1(a) says **k≥5**, including the endpoint; the OCR text had lost the equality sign. The preceding formulas(4) and(4′) likewise use n≥5. Image SHA256:`366c90c16b7d4031805d14ea7054b97bcc7895fe13024034485f08e4c3e41faa`;8,203bytes. This is an original-page preview, not a downloaded full PDF. Direct AMS requests returned403, and a JSTOR “PDF” response was HTML, not a paper; neither is counted as a successful primary PDF retrieval.

A modern primary corroboration was opened and downloaded: Kupers,A.(2019). Some finiteness results for groups of automorphisms of manifolds. *Geometry & Topology,23*,2277–2333. https://msp.org/gt/2019/23-5/gt-v23-n5-p03-p.pdf . Theorem5.11 on printedp2322 gives n≥5 and the PL(n)/O(n) stabilization range through n+1. SHA256:`f11e3fb1d2329ac7617a58f1027e06a25d65ea2831495a4528c073b6e035fedf`;693,446bytes. This is used as corroboration of the classical scope, not as a claim to have read the entire58-page article.

The two-sphere publication proof uses the direct three-dimensional category comparison and bottom correction. The five-dimensional proof uses the now visually checked high-dimensional endpoint. Neither argument invokes a general base-dimension-three or four smooth/PL concordance comparison. Kirby–Siebenmann's handle-smoothing passages onpp39,200,211 concern the compatible smoothing and twisted-sphere obstructions; they are distinct from the concordance comparison range.

Broad searches for additional exact counterparts and the other required repositories are recorded separately by the source reviewer. A bounded search is never used here to infer novelty or priority.


### continuation_paper02_A119_A124_literature.md

# A.119–A.124: dated prior-art and exact-input review

**Search date:** 23 November 2026 (UTC). **Reviewer:** int_free_independent. The original target proofs were already exposed to this reviewer. This is a fresh literature and targeted source comparison, not an independent reconstruction. Earlier audit notes are not counted as new proof checks.

## Conclusion at the claimed scopes

The stable classification underlying A.119–A.120 is classical. The countable cycle quotient is an explicit presentation obtained from established \(A\)-theory, group completion, covering-space, and Hurewicz machinery. This comparison does not justify advertising a new stable pseudoisotopy classification. The three-skeleton localization in A.121 has a classical Burghelea–Lashof–Rothenberg precedent. A.122 uses the corrected Igusa obstruction sequence in dimension at least six. A.123 assembles classical low-dimensional results at its stated surfaces and balls. A.124 is held at the full dimension-five geometric exact-sequence gate.

Searches did not locate a prior source using exactly the staged normalized-chain formula for \(E(K)\). This establishes neither novelty nor absence of such a presentation. The formula should be described as an explicit consequence or presentation of the cited theory until a stronger comparison is available.

| Row | Exact scope compared | Checked counterpart and attribution limit |
|---|---|---|
| A.119 | Countable normalized integral cycle presentation of stable smooth pseudoisotopy components, and the stated unstable stable-range consequence | Waldhausen–Jahren–Rognes, stable parametrized \(h\)-cobordism; Igusa stability; monomial units; acyclic group completion; covering spaces and Hurewicz. Credit the established classification. The countable-model and chain descriptions are the explicit deductions. No finite effective presentation or decision procedure is asserted. |
| A.120 | Stable PL component classification and its definable interpretation over real closed fields | Waldhausen–Jahren–Rognes, including the PL comparison, and low-degree vanishing of the point Whitehead spectrum. The relative PL/definable comparison is separately checked. No new general stable PL \(h\)-cobordism theorem is claimed. |
| A.121 | Localization to a smoothable regular neighborhood of the three-skeleton, and the stated \(E(K^{(3)})\) consequence | Burghelea–Lashof–Rothenberg (1975), extension connectivity. Original Theorem 3.1 and its proof on pp. 28–29 were read. The exact primed PL range was read in Galvin–Nonino (2025), Theorem 3.3, p. 17; the original primed theorem page remains unaccessed. |
| A.122 | Components for compact PL manifolds of dimension at least six, with the full corrected obstruction map | Igusa (1984), Section 8.a and Theorem 8.a.2, pp. 149–150; Jahren (2010), Corollary 2.7, p. 250; framed positive suspension in Igusa (2021). Both Grassmann and first-Postnikov contributions remain. No splitting is assumed. |
| A.123 | Vanishing in dimensions 0–1, for PL balls, orientable surfaces except \(S^2\), and closed nonorientable surfaces except \(\mathbb{RP}^2\) | PL Alexander extension; Epstein (1966); Waldhausen (1968), Lemma 3.5; Laudenbach (1974), Theorem II.6.1 and category comparison. No extension in this row to the exceptional surfaces, nonorientable surfaces with boundary, or higher homotopy groups. |
| A.124 | \(E(K)\) for compact connected PL five-manifolds with \(\pi_2=0\) | Igusa (2025), Theorem 7.1, p. 23, states the needed exact sequence. Pages 24–27 supply a partial geometric argument and refer a hard reduction elsewhere. **Held:** the conditional comparison is checked; the complete dimension-five geometric classification has not been read or independently reconstructed. |

## Searches run and outcomes

Both available search engines were used. Exact submitted queries and complete returned responses are saved as JSON outside the deliverable tree; the companion receipt lists their paths and hashes. Broad searches sometimes returned irrelevant results. These are recorded as coverage limits, not as evidence of absence.

### arXiv and journals

Queries included pseudoisotopy/concordance with “countable”, “presentation”, and “Whitehead”; pseudoisotopy with “3-skeleton”, “three-skeleton”, “Burghelea Lashof”, “group completion”, “dimension six”, and “dimension 5” with “Igusa exact sequence”. Both site-qualified arXiv queries and an explicit arXiv domain filter were used. The journal search also used “pseudoisotopy group complete presentation algebraic K theory Whitehead countable”.

Useful results led to the checked classical sources and recent work on higher or rational pseudoisotopy ranges. Krannich's publisher version of *A homological approach to pseudoisotopy theory. I* was opened; its introduction, defining formulas, and Theorem A were read. It concerns a highly connected \(p\)-local comparison for even-dimensional disks and explains the established stable \(A\)-theory approach. It is not the exact countable integral cycle presentation here. Randal-Williams's stable-range upper-bound paper was found but is not used as an input in this audit.

Samuel Muñoz Echániz's 2025 Cambridge thesis was opened, and introductory printed pp. 8–10 were read. These describe \(A(X)\) through the suspension ring spectrum of the loop space, the Whitehead fiber sequence, and the parametrized \(h\)-cobordism connection. A full-text search for “countable” returned no match in the exposed text. This is a scope comparison and keyword search, not a full review of the thesis or an argument for novelty.

### Authors and the exact dimension-five source

Queries included “pseudoisotopy” with “countable” and “presentation”; “Burghelea” with “k+3” and “skeleton”; and “Jahren” with the exact Bökstedt-trace title. Jahren's institutional publication page and original article were returned. The article's exact relevant portions were already directly read in the current primary-source audit. The Burghelea author upload located front matter; it did not supply the original primed PL theorem page.

For A.124, both engines were used for Igusa's 1979 Wh3 thesis, the exact thesis title, Princeton plus download/PDF, and the exact title “Second obstruction to pseudoisotopy II”. The author's CV and bibliography identify the thesis. The searches also returned the 2021 and 2025 papers that cite it. They did not retrieve a full thesis proof at the required dimension-five scope. A direct selected-works-page request returned a 502 error; cached search descriptions are not treated as a read full document. Reference [I8] in the downloaded 2025 version identifies the dimension-five continuation as under revision. This is an access finding, not a claim that no manuscript exists.

The exact original 1984 corrected-obstruction chapter has now been downloaded and inspected. It is the same work as the original source's Igusa1984Postnikov and the manuscript's Igusa1984, not another 1984 paper. Its access upgrade is recorded in the separate A122 original-primary addendum.

### ECCC

Queries included pseudoisotopy/concordance/Whitehead directed to ECCC, a site-qualified exact pseudoisotopy query, and an explicit ECCC-domain-filtered query. Returned responses contained unrelated repository material or cross-domain results. No exact counterpart was identified through them. The relevance and coverage are weak; this is not an exhaustive ECCC search and implies nothing about novelty.

### Hexagon

Queries combined pseudoisotopy and presentation, a site-qualified pseudoisotopy query, and Grothendieck with concordance, followed by an explicit domain filter.

The related Michel Hua deposit, *Grothendieck Montpellier. Énoncés des lectures modernisées démontrés*, version 2 (5 November 2026), was directly opened at [the repository page](https://hexagonmath.org/2611.00125v2), and its 409-page PDF was opened through that page's PDF link. The abstract and provenance statement were read. Full-text searches in the exposed PDF found no occurrence of “concordance” or “Whitehead”; “pseudo” returned unrelated pseudoline material. This was a targeted keyword screening of one deposit, not a full audit of its 409 pages or of the repository. No exact A.119–A.124 counterpart was identified by that screening. Root's separate archival and King comparisons remain separate work.

## A.124: the distinction controlling acceptance

The original Igusa (1984) result assumes dimension at least six. Igusa (2025), Theorem 7.1, states the dimension-at-least-five sequence under \(\pi_2=0\), but the introduction calls the treatment a partial proof. On p. 27, Theorem 7.5 refers the difficult null-deformation reduction to the 1979 thesis and to the dimension-five continuation. That complete reduction has not been retrieved in this audit.

Bustamante, Krannich, and Kupers (2023), §2.2(b), PDF p. 18, record the earlier published-proof gap for a broader dimension-five application. Their historical statement does not settle the narrower later theorem and is retained only as context. The existence of the 2025 theorem statement does not close this project's full-proof gate. The separate A124 held review records the sound conditional deductions and the unresolved input. No unconditional dimension-five theorem is released for integration by this reviewer.

## Primary references and exact portions

- Burghelea, D., Lashof, R., & Rothenberg, M. (1975). *Groups of automorphisms of manifolds*. Lecture Notes in Mathematics, 473. Springer. https://doi.org/10.1007/BFb0079981. Original Theorem 3.1 and proof, pp. 28–29; the original primed PL statement was not directly retrieved.
- Bustamante, M., Krannich, M., & Kupers, A. (2023). Finiteness properties of automorphism spaces of manifolds with finite fundamental group. *Mathematische Annalen*. https://doi.org/10.1007/s00208-023-02594-x. Section 2.2(b), PDF p. 18, historical scope only.
- Galvin, D., & Nonino, I. (2025). *Pseudo-isotopy versus isotopy for homeomorphisms of 4-manifolds* (arXiv:2506.11905v1). https://arxiv.org/abs/2506.11905v1. Theorem 3.3, p. 17.
- Igusa, K. (1984). What happens to the formula of Hatcher and Wagoner when the first Postnikov invariant of \(M\) is nontrivial? In *Algebraic K-Theory, Number Theory, Geometry and Analysis* (Lecture Notes in Mathematics 1046, pp. 104–172). Springer. https://doi.org/10.1007/BFb0072020. Printed pp. 104–105 and 149–150 directly read.

- Jahren, B. (2010). Pseudoisotopies and the Bökstedt trace. *Geometriae Dedicata, 148*, 245–261. https://doi.org/10.1007/s10711-009-9441-7. Section 2, pp. 247–250, particularly Corollary 2.7.
- Krannich, M. (2022). A homological approach to pseudoisotopy theory. I. *Inventiones Mathematicae, 227*, 1093–1167. https://doi.org/10.1007/s00222-021-01077-7. Publisher introduction and Theorem A, scope comparison only.

[Primary repository PDF](https://api.repository.cam.ac.uk/server/api/core/bitstreams/90941cda-88d8-454b-87a4-ada4e99a870c/content). Printed pp. 8–10, scope comparison only.

Theorem 0.1, Theorem 0.3, and the category/comparison portions listed in the frozen primary receipts; [author manuscript](https://www.mn.uio.no/math/personer/vit/rognes/papers/plmf.pdf).

The exact remaining receipts, including Epstein, Waldhausen, Laudenbach, the stable-model ingredients, and Gritschacher's acyclic group-completion result, are in the A117–A123 primary-receipt files. This note supplements them; it does not replace them or broaden any prior checked scope.


### continuation_paper02_A125_A127_literature.md

# A.125–A.127: dated main-result literature comparison

Date: 23 November 2026, searches and primary-page inspection between approximately 15:56 and 15:58 UTC. Reviewer: int_free_independent. This is a source-exposed literature comparison, not a statement-only reconstruction. Root separately owns the A.117 search record. The associated JSON preserves the exact queries and hashes of returned search/open responses outside the deliverable tree.

## Exact claims and attribution

| Row | Exact claim being compared | Verified classical scope | Publication treatment |
|---|---|---|---|
| A.125 | The bottom-fixed, top-free PL concordance group of the standard two-sphere has one component; the stipulated definable models have the same result over every real closed field. | Cerf's restriction and boundary-jet theorems, his three-disk theorem in Laudenbach's formulation, and the three-dimensional PL–TOP comparison give the real PL calculation. The sphere–disk–rotation homotopy fibration also appears in Burghelea–Lashof, p.43. | Present as a classical consequence with the finite-family, jet, and bottom-correction details made explicit. Do not advertise the sphere component calculation as a new theorem. The field extension is an application of the already checked relative transfer. |
| A.126 | Every closed connected simply connected PL five-manifold has trivial concordance components, with no condition on its second homotopy group; likewise for the stipulated definable models. | Cerf's original 1970 Definition 2 and Theorem 0, pp.7–8, concern connectivity of the full pseudoisotopy group in dimension at least five. Compatible PL smoothing and the checked dimension-five endpoint of Burghelea–Lashof Theorem 6.1 give the PL assertion. | Explicitly credit Cerf and the smoothing/component comparison. This is not a newly lowered version of a dimension-six general classification. |
| A.127 | The bottom-fixed, top-free concordance group of the standard PL projective plane has one component; likewise for its stipulated definable models. | Kalliongis–McCullough (1992), §4.1 and Lemma 4.1.1, p.110, give the stronger full-boundary-fixed homeotopy calculation, of order two and generated by a rotation. Freeing the top contracts the generator. Epstein and Laudenbach supply the top and category corrections. | Credit the classical relative calculation; the present free-top argument needs its generation assertion only. The original all-PL geometric proof is an alternate exposition, not evidence of novelty. |

The source and independent-proof comparison in `continuation_paper02_A125_A127_comparison.md` checks the full target proofs. This literature note does not replace that proof audit. In particular, the printed all-dimensional wording of Burghelea–Lashof Theorem 6.1 is not imported without checking its proof: A.125 uses Laudenbach in dimension three; A.126 uses the visually verified inclusive bound in Theorem 5.1(a), p.37.

## Repository and journal coverage

### Primary journals and classical originals

The original [Cerf article record](https://numdam.org/item/PMIHES_1970__39__5_0/) was reopened; it verifies the 1970 publication, volume 39, pages 5–173, and DOI. Its mathematical theorem and definition pages had already been directly read in the original PDF for this audit. The 1971 author summary is corroborating evidence, with its first-page theorem on printed p.76, and is not substituted for the original.

The [Kalliongis–McCullough original PDF](https://msp.org/pjm/1992/153-1/pjm-v153-n1-p05-s.pdf) was reopened and its complete §4.1 Lemma 4.1.1 was located again. Its exact title is *Homeotopy groups of irreducible 3-manifolds which may contain two-sided projective planes*. Both the original article and the author's indexed CV identify pages 85–117. The last article page and the mathematical locator p.110 were directly read in the earlier proof check.

Boyle and Chen's *Negative amphichiral knots and the half-Conway polynomial* (2024), Appendix A, is a relevant later source because its Theorem A.6 displays a conflicting relative-cylinder calculation. The formula in Figure 24 has been directly read and independently checked: its half-turn at the upper disk does not fix the upper projective plane in the stated boundary-antipodal quotient. This precise formula defect is documented by the unchanged original certificate and a new displacement-matrix calculation. We do not use that theorem as an input. The source check does not claim a new topological classification from a point calculation.

### arXiv and current research

Queries included the phrases “pseudoisotopy”, “pseudo-isotopy”, “projective plane”, “S^2”, “simply connected”, and “five”, with arXiv-targeted searches and a second search engine for coverage. The relevant current result retrieved was Gabai, Gay, Hartman, Krushkal, and Powell, [*Pseudo-isotopies of simply connected 4-manifolds*](https://arxiv.org/abs/2311.11196), version 2 dated 1 February 2026, published in *Forum of Mathematics, Pi*, 14, e9. The current abstract and metadata were directly read. It repairs arguments for topological and stably smooth four-dimensional pseudoisotopies; it is outside the exact two-sphere, projective-plane, and unstabilized smooth five-manifold scopes here. Its existence is not a justification for broadening the manuscript into a general four-dimensional PL result.

Krannich's [*A homological approach to pseudoisotopy theory. I*](https://doi.org/10.1007/s00222-021-01077-7) (2022), introduction and Theorem A, were directly read on the publisher's full article page. The introduction explicitly credits Cerf's connectivity result in dimensions at least five. Its new comparison concerns high even-dimensional disks and a prime-local range, and is not the source of the three component calculations under review.

### Author pages

The search reached McCullough's author-hosted papers and indexed CV, and Krannich's publication list. McCullough's CV index corroborated the exact *Homeotopy* title and pages 85–117. Two attempts to open the CV directly failed (502/inaccessible); this is recorded as search-index corroboration, not as a newly read full CV. The original journal PDF supplies the primary bibliographic verification. The author's original Cerf summary and the Burghelea–Lashof author-upload text are separately receipted mathematical sources.

### ECCC

ECCC-targeted searches used both “pseudoisotopy” and “pseudo-isotopy”, with concordance/topology terms. Returned results concerned computational topology, pseudofinite limits, sign-rank, and high-dimensional expansion; no exact component theorem was identified. Some sparse exact-phrase queries caused the search system to return out-of-domain fallback material. Thus this is a documented limited search, not evidence that ECCC contains no relevant work.

### Hexagon

The correct repository, `hexagonmath.org`, was searched for pseudoisotopy/concordance and for projective-plane isotopy. Two preliminary queries used incorrect repository domains; they contribute no coverage and are retained in the query log only for transparency.

The related [Hua deposit, version 2](https://hexagonmath.org/2611.00125v2), was reopened. Its abstract/provenance and landing-page keyword screening were checked; no “pseudo” or “concord” occurrence appeared there. The earlier A.119–A.124 record separately screened the 409-page PDF for concordance and Whitehead terms. Neither screening is a complete audit of that deposit.

A returned [Ren deposit](https://hexagonmath.org/2611.00112v1), dated 5 November 2026, was opened at its abstract and references. It concerns universal group presentations and unrecognizability of connected sums of two-sphere products, with embedded-surface applications. Its scope does not match A.125–A.127. Other results concerned pseudoline arrangements, finite projective planes, and Finsler systolic inequalities. No exact additional counterpart was identified in these results; no novelty inference follows.

## Publication and receipt decisions

The mathematical source gates recorded for A.125–A.127 are closed at the stated classical-input scopes. Their publication should retain those credits and distinguish the stipulated real-closed-field transfer from the classical real PL conclusions. No negative search result is used to label any conclusion new.

The all-closed-surfaces corollary in the staged exceptional-surface input depends additionally on A.123. Root is deliberately withholding that corollary until the separate A.123 independent gate closes; the three released theorem statements do not require that gate.

The isolated compile used a pre-existing same-key bibliography entry before the newly supplied entry during deduplication, so its rendered KM title reads “Homotopy”. The released `continuation_paper02_A125_A127_references.bib` already correctly reads “Homeotopy”. The mathematical text is unaffected.

### continuation_paper02_A142_literature.md

# A142: dated exact-scope literature check

Search date: **23 November 2026**, approximately 18:54–19:12 UTC. This is source-exposed literature work after the target statement/proof was known, not an independent mathematical reconstruction. No novelty is inferred from a missing search result.

## Claimed scope and principal overlap

The claim under review concerns the failure of `pi_0 P_PL(B) -> pi_0 P_TOP(B)` for closed oriented real PL three-manifolds whose fundamental group is finite cyclic of even order. Both ends of the chosen representative are fixed, while an isotopy in the ambient concordance space may move the top. The definable conclusion is for each fixed expansion of the real field.

The decisive prior work is Galvin's relative Casson–Sullivan realization and the block-space application in his thesis. The publication input credits those results and supplies the explicit ordinary free-top trace correction. It is presented as a checked consequence, not as a new realization theorem.

## Primary literature checked

1. **Final journal source.** Galvin, D. A. P. (2026), *The Casson–Sullivan invariant for homeomorphisms of 4-manifolds*, Compositio Mathematica, 162(11), 2496–2544, DOI [10.1017/S0010437X26103510](https://doi.org/10.1017/S0010437X26103510). The official publisher page confirms publication online 28 October 2026. The full exact proof portions and original images are listed in the primary receipts. The finite cyclic unstabilized realization is explicit; stable smoothing is not substituted for ordinary isotopy in the target consequence.

2. **Official thesis.** Galvin, D. A. P. (2024), *Non-smoothable homeomorphisms of 4-manifolds* (PhD thesis, University of Glasgow), [official record](https://theses.gla.ac.uk/84595/), DOI 10.5525/gla.thesis.84595. Full Chapter 9, pp. 109–117, was read. Theorem 9.0.1 and Corollary 9.0.2 already address related failures for block spaces; the finite-cyclic instance directly overlaps the present application. The broader elliptic-group/Sylow argument is not certified by this A142 audit.

3. **arXiv version check.** The [official arXiv record 2405.07928](https://arxiv.org/abs/2405.07928) was opened on the search date. It records v1 submitted 13 May 2024 and v2 revised 9 July 2026, described as the final version to appear in Compositio. The exact proof locators used here come from the published version, not from mixing its pagination with the arXiv version.

4. **Author source check.** The [author's homepage](https://dapgalvin.github.io/) was opened. Its current list records the final Compositio volume and pages, links the thesis, and lists current work on three-manifold mapping class groups. This establishes a useful primary-source trail; it is not a substitute for reading any theorem's proof. The official MPIM [15 October 2024 seminar record](https://www.mpim-bonn.mpg.de/node/13586) also explicitly describes the block three-manifold comparison question. A talk abstract is treated as historical context, not theorem verification.

5. **Classical algebraic input.** The publisher record for [Bak's 1976 chapter](https://link.springer.com/chapter/10.1007/BFb0080015) verifies the title, LNM551, pp. 384–409, and DOI. Its publicly supplied two-page preview was read; the full chapter was not accessible through that page. The required finite-cyclic `L_5^s=0` formula was directly read in Galvin's primary article p. 2528. The [author-hosted surgery-survey compilation](https://webhomes.maths.ed.ac.uk/~v1ranick/books/wall1.pdf) contains Hambleton–Taylor's explicit cyclic 2-group computation, Example 11.1, pp. 252–253, which was independently inspected as an additional bounded classical check.

Search results also returned Watanabe's *Unstable pseudo-isotopies of spherical 3-manifolds*, [arXiv:2303.13877](https://arxiv.org/abs/2303.13877), and Galvin–Nonino's *Pseudo-isotopy versus isotopy for homeomorphisms of 4-manifolds*, [arXiv:2506.11905](https://arxiv.org/abs/2506.11905). Their returned abstracts concern different smooth or four-dimensional questions. No fresh full-proof reading or exact-scope overlap determination for these two papers is claimed in A142.

## Queries and limits

The searches included `site:arxiv.org Galvin "pseudo-isotopy" "3-manifolds"`, `Galvin "Non-smoothable homeomorphisms" thesis "3-manifolds"`, the exact Bak chapter title, and the Hambleton–Taylor article title. A stronger second-engine pass included `site:arxiv.org "Casson-Sullivan" Galvin` with a 365-day recency filter, plus direct opening of the official arXiv and author pages. Journal metadata was verified on the publisher page rather than a third-party indexing summary.

The requested cross-field terms were also included: `site:eccc.weizmann.ac.il "Casson" "Sullivan"`, `site:eccc.weizmann.ac.il "pseudo-isotopy"`, `"Hexagon" "Casson-Sullivan"`, and `"Hexagon" "pseudo-isotopy" "3-manifolds"`. No matching technical primary item from those exact queries appeared in the returned result sets. This is a bounded search outcome, not an exhaustive search of ECCC, an assertion about an unidentified Hexagon repository, or evidence of novelty.

Raw web result payloads are hashed in the release receipt and stored only under `/workspace/scratch/30b215c42f62/continuation_external_primary/` as `A142_primary_search_web.json`, `A142_primary_strong_web.json`, `A142_Bak_open_web.json`, `A142_Bak_preview_web.json`, `A142_HT_metadata_web.json`, `A142_exact_scope_literature_search_web.json`, and `A142_exact_scope_literature_strong_web.json`. The actual source-read scopes are recorded independently in the primary receipts.

**Literature disposition:** established Galvin/classical input with an explicit ordinary-concordance and definable consequence. No independent priority or novelty claim is supported by this search.


### continuation_paper02_A144_literature.md

# A144: dated literature and exact prior-input scope

Search date: 23 November 2026. This is a source-exposed review. It makes no claim of novelty from an unsuccessful query.

## Sources bearing directly on the argument

1. **Hambleton, Milgram, Taylor and Williams (1988).** The original author-hosted PDF was downloaded afresh and its printed pp.349–354 were read. Theorem A(b), Definition (0.6), and the final introductory paragraph on p.352 give the exact finite-pair/simple-decoration and periodic-group Sylow inputs used in A144. The official [journal record](https://academic.oup.com/plms/article-abstract/s3-56/2/349/1509484) and [current publisher record](https://londmathsoc.onlinelibrary.wiley.com/doi/10.1112/plms/s3-56.2.349) confirm 1988, series 3, volume 56(2), pp.349–379, DOI 10.1112/plms/s3-56.2.349. These are classical inputs, not new assertions of the manuscript.

2. **Oliver (1988).** The complete publicly hosted book was downloaded for targeted inspection, not wholesale auditing. Introduction Example 2 p.14 gives the all-order generalized quaternion conclusion; Example 14.4 p.333 supplies its explicit two-primary formula. The [publisher's book page](https://www.cambridge.org/core/books/whitehead-groups-of-finite-groups/FEEEE48B62CAB93D3631744C457E391B) confirms the original print date in 1988, series volume 132, and DOI 10.1017/CBO9780511600654. The inspected scan's copyright page distinguishes its 2008 digital reissue from the original publication.

3. **Galvin (2026), final published article.** The exact published version is [Compositio Mathematica 162(11), 2496–2544](https://www.cambridge.org/core/journals/compositio-mathematica/article/cassonsullivan-invariant-for-homeomorphisms-of-4manifolds/8376A5449442D18DD6C9E39C587849CA), DOI 10.1017/S0010437X26103510. The compact relative realization construction at pp.2524–2528, finite-group discussion at pp.2528–2530, and their exact marked-boundary conventions were already read in full during A142 and are reused here. The auxiliary realization lemma in A144 is an explicit relative presentation of this classical construction using the exact HMTW hypothesis. It is not credited as a newly invented surgery theorem.

4. **Galvin (2024), thesis.** The [official thesis record](https://theses.gla.ac.uk/84595/) and original PDF were inspected. Chapter 9, especially Theorem 9.0.1 p.109 and Propositions 9.3.2–9.3.3 pp.116–117, is close prior work on finite elliptic three-manifolds and smooth/block comparison. Its block-space conclusion is not silently identified with the ordinary top-free concordance-component formulation in A144. The precise case-list issue is documented solely in `continuation_CORRECTIONS_Galvin_Thesis_Sylow.md`, outside the paper. No complete replacement spherical-group classification or theorem-level disproof is claimed.

## Current author, arXiv and journal checks

The [official arXiv record 2405.07928](https://arxiv.org/abs/2405.07928) was opened afresh. It lists v1 submitted 13 May 2024 and v2 revised 9 July 2026, with the latter identified as the final version for Compositio. The [author's current page](https://dapgalvin.github.io/) was opened and directly confirms the final article and links the thesis. The exact proof locators in this audit are the published pagination, not mixed preprint page numbers. The same page lists later and related four-manifold work; its listing alone is not treated as proof of any statement used here.

Queries included `"Casson-Sullivan" "finite fundamental" "three"`, `site:arxiv.org "Casson-Sullivan" "concordance"`, `"Galvin" "Casson-Sullivan"` restricted to arXiv, and `site:arxiv.org "finite" "three-manifolds" "Casson-Sullivan"`. Journal and author pages supplied the direct close sources described above. A returned Nagoya article, *Some remarks on h-cobordisms between smooth 4-manifolds*, was only seen as a search result and has not been fully read; no theorem or proof dependency is attributed to it.

## ECCC and Hexagon

The searches included `site:eccc.weizmann.ac.il "Casson-Sullivan"` and `site:eccc.weizmann.ac.il "pseudo-isotopy"`. No exact technical counterpart appeared in the returned result sets. This is a bounded query outcome, not an exhaustive statement about the ECCC archive.

An initial broad `"Hexagon" "Casson-Sullivan"` query did not reliably identify repository coverage. A follow-up verified the repository as **hexagonmath.org**, then searched `site:hexagonmath.org "Casson" "Sullivan"` and `site:hexagonmath.org "pseudo-isotopy" "three"`. The returned material was a broad works listing with unrelated topics, not an exact A144 counterpart. No whole-repository or whole-PDF inspection is claimed from that listing, and no novelty conclusion follows.

## Assessment

The sources already establish the surgery machinery, the all-order Sylow arithmetic and close finite-three-manifold obstruction results. The useful content of the proposed section is its explicit relative proof and its transfer to the precisely defined ordinary component homomorphism, including the real definable comparison convention. This review does not establish a claim of historical priority for that formulation. The root's independent and publication decisions remain separate.

The complete search responses are archived only in the external primary cache under `A144_*search*.web.json`. Download hashes, dates and inspected-page scopes are in `continuation_paper02_A144_primary_receipts.json`.


### continuation_paper02_A145_literature.md

# A145: dated literature scope

Search date: 23 November 2026. This is a source-exposed audit; unsuccessful searches are not evidence of novelty.

The proof is a localization consequence of the compact relative Casson–Sullivan realization construction checked in A144. Its additional ingredients are the classical PL collar theorem, relative obstruction naturality, extension by zero, and Poincaré–Lefschetz duality. The ready-to-integrate section therefore makes no claim that these constructions or the connected-sum homology formula are new.

## Directly checked classical inputs

- Rourke, C. P., and Sanderson, B. J. (1972), *Introduction to piecewise-linear topology*: Theorem 2.25 and its complete proof, pp.24–25, and Corollary 2.26 p.26 were read from the original cached book. The original p.26 image was viewed. The exact result concerns a full PL collar of a compact boundary and includes the underlying PL manifold of a product with corners.
- Hatcher, A. (2002), *Algebraic topology*: the current [author PDF](https://pi.math.cornell.edu/~hatcher/AT/AT.pdf) was downloaded afresh. The exact reading covers pp.239–241, 244–248 and 254: relative cap products, their naturality, compact-support maps, the complete duality argument there, and the full proof of the boundary version in Theorem 3.43. Original pp.245 and 254 were viewed. The author's [official faculty page](https://math.cornell.edu/allen-hatcher) confirms the 2002 Cambridge book. This is the currently served author version, not a claim that its bytes coincide with the first printing. The printed-page locators are bound to the recorded hash.
- The exact primary surgery and smoothing inputs are those independently located and read in the A144 source-exposed audit. That release's source receipts are reused explicitly, rather than claimed as new A145 downloads or full-source rereadings.

## Close work and current searches

Galvin's [final 2026 article](https://www.cambridge.org/core/journals/compositio-mathematica/article/cassonsullivan-invariant-for-homeomorphisms-of-4manifolds/8376A5449442D18DD6C9E39C587849CA) remains the closest realization source. The article also advertises a connected-sum-along-a-circle formula. That advertised operation is not substituted for the exact codimension-zero support/excision argument in A145. This audit does not claim a new full reading of that separate formula's proof. The final publisher version, the [arXiv record](https://arxiv.org/abs/2405.07928), and the [author page](https://dapgalvin.github.io/) were checked during the immediately preceding dated A144 audit.

Fresh queries included `"Casson-Sullivan" "support" "connected sum"`, `"Casson-Sullivan" "supported" "3-manifolds"`, and `site:arxiv.org "Casson-Sullivan" "connected sum"`. They returned the known Galvin source and unrelated results about the SU(3) Casson invariant or knot concordance. Those latter topics are not evidence for the manifold-concordance statement here, and their complete proofs were not audited for A145.

The repository queries `site:eccc.weizmann.ac.il "concordance" "manifold"`, `site:hexagonmath.org "Casson-Sullivan"`, and `site:hexagonmath.org "concordance" "three-manifold"` produced no exact additional primary counterpart in the returned result sets. The repository is correctly identified as hexagonmath.org. These bounded queries do not establish exhaustive repository coverage or priority.

The section's role is to give an explicit proof that the ordinary component obstruction receives the stated locally supported classes, including in a nonorientable ambient manifold with arbitrary fundamental group. Its novelty is not established by this search. The main clean-independent gate, and the corresponding gates for its A143/A144 prerequisites, remain separate from the present targeted verification.


### continuation_paper02_A146_literature.md

# A146 — dated literature and attribution check

Checked on **23 November 2026**, during the source-exposed A146 audit. The bounded searches below supplement exact primary readings. Search silence is not a novelty claim, and no source has been credited with a theorem at a broader scope than was read.

## Exact claimed scope

The standard real PL three-torus has zero formal Casson–Sullivan component homomorphism on its ordinary bottom-fixed, top-free concordance group. The argument uses a relative five-dimensional mapping-cylinder normal invariant and injectivity of the degree-five free-abelian quadratic assembly map. It does not classify four-dimensional homeomorphisms up to isotopy or assert that every torus concordance has a PL representative.

## Classical credit

The top-map normalization uses Waldhausen's classical homotopy–isotopy theorem for sufficiently large irreducible three-manifolds (1968, Theorem 7.1, pp. 80–81), with the actual joint PL convention supplied by the separately checked automorphism comparison. The exact theorem's full pp. 81–85 proof was read in this audit, and the relevant original page images were viewed.

The decisive assembly statement is classical: Ranicki (1992), *Algebraic L-theory and topological manifolds*, Cambridge Tracts in Mathematics 102, Definition 18.2, Proposition 18.3, Theorem 18.5 with the explicit relative-version paragraph, and Example 24.16. The primary PDF is the author's corrected complete text dated 28 January 2018. It explicitly supplies free-abelian assembly in degrees at least the rank and Whitehead vanishing. The publication presents the torus calculation as a consequence of these classical inputs; it makes no novelty claim for them or for the resulting corollary.

APA references:

- Ranicki, A. A. (1992). *Algebraic L-theory and topological manifolds* (Cambridge Tracts in Mathematics, Vol. 102). Cambridge University Press. Author's corrected text, 28 January 2018. https://webhomes.maths.ed.ac.uk/~v1ranick/books/topman.pdf
- Waldhausen, F. (1968). On irreducible 3-manifolds which are sufficiently large. *Annals of Mathematics, 87*(1), 56–88. Exact original cached from https://www.maths.gla.ac.uk/~mpowell/Waldhausen-Irreducible3ManifoldsSufficiently-1968.pdf
- Galvin, D. A. P. (2026). The Casson–Sullivan invariant for homeomorphisms of 4-manifolds. *Compositio Mathematica, 162*(11), 2496–2544. https://doi.org/10.1017/S0010437X26103510

### continuation_paper02_A163_literature.md

# A163 — dated literature and classical attribution

Search and primary followup conducted on **23 November 2026**. This is an exact-scope literature ledger for a source-exposed audit. No priority or novelty is inferred from search silence.

## The claimed theorem and its classical inputs

The result concerns the PL-to-TOP comparison for ordinary, fully boundary-fixed automorphisms of `B x I`, where B is a closed connected oriented aspherical real PL four-manifold. Conditional injectivity of the **geometric quadratic** assemblies A7, A8 and A6 is used at separately specified stages. The central evaluation realization condition is retained for component surjectivity. The result computes neither the ordinary four-dimensional homeomorphism group itself nor the entire free-top comparison fibre.

The relative PL structure classification is classical Kirby–Siebenmann theory, with the exact open-five-dimensional, closed-relative-set implementation already checked in A135. The normal-invariant/assembly comparison is Ranicki's classical compact relative surgery sequence. The explicit hyperbolic and finite-dimensional CAT(0) assembly scope is supplied by Bartels–Lück (2012), not a new proof of Farrell–Jones or a four-dimensional Borel theorem.

The centre/evaluation calculation is also classical. The final published paper of Enkelmann et al. (2018), Lemma 4.3, p. 3336, states the identity-component fundamental group of the homotopy-self-equivalence space of an aspherical CW complex as the centre of its fundamental group. This is exactly the mapping-space calculation needed, because every map in the identity component is a homotopy equivalence. The adjacent block-homeomorphism and dimension-at-least-five statements are not substitutes for the current ordinary comparison. The publication gives that exact credit and retains a direct relative K(pi,1) proof.

### Primary bibliographic records

- Bartels, A., & Lück, W. (2012). The Borel conjecture for hyperbolic and CAT(0)-groups. *Annals of Mathematics, 175*(2), 631–689. https://doi.org/10.4007/annals.2012.175.2.5. Definition of the group class p. 632; Theorem B pp. 633–634; Proposition 0.3 and complete proof pp. 634–635. Exact final published PDF was downloaded, read and viewed at those locators.
- Enkelmann, N.-E., Lück, W., Pieper, M., Ullmann, M., & Winges, C. (2018). On the Farrell–Jones conjecture for Waldhausen's A-theory. *Geometry & Topology, 22*(6), 3321–3394. https://doi.org/10.2140/gt.2018.22.3321. Lemma 4.3, printed p. 3336, exact final publisher PDF. The author homepage link serves the older arXiv:1607.06395v2, so the publication locator was checked in the separate 74-page published PDF, not inferred from the 49-page preprint.
- Ranicki, A. A. (1992). *Algebraic L-theory and topological manifolds* (Cambridge Tracts in Mathematics, Vol. 102). Cambridge University Press. Exact complete author text corrected 28 January 2018. Definition 18.2, Proposition 18.3, Theorem 18.5 and explicit relative paragraph pp. 197–201; Example 24.16 p. 278. Reused from this reviewer's immediately preceding A146 full bounded primary read.
- Kirby, R. C., & Siebenmann, L. C. (1977). *Foundational essays on topological manifolds, smoothings, and triangulations*. Princeton University Press. Essay V, automorphism/structure fibration (1.5) p. 229; relative structure classification (3.2) pp. 240–241; stable relative PL reduction identification pp. 251–253. Exact prior own A135/A136 readings are reused, not recounted as a new complete source audit.

## Close modern antecedent with a different exact scope

Orson, Powell and Randal-Williams, *Smoothing topological pseudo-isotopies of 4-manifolds*, use an aspherical closed smooth four-manifold A in Section 8.1, including the hypothesis that diffeomorphism loops surject onto `pi1 hAut(A)`. Their Remark 8.2, p. 25, identifies this with central evaluation, and Example 8.3 uses an explicit circle-action realization. The subsequent argument concerns the stabilized manifold `A # g(S2 x S2)` and smooth/pseudo-isotopy invariants. Those categories, framing assumptions, stabilization and quotient constructions differ from the fully boundary-fixed ordinary PL/TOP comparison here.

The current exact author PDF was already cached and is reused: 40 pages, SHA256 `0000528ebceb135ad9b7300b126e42b78a700bc122dd25c13034dbe7229fd559`. For this audit, Section 8.1 through the discussion and Lemma 8.5 proof in Section 8.2, pp. 25–27, was read; the original p. 25 image was viewed. This is a bounded antecedent comparison, not a new full audit of their complete main proof. The Glasgow repository https://eprints.gla.ac.uk/397578/ records acceptance in *Journal für die reine und angewandte Mathematik* on 31 October 2026; an unseen typeset journal version is not being attributed the author PDF's page numbers.

The central-realization idea is therefore a documented antecedent. The present proof is an explicit consequence of the stated classical relative structure and assembly inputs. No claim that its exact full statement is new has been established or made. A separate minor printed homotopy-degree range correction is retained only in `continuation_CORRECTIONS_OPR_A163.md`, outside the paper.

# A201 dated classical comparison

On 2026-11-23 the query `semialgebraic mapping cylinder neighborhoods triangulation zero locus` located the original Delfs–Knebusch paper through the Regensburg repository, then its final publisher PDF. The final article is *Pacific Journal of Mathematics* 114(1) (1984), 47–71. Theorem2 p.48 and its mapping-cylinder proof pp.63–65 give the classical arbitrary-real-closed-field semialgebraic existence statement; Proposition4.1 p.66 gives relative homotopy uniqueness of extensions. These exact portions and their local-completeness scope were read, with direct page images48,65,66.

A later lecture-note search hit (https://pbrdng.github.io/rag.pdf, indexed Theorem3.29) gives the exact small-sublevel assertion and the same scalar-triangulation proof. Its full document/author metadata were not independently opened in this audit, so it remains a retrieval lead and is not a publication source or an asserted fresh primary reading. Another returned research-paper lead concerned systems of quadratic inequalities; it is not needed for this release and was not treated as proof evidence.

The new publication attributes mapping-cylinder existence and relative homotopy uniqueness classically, and spells out the scalar-triangulation construction, strict outer collar, gluing to a preassigned positive-radius tube, and empty conventions. No novelty claim is made, including from arbitrary-real-closed-field language. Exact URLs/hashes/pages are retained in the source receipts; all raw PDFs and web payloads remain outside deliverables.


### continuation_paper02_A202_A207_A209_literature.md

# A202/A207/A208/A209: dated literature comparison

Search and reading date: **23 November 2026**. Scope: strict mapping-cylinder tube attachments, the fibre-dimension condition, boundary-core fibre contractibility, and survival of concordance components when noninjective attachments are allowed. This is a bounded source-exposed comparison. It establishes the classical credits below and does not assign novelty from absence of search results.

## Exact primary comparisons

**Lacher, R. C. (1969). Cell-like mappings. I. *Pacific Journal of Mathematics, 30*(3), 717–731.** [Original publisher PDF](https://msp.org/pjm/1969/30-3/pjm-v30-n3-p14-p.pdf). The definition of (UV^\infty) and Theorem 1.1 on printed pp. 718–719 identify a finite-dimensional compact metric space as cell-like precisely through its neighborhood homotopy condition in an ENR. The preliminary lemma on p. 718 relates finite-dimensional locally compact ANRs and ENRs. These are the precise known inputs for the real clause of A208. The exact downloaded edition used for the initial direct page view is the author/academic mirror recorded in the primary receipt.

**Lacher, R. C. (1970). Cell-like mappings. II. *Pacific Journal of Mathematics, 35*(3), 649–660.** [Original publisher PDF](https://msp.org/pjm/1970/35-3/pjm-v35-n3-p14-p.pdf). Theorem 2.2, pp. 652–653, proves the equivalence between local connectivity of a mapping cylinder relative to its image and the corresponding UV condition on fibres. Corollary 2.4 on p. 654 specializes this to cell-like maps of ENRs. The complete relevant proof was read and its two central pages viewed. This is a direct antecedent for the boundary-core real statement, substantially closer than a general citation to shape theory. The publication input credits it and gives the explicit definable neighborhood/retraction argument. The general local-collar theorem on pp. 649–651 was also read for context; it is not used to assert that an attachment map is injective.

**Edwards, R. D., & Kirby, R. C. (1971). Deformations of spaces of imbeddings. *Annals of Mathematics, 93*(1), 63–88.** [Academic-hosted original](https://www.maths.gla.ac.uk/~mpowell/Edwards%20Kirby.pdf). Corollary 1.1 on p. 63 and its proof on p. 77 give local contractibility of the manifold homeomorphism group, with the boundary conventions read on pp. 64–65 and Theorem 5.1 on p. 70 directly viewed. A209 uses an explicit continuous product normalization to obtain the fixed-top subgroup; it does not cite an unstated relative version wholesale.

**Siebenmann, L. C. (1972). Approximating cellular maps by homeomorphisms. *Topology, 11*(3), 271–294.** https://doi.org/10.1016/0040-9383(72)90014-6. [Academic-hosted original](https://webhomes.maths.ed.ac.uk/~v1ranick/papers/siebappr.pdf). Theorem A, printed p. 271, was directly viewed with its boundary restriction and dimension clauses. The seven-dimensional application satisfies condition (a). The baseline A209 argument needs individual approximation only. The adjacent published material on p. 272 and the bibliographic context on p. 294 were read; they are not used to insert an unproved global parametrized approximation theorem. The separate stronger component candidate remains outside the baseline release.

**Shiota, M. (2010). *PL and differential topology in o-minimal structure* (arXiv:1002.1508v3).** [Author preprint](https://arxiv.org/pdf/1002.1508v3). Author p. 5, Theorem 2.1, supplies Hauptvermutung for the three-dimensional surface-link argument. Author p. 6, Theorem 2.2(1), supplies compact pair triangulation for the fibre retraction. The distinction between these statements is preserved. The introductory ordered-field conventions were reread, including the warning that compact polyhedra over a non-Archimedean field need not be compact in the ordinary order topology.

**Swan, R. G. (n.d.). *Tarski's principle and the elimination of quantifiers*. Author's notes.** [Author PDF](https://www.math.uchicago.edu/~swan/expo/Tarski.pdf). Theorem 3.2, p. 2, states Tarski quantifier elimination; Corollary 3.3, p. 3, states preservation of formulas with parameters across real-closed field extensions and gives its proof. The exact corollary page was viewed. This supplies the precise logical input for the semialgebraic endpoint transfer. The theorem is credited to Tarski; no date is guessed for the undated notes.

The nonzero torus concordance computation and finite real-algebraic coefficient representatives are previously accepted results of this project with their existing classical credits. Their full original source proofs and current exact statements were reread here; this audit does not renew or appropriate earlier independent acceptance.

## Search coverage and limits

Queries were issued through both available search systems. Exact result receipts are retained outside the deliverable tree under `continuation_external_primary/A202_A207_A209_primary/`, and their hashes are recorded in the receipt inventory. Representative query families were:

| Parametrization and concordance terminology | `"space" "mapping cylinder" "concordance" parametrizations`; `"mapping cylinder" "parametrizations" concordance`; `"parametrization" "mapping cylinder" "torus"`; `"mapping cylinder" "rigidification" torus` | Returned chiefly homology-cylinder, quantum Teichmüller, and unrelated categorical rigidification literature. No additional exact four-row counterpart was identified in these displayed results. |

A Coste author-list route timed out; it was not needed for the transfer input. Existing Coste notes pp. 5–10 were background only. |

A final query batch used a 3650-day recency window for the arXiv/ECCC/Hexagon and rigidification terms. Search-engine crawl dates were not substituted for article publication dates. An initial `hexagon.com` query used a corporate domain and is explicitly **not counted as mathematical-repository coverage**; the later searches used `hexagonmath.org`.

## Attribution decision

The strongest justified framing is: the real boundary-fibre result is a classical mapping-cylinder/UV consequence of Lacher; the definable assertion is proved by an explicit finite triangulation and neighborhood retraction; the unrestricted component obstruction combines Siebenmann approximation, Edwards–Kirby local contractibility, a uniform-metric argument, and the accepted nontrivial torus concordance class. The finite circle example is a direct worked mapping-cylinder example with two distinct exact checks. The disk-and-normal-arc counterexample is fully proved at its stated semialgebraic scope. These descriptions do not claim any result is new merely because no exact search match appeared.

### continuation_paper02_A301_A309_literature.md

# Dated literature and exact-scope comparison: A301/A302/A305–A309

Date of searches and reading: **23 November 2026 UTC**. This is a source-exposed literature audit. It neither establishes priority from search absence nor imports a separate reviewer's independence. All raw third-party files, extracts, search responses and page images are retained outside the deliverable tree in the primary-cache paths recorded in the accompanying receipt.

## 1. Classical inputs and the scope actually used

The first-order reconstruction in A302 and A305 uses the classical correspondence between definable sets and closure under Boolean operations and coordinate projections. Coste (1999), §1.3, printed pp. 9–11, supplies the precise ordered-field/parameter conventions. The candidate gives its entire formula induction and constructs diagonals before using graph composition. It does not present those logical operations as a new theorem of model theory.

A306 gives an explicit coding equivalence between the predicates for all closed bounded definable sets and the open core, in every finite arity over a real closed field. The terminology and the distinction between an expansion and its open core are classical (Dolich et al., 2010, introduction, author-preprint pp. 1–2). The coding by a bounded semialgebraic homeomorphism is proved in full. The realization criterion is stated with exact equality of the resulting closed bounded families; o-minimality of the open core is not substituted for o-minimality of the original expansion.

The example of a dense pair with an o-minimal open core is classical. Van den Dries (1998), Theorem 5, printed p. 63, applies to a dense pair whose large structure is based on the ordinary real field. Its proof in §4.8 and Corollary 4.9, pp. 77–78, was read in the primary indexed text. The earlier unary-function input is **Corollary 3.4**, printed p. 72, not a lemma with that number. The published repair by Baro and Martín-Pizarro (2021), author version arXiv:1801.08744v2, Appendix 5, pp. 21–24, especially Theorem 5.6, was read completely. Their author version is dated November 2019; the journal publication is 2021. The separate correction note records the older proof qualification without attributing a new correction to this audit. The elementary coding theorem does not depend on the dense-pair example.

For A307, the positive compatible-triangulation assertion is Coste (1999), Theorem 4.4, printed pp. 42–44. Its full proof was read, and those pages were viewed. It permits a triangulation compatible with finitely many prescribed definable subsets; it does not promise to extend every prescribed realization map on a closed subset. The candidate's PL-transition extension follows by subdivision of a subcomplex and the explicit coning construction. The negative circle example concerns the pointwise-prescribed realization, with finite linear subdivisions allowed.

For A308, interval extension is the classical Tietze theorem. The exact original bibliographic entry was verified, but the original 1915 paper was not obtained or read. The candidate proves the entire needed compact-metric case, including threshold-set edge cases, the convergent series and the interval bound. Thus the proof does not rely on an uninspected stronger version. Its finite semialgebraic counterpart is proved by compatible triangulation and a finite barycentric formula. The compact graph, quotient and pasting arguments are elementary and are also given in full.

For A309, Coste (1999), Theorem 3.9, printed pp. 28–29, supplies finite **definable** connected-component decompositions over arbitrary real closed fields. Dimension drop is justified from §3.3, especially Theorem 3.22, printed pp. 31–35. The local-section reduction and the contrast with ordinary connectedness over an incomplete ordered field are proved explicitly. No ordinary topological connectedness conclusion is imported from a definable-connectedness theorem.

## 2. Grothendieck's actual manuscript variants

The official complete cotes 120 and 121 were downloaded and the relevant original images directly read. Exact pagination and hashes are in the source receipt and the earlier immutable definitions-only packet.

Cote 120 makes its whole-line M0 optional (PDF p. 7), discusses compactness in M9 (PDF p. 17), and explicitly sets aside the preceding topology when starting the field-operations discussion (PDF p. 21). A301 is therefore a valid test of an expressly stipulated simultaneous system with the order topology, not an allegation that the author adopted that inconsistent combination.

Cote 121 has several successive compact formulations. PDF pp. 54–64 and 70–73 discuss the categorical/coordinate structure, interval extension, zero fibres, gluing and neighborhoods. The interval variants and the distinct axes/diagonal clauses are not silently merged. The exact prescribed-triangulation clause is on PDF p. 65, archive footer 64, handwritten p. 66. Its bounded correction is outside the publication inputs in `continuation_CORRECTIONS_Grothendieck_120_121.md`.

## 3. Hua v2: exact contemporary comparison

The inspected object is Michel Hua's **hexagon:2611.00125v2**, 409 pages, SHA256 `11d22eeee51d4bb73bd2bd899e4f0676d9ad286141b7fcfd8af148941f62208b`, downloaded from <https://hexagonmath.org/pdf/2611.00125v2>. The landing title is *Grothendieck Montpellier. Énoncés des lectures modernisées démontrés.* The PDF cover title is *Énoncés des notes de travail de Grothendieck démontrés en Lean 4, et questions ouvertes*. The cover says November 2026. On the landing page, the header displays “Version 2 / Nov 05, 2026”, while the version-history entry for the current 409-page v2 displays November 9, 2026. Both observed dates are preserved; the PDF hash fixes the compared bytes. The record licenses the work under CC BY 4.0.

Complete text read: §71, printed/PDF pp. 341–342, and §72, pp. 342–344. Images actually viewed: pp. 341, 343 and 344. No Lean build or formal-certificate rerun is claimed. The following records the complete hypotheses relevant to the mathematical comparison; it is not a reproduction of the source proof.

* **Theorems 71.1–71.2:** A Boolean family of subsets is closed under closure; each member contains a relatively open dense subset of its closure; descending chains of closed members with each successor nowhere dense terminate at the empty set. Every finite marked family then admits a coarsest admissible finite cellular partition. Adding finiteness and membership of the connected components of each difference of closed members gives the corresponding coarsest partition with connected cells. This concerns finite cellular partitions; it does not supply all-arity field coordinates or A302's exact trace criterion.
* **Coordinate conventions in §72:** For finite index sets, with coordinate maps (u^*:R^J\to R^I), the four displayed clauses are coordinate images, inverse images along surjective (u), intersection of a coordinate inverse image with an already designated ambient set, and products. A function is designated through its graph; (R_0) denotes elements whose singleton is designated. Lemma 72.1 gives tupling, image and composition operations, and, with the restricted subtraction/multiplication clause, those arithmetic operations, coordinate functions and constants from (R_0).
* **Theorem 72.3:** Under these coordinate clauses and M11 over a field, let finitely many designated functions (f_\alpha) on a designated (X) have designated constants (e_\alpha\in R_0), and let designated functions (g_\beta) embed (X). Then the functions (f_\alpha-e_\alpha) and ((f_\alpha-e_\alpha)g_\beta) have common zero set (Y=\bigcap f_\alpha^{-1}(e_\alpha)) and separate points outside (Y); finite coordinate functions provide a finite such family. This is a collapse/separation statement, not A305's assertion that total zero-set functions force complement closure.
* **Theorem 72.4:** For nonempty (R), finite-union/empty-set closure and two designated embeddings of (Z), compatible injective designated coordinate families whose images meet only over (Z) yield their set pushout and the designated universal map. The zero-fibre/separation axiom M6 together with extension M7 supplies such families. Conversely a designated coordinate presentation of the underlying set pushout has that separation property. The source explicitly does not establish its separate finite-limit/category assertions here. A308 gives a concrete compact model and also treats a closed embedding amalgamated with an arbitrary map; its full topological proof is provided directly.

These are substantial overlapping antecedents for the manuscript's general coordinate and gluing context. They are credited as such. Neither the inspected sections nor a negative search is used to certify a new-result claim for the seven-row package. The A401/A402 comparison belongs to the root's separate assignment; this worker has supplied only these exact source locators and bounded statement comparisons.

### continuation_paper02_A303_A304_A206_literature.md

# A303/A304/A206: dated bounded literature and attribution check

Search date: 23 November 2026. This is an exact-scope comparison, not a novelty determination.

## Exact inputs read

Stasica, J. (2003). Smooth points of a semialgebraic set. *Annales Polonici Mathematici, 82*(2), 149–153. https://doi.org/10.4064/ap82-2-5

The full five-page original was read. Theorem 4 (p. 152) treats the smooth points of each dimension of an arbitrary semialgebraic set. Page 150 gives the local definition, and pp. 151–152 contain the topographic-coordinate proof. The theorem's cited asymptotic analytic inputs were identified but not independently reproved. Its author page and the journal's official volume listing were checked as metadata, not substituted for the original theorem.

A bounded p. 151 proof-detail correction, with an explicit first-order repair, is preserved outside the publication input in `continuation_CORRECTIONS_Stasica2003_p151.md`. It concerns a continuity criterion for graphs inside open product windows; it does not change Theorem 4's conclusion or the theorem's classical credit.

Fernando, J. F. (2018). On Nash images of Euclidean spaces. *Advances in Mathematics, 331*, 627–719. https://doi.org/10.1016/j.aim.2018.04.011

The author-hosted final PDF was read at pp. 629–630 and 633–636, together with the reference to Stasica. The exact smooth operator is `Sth`, including `Sth_k` for each local dimension. `Reg` is explicitly a different operator. Lemma 2.3 (p. 636) gives Nash-diffeomorphism naturality. The main Nash-image classification is not used to prove the present partition theorem. The author's publication page and arXiv record 1503.05706 corroborate bibliographic provenance only.

Coste, M. (2007). Real algebraic sets. In *Arc spaces and additive invariants in real algebraic and analytic geometry* (Panoramas et Synthèses, Vol. 24, pp. 1–32). Société Mathématique de France.

The official publisher sample contains pp. 1–5; those pages were fully read. Theorem 1.3 (p. 3), its frontier-refinement paragraph (p. 4), and Propositions 1.6–1.9 (p. 5) give the exact marked finite Nash partition, components, dimension, and frontier facts. The stronger local-triviality theorem 1.5 was also read, but no local-triviality conclusion is claimed for the coarsest partition. The official chapter and volume pages verify the bibliographic title and range. No reading of pp. 6–32 is claimed from the sample.

## Search coverage and outcomes

Queries included `"coarsest" "semialgebraic" "smooth" "stratification"`, `"semialgebraic" "closed unions" "stratification"`, and the exact Stasica title. A further query combined coarsest, closed, singular, partition, and semialgebraic. Returned material included the primary Stasica article and related Whitney/canonical-stratification literature. The retrieved Whitney references concern different stated regularity requirements; their abstracts/search excerpts are not treated as a theorem-level equivalence with the all-closed-union operator used here.

An arXiv-restricted coarsest/semialgebraic/stratification search and author/publication-page checks were performed. The returned author and journal material confirms the classical inputs and points to current work on effective Whitney stratification; the complete proofs of those additional Whitney papers were not read in this bounded audit. No exact global identification with the present partition class is asserted from those search results.

ECCC results concerned semialgebraic proof systems or unrelated complexity topics. The Hexagon-restricted query did not yield an exact counterpart in the returned results. The partition argument is presented explicitly from classical semialgebraic smooth-locus and frontier-partition inputs, with its Boolean common-coarsening step and exact admissibility class proved in full. The cusp obstruction is an elementary instance of the distinction between topological equivalence and embedded smoothness; no novelty is claimed for that phenomenon. ### continuation_root_A205_Beke_literature.md

# Beke's intrinsic locally compact filtration: exact comparison

23 November 2026. Root read the entire eight-page author manuscript,
including all proofs and references, and actually viewed complete original
pages4 and6. Its downloaded bytes are160704, SHA256
`c75ea3604251da9335ca1b164124d3c397d13c8a0f2c8e55347db6dcfb6f26c2`.
Source: https://faculty.uml.edu/tbeke/o-min.pdf . The raw PDF, download
receipt and page images remain outside the deliverable project.

Beke, T. (2011). Topological invariance of the combinatorial Euler
characteristic of tame spaces. *Homology, Homotopy and Applications,
13*(2),165–174. The eight-page author manuscript has the earlier title
*Topological invariance of the combinatorial Euler characteristic of
o-minimal sets*. All mathematical locators below refer to that author
manuscript. The author's publication page explicitly associates this file
with the2011 volume and pages; indexed publisher material gives the final
title. No claim of reading the different ten-page final layout is made.

The local-compactness operator is defined on p.3. Lemma1.8, p.4, proves
that its iteration yields a finite intrinsic stratification of an o-minimal
set over the real numbers. Proposition1.12, p.6, relates it to a finite
stratification of a locally compact Hausdorff ambient space. The
coarseness qualification is stated on p.5. This is a classical antecedent
for intrinsic partitions, with a different regularity requirement.

The difference from A205 is already visible on the compact interval.
Every point of the interval has a compact neighbourhood, so the
local-compactness iteration ends with one piece. In the PL convention of
A205, manifolds have no boundary. Interior points have open-interval
neighbourhoods and the two endpoints do not. Thus their two-point set
is the non-PL-manifold locus of the whole interval. Saturation forces
it to be a union of strata. The indiscrete partition is inadmissible;
the partition into the endpoints and the open interval is admissible
and coarsest in the pure class. This elementary comparison uses the
definitions, not an assertion about the unread final layout.

Searches used both web engines on the exact author/title and checked
the author's institutional page. The legacy public publisher PDF URL
and its current indexed public resource URL both returned403, including
direct download attempts. Domain searches at EMIS and Project Euclid
returned no matching result. These are bounded access outcomes; they
do not reduce the exact King full-source gate. Earlier notes recording
this as an unopened lead remain historical and are superseded by this
dated reading. No original King page was obtained here.

The comparison is integrated in the intrinsic-partition section with
the exact author-version citation. It neither establishes a priority
claim nor counts as mathematical movement at an hourly review.

### continuation_smooth_components_six_root_literature.md

# Source-complete smoothable components and the remaining PL localization scope

Date: 23 November 2026.

## Accepted scope and exact independence

Root read in full the frozen independent A121/A122 reconstruction
(SHA256 07c55953eba69b921aad5d0b2cf1a513c3885be86d1c79f8aa44fa03be6a1f82),
the full authorized comparison
(SHA256 976a09c31d409f0504f5e33ced3e5763d350942d07fe0a4e12c504400e8fdcfc),
all repair fragments and the complete staged proof. The smoothable
dimension-six theorem has no remaining first-source access gate. Its
positive-suspension argument preserves the full framed second
obstruction and the first obstruction, and uses a diagram of short
exact sequences without a chosen splitting. The fixed-index inequality
is n−k≥k=3; the weaker-looking n−k≥3 wording has been replaced.

The new publication input contains the smoothable n≥6 theorem, the
PL dimension-six/seven corollary and the E(K³)≅E(K) lemma. It omits the
unverified extension-by-identity localization assertion. The complete
broader A121/A122 staging and both frozen records remain unchanged.
The original A121 and A122 rows are not accepted as a result of this
partial-scope integration. One separately identified supplemental
statement records exactly what now enters the paper; it is excluded
from the 1,028-original-row denominator.

The dimension-seven corollary is a further explicit deduction from
Hirsch–Mazur. If PL/O is 6-connected, existence obstructions to lifting
the stable PL tangent map start in H⁸(M;π₇(PL/O)); all vanish on a
seven-dimensional complex. Root checked this indexing directly, and
the original separate reconstruction worker independently confirmed
it after its freeze without reading additional project material.
Existence in dimension seven does not imply uniqueness there. For a
boundary, the interior/collar and locally flat hypersurface argument
gives a compatible smoothing without prescribing a boundary smoothing.

## Primary sources carrying the accepted proof

π₀C(M) when the first Postnikov invariant of M is nontrivial? In
A. Bak (Ed.), *Algebraic K-theory, number theory, geometry and analysis*
(Lecture Notes in Mathematics, Vol.1046, pp.104–172). Springer.
https://doi.org/10.1007/BFb0072020 . Original chapter: definition p.104;

manifolds*. Astérisque,6. Normalization and positive bending pp.54–61;

Theorem3.1(a), p.184; PartII, §2, p.244. The classical two-index
theorem and fixed-index marked-lens formulas justify the same index
in every dimension n≥6.

(arXiv:2112.08293v1), Theorem3.1 p.6 and §§4.1–4.2 pp.8–9.

explicit dimension-qualified theorem; the n≥6 scope is checked.

of diffeomorphisms. II. *Transactions of the American Mathematical

## Remaining exact gate

For the broader original A121/A122 assertions, the unclosed
